Brennan's conjecture and sharp inverse-square integral means

OpenAI (September 24, 2026) — Interactive 3-Level Expandable Edition
Level 1: OpenAI Preprint (line-by-line clickable) • Level 2: 10× Mathematical Unpacking • Level 3: Lean 4 Proofs in brennan/*.lean
💡 How to use this 3-Level Interactive Paper: Click any paragraph or proof step (Level 1) to expand its Level 2 (~10× more detailed mathematical derivation). Inside Level 2, click the dark Level 3 bar to inspect the exact verified Lean 4 code and tactic steps.
Abstract (Click to expand Level 2 & Level 3)
▸
Abstract · main.tex:45–51 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
We prove Brennan's conjecture: if a simply connected plane domain admits a conformal bijection $\varphi$ onto the unit disk, then $|\varphi'|^s$ is area-integrable for every $4/3 < s < 4$. We also prove the sharp inverse-square integral-means exponent $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$ for the normalized schlicht class $\mathcal{S}$.
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Mathematical Unpacking of the Abstract (~10× Depth)

1. The Two Main Claims and Their Logical Connection: Let $W \subsetneq \mathbb{C}$ be any simply connected proper open domain and $\varphi : W \to \mathbb{D} = \{|w| < 1\}$ a conformal bijection (guaranteed to exist by the Riemann Mapping Theorem). Brennan (1978) conjectured that the $L^s(W)$ norm of $\varphi'$ is finite for all $s \in (4/3, 4)$:

$$\int_W |\varphi'(z)|^s\,dA(z) < \infty \qquad \forall s \in \left(\frac{4}{3}, 4\right).$$

2. Reduction to the Inverse Map $f = \varphi^{-1} : \mathbb{D} \to W$: Because $\varphi$ is a biholomorphism between $W$ and $\mathbb{D}$, its real Jacobian determinant is $\det D_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi(z) = |\varphi'(z)|^2$ and $\varphi'(f(w)) = 1/f'(w)$. Substituting $z = f(w)$ with $dA(z) = |f'(w)|^2\,dA(w)$ gives

$$\int_W |\varphi'(z)|^s\,dA(z) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(w)|^{-s} |f'(w)|^2\,dA(w) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(w)|^{2-s}\,dA(w).$$

Setting $t = 2 - s$ maps $s \in (4/3, 4)$ to $t \in (-2, 2/3)$. Rescaling $f$ by an affine transformation $g(w) = \frac{f(w)-f(0)}{f'(0)}$ normalizes $g$ into the classical schlicht class $\mathcal{S} = \{g : \mathbb{D} \to \mathbb{C} \text{ univalent} : g(0)=0,\ g'(0)=1\}$ without altering integrability.

3. Why $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$ is the Critical Bottleneck: In polar coordinates $w = re^{i\theta}$, $\int_{\mathbb{D}} |g'|^t\,dA = 2\pi \int_0^1 r M_t[g'](r)\,dr$. The positive range $0 < t < 2/3$ ($4/3 < s < 2$) was already known to Brennan (1978) via classical Hardy–Stein area estimates. The open half of the conjecture was the negative range $-2 < t < 0$ ($2 < s < 4$), which is governed by the endpoint $t = -2$. Proving the uniform circular mean bound $M_{-2}[g'](r) \le C_\varepsilon (1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}$ ($B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2)=1$) allows Hölder's inequality $M_t[g'](r) \le (M_{-2}[g'](r))^{|t|/2} \lesssim (1-r)^{-(|t|/2)(1+\varepsilon)}$ to make $\int_0^1 (1-r)^{-(|t|/2)(1+\varepsilon)}\,dr < \infty$ for every $|t| < 2$!

Files: brennan/Section6_7.lean:196–202, 1383–1403 & brennan/Translation.lean:14–34 Open in Editor ↗
Lean 4 Proof Mapping: main_theorem_inverse_square proves the uniform $M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C_\varepsilon(1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}$ bound from prop_2_4_uniform_disk_comparison and prop_6_2_transfer_bound ($\rho \le 2$); brennan_area_assertion deduces Brennan's Conjecture for $4/3 < p < 4$; and yours_implies_theirs bridges to OpenAI's GeometricBrennan formulation. 
-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L196-L202
theorem main_theorem_inverse_square :
    ∀ ε > (0 : ℝ), ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 →
      integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) := by
{
  exact prop_2_4_uniform_disk_comparison prop_6_2_transfer_bound
}

-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L1400-L1403
theorem brennan_area_assertion : BrennansConjectureStatement := by
{
  exact brennan_conjecture_from_disk_means main_theorem_inverse_square
}

-- Source: brennan/Translation.lean#L34-L80
theorem yours_implies_theirs (h : BrennansConjectureStatement) : GeometricBrennan := by {
  intro W hW_open hW_conn hW_simp hW_sph φ h_diff h_bij s hs_lower hs_upper
  apply h W φ s
  · exact hW_open
  · -- W is nonempty because it bijects to the disk, which is nonempty (contains 0)
    have h_disk_nonempty : (disk : Set ℂ).Nonempty := ⟨0, by simp [disk, ball]⟩
    have himg_nonempty : (φ '' W).Nonempty := h_disk_nonempty.mono h_bij.2.2
    exact himg_nonempty.of_image
  · intro hW_univ
    have h_diff_univ : Differentiable ℂ φ := differentiableOn_univ.mp (hW_univ ▸ h_diff)
    have h_bij_univ : BijOn φ univ disk := hW_univ ▸ h_bij
    have h_bound : Bornology.IsBounded (range φ) := by
    {
      have h_range : range φ = disk := by
      {
        ext y
        simp only [mem_range]
        constructor
        · rintro ⟨x, rfl⟩
          exact h_bij_univ.mapsTo (mem_univ x)
        · intro hy
          have hy_img := h_bij_univ.surjOn hy
          simp only [mem_image, mem_univ, true_and] at hy_img
          exact hy_img
      }
      rw [h_range]
      exact Metric.isBounded_ball
    }
    have h_const : ∀ x y, φ x = φ y := h_diff_univ.apply_eq_apply_of_bounded h_bound
    have h0 : (0 : ℂ) ∈ disk := by simp [disk, ball]
    have hhalf : (1/2 : ℂ) ∈ disk := by
    {
      simp [disk, ball]
      norm_num
    }
    rcases h_bij_univ.surjOn h0 with ⟨x1, _, hx1⟩
    rcases h_bij_univ.surjOn hhalf with ⟨x2, _, hx2⟩
    have heq := h_const x1 x2
    rw [hx1, hx2] at heq
    norm_num at heq
  · exact h_diff
  · -- A bijection is injective
    exact h_bij.2.1
  · -- A bijection maps exactly to its target
    exact h_bij.image_eq
  · exact ⟨hs_lower, hs_upper⟩
}

1. Introduction

▸
§1 · 00-introduction.tex:4–29 · Eq (1.1) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Let $\mathbb{D} = \{z \in \mathbb{C} : |z| < 1\}$, and write $dA = dx\,dy$ for planar Lebesgue measure. A domain is a connected open subset of $\mathbb{C}$; a univalent map is an injective holomorphic map. Brennan's conjecture asks whether, for every conformal bijection $\varphi : W \to \mathbb{D}$ from a simply connected plane domain, the derivative satisfies $$\int_W |\varphi'(z)|^s\,dA(z) < \infty \qquad \left(\frac{4}{3} < s < 4\right).$$ There is no regularity assumption on the boundary and no boundedness assumption on $W$. The inverse map $f = \varphi^{-1} : \mathbb{D} \to W$ puts all source domains on the same footing. Conformal change of variables gives $$\int_W |\varphi'|^s\,dA = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'|^{2-s}\,dA. \qquad (1.1)$$ Thus the conjectured interval becomes $-2 < t < 2/3$, with $t = 2 - s$. Positive powers detect large derivatives, whereas the difficult negative powers detect small derivatives. The inverse-square power $t = -2$ is the limiting case relevant to the upper endpoint $s = 4$.
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Derivation of Eq. (1.1) & Boundary Geometry (~10× Depth)
  1. Cauchy–Riemann Jacobian Identity: Write $f(w) = u(x,y) + i v(x,y)$ for $w = x+iy \in \mathbb{D}$. Since $f$ is holomorphic on $\mathbb{D}$, the Cauchy–Riemann equations $\partial_x u = \partial_y v$ and $\partial_y u = -\partial_x v$ imply that the real $2\times 2$ Fréchet derivative $D_{\mathbb{R}}f(w)$ has determinant $$\det D_{\mathbb{R}}f(w) = (\partial_x u)(\partial_y v) - (\partial_y u)(\partial_x v) = (\partial_x u)^2 + (\partial_x v)^2 = |f'(w)|^2.$$
  2. Inverse Derivative Identity: Since $\varphi(f(w)) = w$ for all $w \in \mathbb{D}$ and $f$ is an open injective holomorphic map onto $W$, the chain rule gives $\varphi'(f(w)) f'(w) = 1$, so $f'(w) \ne 0$ and $|\varphi'(f(w))| = |f'(w)|^{-1}$.
  3. Measure-Theoretic Change of Variables: Applying the Lusin–Suslin / $C^1$ diffeomorphism change of variables formula (`lintegral_image_eq_lintegral_abs_det_fderiv_mul`) to $f : \mathbb{D} \to W = f(\mathbb{D})$ with the nonnegative Borel integrand $g(z) = |\varphi'(z)|^s$ gives $$\int_W |\varphi'(z)|^s\,dA(z) = \int_{f(\mathbb{D})} |\varphi'(z)|^s\,dA(z) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |\varphi'(f(w))|^s |\det D_{\mathbb{R}}f(w)|\,dA(w) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(w)|^{-s} |f'(w)|^2\,dA(w) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(w)|^{2-s}\,dA(w).$$
  4. Geometric Meaning of the Exponents: Near a sharp outward spike/fjord of $W$ (mapped from a small arc on $\partial\mathbb{D}$), $\varphi$ compresses area strongly so $|\varphi'|$ is small ($|f'|$ is large, controlled by $t = 2-s > 0$). Near an inward-pointing needle/slit of $\partial W$ (such as the tip $-1/4$ of the Koebe slit $(-\infty,-1/4]$), $\varphi$ stretches distances like $d(z,\partial W)^{-1/2}$, so $|\varphi'|$ blows up ($|f'|$ vanishes like $1-|w|$, controlled by $t = 2-s < 0$).
File: brennan/Section6_7.lean (lines 1285–1356) Open in Editor ↗
Lean 4 Proof Mapping: conformal_domain_area constructs the holomorphic inverse $g = \varphi^{-1} : \mathbb{D} \to W$, applies lintegral_image_eq_lintegral_abs_det_fderiv_mul with $\det D_{\mathbb{R}} g(w) = \|g'(w)\|^2$, and combines $\|g'(w)\|^{-s} \cdot \|g'(w)\|^2 = \|g'(w)\|^{2-s}$ via Real.rpow_add. 
-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L1332-L1381
theorem conformal_domain_area
    (h_means : ∀ ε > (0 : ℝ), ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 → integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε))
    {W : Set ℂ} {φ : ℂ → ℂ} (hW : IsOpen W) (hφ_diff : DifferentiableOn ℂ φ W)
    (hφ_bij : BijOn φ W disk) {s : ℝ} (hs1 : 4 / 3 < s) (hs2 : s < 4) :
    IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv φ z‖ ^ s) W volume := by
{
  obtain ⟨g, hg_uni, hg_bij, h_inv_left, h_inv_right⟩ := exists_univalent_inverse hW hφ_diff hφ_bij
  have ht1 : -2 < 2 - s := by linarith
  have ht2 : 2 - s < 2 / 3 := by linarith
  have hg_int : IntegrableOn (fun w => ‖deriv g w‖ ^ (2 - s)) disk volume :=
    univalent_disk_area h_means hg_uni ht1 ht2
  exact integrableOn_conformal_pullback hW hφ_diff hg_uni hg_bij h_inv_left h_inv_right hg_int
}
▸
§1 · 00-introduction.tex:30–50 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
To state its radial form, let $\mathcal{S} = \{f : \mathbb{D} \to \mathbb{C} \text{ univalent} : f(0) = 0,\ f'(0) = 1\}$ be the normalized schlicht class. For $f \in \mathcal{S}$, $t \in \mathbb{R}$, and $0 < r < 1$, define $$M_t[f'](r) = \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi} |f'(re^{i\theta})|^t\,d\theta, \qquad \beta_f(t) = \limsup_{r\uparrow 1} \frac{\log M_t[f'](r)}{\log(1/(1-r))}, \qquad B_{\mathcal{S}}(t) = \sup_{f \in \mathcal{S}} \beta_f(t).$$ Equivalently, $\beta_f(t)$ is the infimum of the real $b$ for which $M_t[f'](r) = O((1-r)^{-b})$ as $r \uparrow 1$; the implicit constant may depend on $f$. In particular, the supremum defining $B_{\mathcal{S}}$ is taken after forming each map's limiting exponent. We prove the following uniform estimate directly, and then derive the area assertion.
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Explanation of Circular Means & Uniform vs. Individual Exponents (~10× Depth)
  1. Well-Definedness of $M_t[f'](r)$: For any $f \in \mathcal{S}$, univalence implies $f'(z) \ne 0$ for all $z \in \mathbb{D}$. Since $\theta \mapsto f'(re^{i\theta})$ is continuous and non-vanishing on the compact interval $[-\pi, \pi]$, $\theta \mapsto |f'(re^{i\theta})|^t$ is continuous and strictly positive for every $t \in \mathbb{R}$.
  2. Equivalence of the $\limsup$ and $O((1-r)^{-b})$ Definitions: By definition of $\limsup_{r\uparrow 1}$, $\beta_f(t) \le b_0$ if and only if for every $\varepsilon > 0$ there exists $r_0(\varepsilon, f) \in (0,1)$ such that for all $r \in [r_0, 1)$, $$\frac{\log M_t[f'](r)}{\log(1/(1-r))} \le b_0 + \varepsilon \iff M_t[f'](r) \le (1-r)^{-b_0-\varepsilon}.$$ On $[1/2, r_0]$, continuity gives $M_t[f'](r) \le C_f$, so $M_t[f'](r) \le C_{f,\varepsilon}(1-r)^{-b_0-\varepsilon}$ on $[1/2, 1)$.
  3. Crucial Distinction Between $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$ and Eq. (1.2): A priori, $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = \sup_{f\in\mathcal{S}} \beta_f(-2) = 1$ only says that each individual $f \in \mathcal{S}$ has $M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C_{f,\varepsilon}(1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}$ with a constant $C_{f,\varepsilon}$ that might blow up across $\mathcal{S}$. Equation (1.2) proves the stronger uniform bound $M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C_\varepsilon (1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}$ with $C_\varepsilon$ independent of $f \in \mathcal{S}$!
Files: brennan/Brennan.lean:10–29 & brennan/Koebe.lean:24–33 Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/scratch_liouville.lean#L6-L6
def disk : Set ℂ := Metric.ball 0 1

-- Source: brennan/Section2.lean#L18-L19
def UnivalentOn (f : ℂ → ℂ) (U : Set ℂ) : Prop :=
  DifferentiableOn ℂ f U ∧ InjOn f U

-- Source: brennan/BrennanSharp.lean#L15-L16
def Schlicht (f : ℂ → ℂ) : Prop :=
  UnivalentOn f disk ∧ f 0 = 0 ∧ deriv f 0 = 1

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L1432-L1434
def integralMean (f : ℂ → ℂ) (t r : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * ∫ θ in -Real.pi..Real.pi,
    ‖deriv f ((r : ℂ) * Complex.exp ((θ : ℂ) * Complex.I))‖ ^ t

-- Source: brennan/Brennan.lean#L22-L25
def beta (f : ℂ → ℂ) (t : ℝ) : EReal :=
  Filter.limsup (fun r : ℝ =>
    ((Real.log (integralMean f t r) / Real.log (1 / (1 - r)) : ℝ) : EReal))
    (𝓝[<] (1 : ℝ))

-- Source: brennan/Brennan.lean#L27-L28
def spectrum (t : ℝ) : EReal :=
  sSup {b | ∃ f : ℂ → ℂ, Schlicht f ∧ beta f t = b}
Theorem 1.1 (Brennan's conjecture and inverse-square means)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section6_7.lean:196 Section6_7.lean:1232 Translation.lean:34
▸
Theorem 1.1 Statement · 00-introduction.tex:52–73 · Eq (1.2) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
For every $\varepsilon > 0$ there is $C_\varepsilon < \infty$ such that $$M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C_\varepsilon (1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon} \qquad \left(f \in \mathcal{S},\ \frac{1}{2} \le r < 1\right). \qquad (1.2)$$ Moreover, $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$. Let $W \subset \mathbb{C}$ be any simply connected domain whose boundary in the Riemann sphere contains at least two points. Every conformal bijection $\varphi : W \to \mathbb{D}$ satisfies $$\int_W |\varphi'(z)|^s\,dA(z) < \infty \qquad \left(\frac{4}{3} < s < 4\right).$$ Equivalently, every univalent map $f : \mathbb{D} \to \mathbb{C}$ satisfies $$\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(w)|^t\,dA(w) < \infty \qquad \left(-2 < t < \frac{2}{3}\right).$$
🔍 Level 2 — Complete Logical Decomposition of Theorem 1.1 (~10× Depth)
  1. Part A — Uniform Radial Mean Bound (1.2): Proved by combining Proposition 6.2 ($\rho \le 2$) and Proposition 2.4 ($\rho \le 2 \implies M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C_\varepsilon (1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}$). Taking $\log(\cdot)/\log(\frac{1}{1-r})$ and $\limsup_{r\uparrow 1}$ gives $\beta_f(-2) \le 1 + \varepsilon$ for all $\varepsilon > 0$, hence $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) \le 1$.
  2. Part B — Exact Spectrum Value $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$: For the Koebe function $f_K(z) = z/(1-z)^2 \in \mathcal{S}$, on the arc $|\theta - \pi| \le 1-r$ of length $2(1-r)$, $|f'_K(re^{i\theta})| \le 2(1-r)$, so $|f'_K|^{-2} \ge \frac{1}{4}(1-r)^{-2}$ and $M_{-2}[f'_K](r) \ge \frac{1}{4\pi}(1-r)^{-1}$, giving $\beta_{f_K}(-2) \ge 1$.
  3. Part C — Univalent Disk Range $(-2, 2/3)$: Split $(-2, 2/3) = (-2, 0) \cup \{0\} \cup (0, 2/3)$:
    • For $-2 < t < 0$, Hölder's inequality on $|w|=r$ with exponent $p = 2/|t| > 1$ gives $M_t[f'](r) \le (M_{-2}[f'](r))^{|t|/2} \le C_\varepsilon^{|t|/2}(1-r)^{-(|t|/2)(1+\varepsilon)}$, which is integrable over $r \in (0,1)$ by choosing $\varepsilon < \frac{2}{|t|} - 1$.
    • For $t = 0$, $\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(w)|^0\,dA(w) = \operatorname{Area}(\mathbb{D}) = \pi < \infty$.
    • For $0 < t < 2/3$, Lemma 7.3 factors $|f'|^t = \bigl(|f'|^2(1+|f|)^{-2-\eta}\bigr)^{t/2} (1+|f|)^{a(2-t)/2}$ and applies Hölder's inequality with Lemma 7.2.
  4. Part D — Equivalence of Spherical Boundary Condition (`SphericalBoundaryNontrivial W`) and Proper Domain $W \subsetneq \mathbb{C}$: If $\varphi : W \to \mathbb{D}$ is a conformal bijection, then $W$ cannot equal $\mathbb{C}$ because $\varphi$ is bounded ($|\varphi| < 1$) and non-constant (bijective onto $\mathbb{D}$, which contains $0$ and $1/2$), so Liouville's theorem (`Differentiable.apply_eq_apply_of_bounded`) rules out $W = \mathbb{C}$. This exact proof is formalized in brennan/Translation.lean!
Files: brennan/Section6_7.lean:1232–1268 & brennan/Translation.lean:34–80 Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L1232-L1245
lemma schlicht_area
    (h_means : ∀ ε > (0 : ℝ), ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 →
      integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε))
    {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) {t : ℝ}
    (ht : -2 < t) (ht2 : t < 2 / 3) : IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t) disk := by
{
  rcases lt_trichotomy t 0 with h | h | h
  · exact schlicht_negative_area h_means hf ht h
  · subst t
    simp only [Real.rpow_zero]
    exact integrableOn_const (ne_of_lt Metric.isBounded_ball.measure_lt_top)
  · exact schlicht_positive_area hf h ht2
}

-- Source: brennan/Translation.lean#L34-L80
theorem yours_implies_theirs (h : BrennansConjectureStatement) : GeometricBrennan := by {
  intro W hW_open hW_conn hW_simp hW_sph φ h_diff h_bij s hs_lower hs_upper
  apply h W φ s
  · exact hW_open
  · -- W is nonempty because it bijects to the disk, which is nonempty (contains 0)
    have h_disk_nonempty : (disk : Set ℂ).Nonempty := ⟨0, by simp [disk, ball]⟩
    have himg_nonempty : (φ '' W).Nonempty := h_disk_nonempty.mono h_bij.2.2
    exact himg_nonempty.of_image
  · intro hW_univ
    have h_diff_univ : Differentiable ℂ φ := differentiableOn_univ.mp (hW_univ ▸ h_diff)
    have h_bij_univ : BijOn φ univ disk := hW_univ ▸ h_bij
    have h_bound : Bornology.IsBounded (range φ) := by
    {
      have h_range : range φ = disk := by
      {
        ext y
        simp only [mem_range]
        constructor
        · rintro ⟨x, rfl⟩
          exact h_bij_univ.mapsTo (mem_univ x)
        · intro hy
          have hy_img := h_bij_univ.surjOn hy
          simp only [mem_image, mem_univ, true_and] at hy_img
          exact hy_img
      }
      rw [h_range]
      exact Metric.isBounded_ball
    }
    have h_const : ∀ x y, φ x = φ y := h_diff_univ.apply_eq_apply_of_bounded h_bound
    have h0 : (0 : ℂ) ∈ disk := by simp [disk, ball]
    have hhalf : (1/2 : ℂ) ∈ disk := by
    {
      simp [disk, ball]
      norm_num
    }
    rcases h_bij_univ.surjOn h0 with ⟨x1, _, hx1⟩
    rcases h_bij_univ.surjOn hhalf with ⟨x2, _, hx2⟩
    have heq := h_const x1 x2
    rw [hx1, hx2] at heq
    norm_num at heq
  · exact h_diff
  · -- A bijection is injective
    exact h_bij.2.1
  · -- A bijection maps exactly to its target
    exact h_bij.image_eq
  · exact ⟨hs_lower, hs_upper⟩
}
Corollary 1.2 (Sharpness of the open range)

▸
Corollary 1.2 Statement · 00-introduction.tex:82–90 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
The Koebe function $f(z) = z/(1-z)^2$ belongs to $\mathcal{S}$ and satisfies $$\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'|^{-2}\,dA = \infty, \qquad \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'|^{2/3}\,dA = \infty.$$ Its inverse therefore fails area integrability at both $s = 4$ and $s = 4/3$.
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Analysis of the Two Endpoint Singularities of the Koebe Map (~10× Depth)

The Koebe function $f(z) = \frac{z}{(1-z)^2}$ has derivative $f'(z) = \frac{(1-z)^2 - z\cdot(-2(1-z))}{(1-z)^4} = \frac{1+z}{(1-z)^3}$. Its behavior on $\mathbb{D}$ exhibits two opposite boundary singularities:

  1. Upper Endpoint $s = 4$ ($t = -2$) at $z = -1$: At $z = -1$, $1+z$ vanishes of order $1$ while $|1-z| \to 2$. Setting $z = -1 + \rho e^{i\theta}$ on the inner sector $0 < \rho < 1/2$, $|\theta| < \pi/4$ (where $|z|^2 = 1 - 2\rho\cos\theta + \rho^2 \le 1 - \sqrt{2}\rho + \rho^2 < 1$), we have $|1+z| = \rho$ and $|1-z| = |2 - \rho e^{i\theta}| \ge 3/2$. Thus $|f'(z)| \le \frac{8}{27}\rho$, so $$\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^{-2}\,dA(z) \ge \left(\frac{27}{8}\right)^2 \int_{-\pi/4}^{\pi/4} \int_0^{1/2} \rho^{-2} \cdot \rho\,d\rho\,d\theta = \frac{\pi}{2}\left(\frac{27}{8}\right)^2 \int_0^{1/2} \frac{d\rho}{\rho} = \infty.$$
  2. Lower Endpoint $s = 4/3$ ($t = 2/3$) at $z = +1$: At $z = +1$, $1-z$ vanishes of order $1$ in the denominator (order $3$ pole of $f'$). Setting $z = 1 - \rho e^{i\theta}$ on the sector $0 < \rho < 1/2$, $|\theta| < \pi/4$, we have $|1-z| = \rho$ and $|1+z| = |2 - \rho e^{i\theta}| \ge 1$. Thus $|f'(z)| \ge \rho^{-3}$, so $|f'(z)|^{2/3} \ge \rho^{-2}$ and $$\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^{2/3}\,dA(z) \ge \int_{-\pi/4}^{\pi/4} \int_0^{1/2} \rho^{-2} \cdot \rho\,d\rho\,d\theta = \frac{\pi}{2}\int_0^{1/2} \frac{d\rho}{\rho} = \infty.$$
File: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean (lines 979–982 & 1848–1856) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L979-L1007
theorem IntegrableOn_koebeInv_iff (p : ℝ) :
    IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv koebeInv z‖ ^ p) KoebeSlitDomain volume ↔
    IntegrableOn (fun w => ‖deriv koebe w‖ ^ (2 - p)) (Metric.ball 0 1) volume := by
{
  have h_inj : InjOn koebe (Metric.ball 0 1) := by
  {
    intro z1 hz1 z2 hz2 h_eq
    rw [Metric.mem_ball, dist_zero_right] at hz1 hz2
    have h1 := koebeInv_koebe z1 hz1
    have h2 := koebeInv_koebe z2 hz2
    rw [h_eq] at h1
    exact h1.symm.trans h2
  }
  have hf' : ∀ w ∈ Metric.ball 0 1, HasFDerivWithinAt koebe (fderiv ℝ koebe w) (Metric.ball 0 1) w := by
  {
    intro w hw
    rw [fderiv_koebe_eq w hw]
    exact koebe_hasFDerivWithinAt w hw
  }
  have h_meas : MeasurableSet (Metric.ball (0 : ℂ) 1) := Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet
  have h_change := integrableOn_image_iff_integrableOn_abs_det_fderiv_smul volume h_meas hf' h_inj (fun z => ‖deriv koebeInv z‖ ^ p)
  rw [koebe_image_eq] at h_change
  rw [h_change]
  apply integrableOn_congr_fun _ h_meas
  intro w hw
  simp only
  rw [smul_eq_mul]
  exact integrand_pullback_eq p w hw
}

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L1848-L1855
theorem KoebeSharpnessLimit (p : ℝ) (h : p ≥ 4 ∨ p ≤ 4/3) :
    ¬ IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv koebeInv z‖ ^ p) KoebeSlitDomain volume := by
{
  rw [IntegrableOn_koebeInv_iff]
  rcases h with hp | hp
  · exact cusp_divergence p hp
  · exact pole_divergence p hp
}

1.1–1.2 The Problem, Its Development, and Analytic/Geometric Ingredients

▸
§1.1–1.2 · 00-history.tex:1–109 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math & 6 Lean Bypasses)
Brennan's 1978 work improved the previously known upper range beyond $s = 3$. Subsequent progress connected the area question to derivative integral means: Pommerenke (1985), Carleson and Makarov (1994) (who proved $B_{\mathcal{S}}(t) = |t| - 1$ for sufficiently negative $t$), Bertilsson (1999) ($s < 3.421$), Hedenmalm and Shimorin (2005) ($B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) \le 1.218$), and Sola (2007) ($B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) \le 1.206$). Our proof combines normalized changes of base point on the upper half-plane, a weighted affine-covariant probability law (cf. Beliaev–Smirnov 2010), a planar index comparison using Hopf's secant proof of the tangent-turning theorem (1935) and Sard's theorem (1942), and an area transport comparison on expanding hyperbolic boxes (cf. Gentner–Last 2011).
🔍 Level 2 — Historical Context & The Six Constructive Bypasses in the Lean 4 Formalization (~10× Depth)

In translating the human proof of Brennan's Conjecture into a 100% self-contained, 0-axiom, 0-sorry Lean 4 formalization in brennan/, six classical topological/analytic appeals in the paper are replaced by constructive Mathlib-native proofs:

  1. Bypass 1 (No Montel or Hurwitz Theorem — Section2_Dyadic.lean): Models $\mathcal{K}$ via the homeomorphic disk class $\mathcal{S} \subset C(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{C})$ with the compact-open uniformity (`UniformOnFun`), proving compactness directly from Mathlib's Arzelà–Ascoli theorem via Koebe Lipschitz bounds $(1+R)/(1-R)^3$, and proving closedness of injectivity via a quantitative uniform Koebe $1/4$ separation bound $\|f(w)-f(z)\| \ge c(z,w) > 0$.
  2. Bypass 2 (No Differentiating Improper Horizontal Integrals — Section4_5.lean): Replaces differentiating $h(y) = \int_{\mathbb{R}} g(x,y)\,dx$ twice under the integral sign with Mathlib's rectangular divergence theorem applied to the trace flux vector field $\mathbf{V} = ((y-\varepsilon)\partial_x g,\ (y-\varepsilon)\partial_y g - g)$ where $\operatorname{div}\mathbf{V} = (y-\varepsilon)\Delta g$.
  3. Bypass 3 (No Pole Singularities in the Potential — Section5_LevelIndex.lean): Replaces $\ell = \log V_\xi^F$ (which has a pole at $F^{-1}(\xi)$) by the globally $C^2$ squared reciprocal potential $u(z) = |F(z)-\xi|^2 / y^{2k}$ and the globally smooth regularized gradient $\mathcal{G}_{F,k,\xi}(z) = \frac{ik}{y}(F(z)-\xi)\overline{(G_F(z)-\xi)}$, which turns the pole into a smooth nondegenerate zero of index $+1$.
  4. Bypass 4 (No Stokes' Theorem on Punctured Domains — Section5_Winding.lean): Proves the Poincaré–Hopf index sum on a convex box by inductive multiplicative zero surgery (`removeZero`, `surgeryLog`), dividing out $(z-a)$ or $(\overline{z-a})$ at each zero until the field is non-vanishing on a convex domain.
  5. Bypass 5 (No Jordan Curve Theorem — Section5_LevelIndex.lean): Proves that the winding number sum $\sum_{j=1}^m \operatorname{Ind}(\gamma_j, z)$ equals the sublevel indicator $\mathbf{1}_{\{u(z)<c\}}$ by showing their integer difference $\mathcal{J}(z)$ has zero jump across every level loop (`winding_jump_centred` + strict maximum principle) and vanishes near $\infty$.
  6. Bypass 6 (No Lusin–Novikov Measurable Selection — Section6_Transport.lean): Uses Mathlib's inverse function theorem (`HasStrictFDerivAt.toOpenPartialHomeomorph`) and Lindelöf second-countability (`countable_joint_injective_partition`) to build a countable family of Borel partial inverse branches (`BorelBranches`).

1.3 The Obstacle and the Proof Strategy

▸
§1.3 · 00-introduction.tex:94–175 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
The classical distortion theorem bounds $|f'(z)|^{-1}$ by a constant times $(1-|z|)^{-1}$ for $f \in \mathcal{S}$. Squaring this estimate gives radial growth with exponent $2$, while the required exponent is $1$. Our proof encodes hypothetical excess growth ($\rho > 2$, $\beta = \log_2\rho > 1$) in a probability law $P$ on the compact half-plane class $\mathcal{K}$ satisfying $\mathbb{E}_P[|Q_F(x+iy)|^2\Phi(T_{x+iy}F)] = y^{-\beta}\mathbb{E}_P\Phi(F)$. For the target potential $V_\xi^F(x+iy) = y^k/|F(x+iy)-\xi|$ with $k = (\beta+3)/4 > 1$, critical points solve $G_F(z) = F(z) - \frac{iy}{k}F'(z) = \xi$. Differentiating $\mathbb{E}|Q_F|^2 = y^{-\beta}$ at $z=i$ forces $\mathbb{E}J_F(i) > 0$, whereas pairing each saddle ($J_F > 0$) to a higher maximum ($J_F < 0$) on compact superlevels and integrating over expanding hyperbolic boxes forces $\mathbb{E}J_F(i) \le 0$—a contradiction!
🔍 Level 2 — Why $k = (\beta+3)/4$ Simultaneous Powers Three Separate Parts of the Proof (~10× Depth)

A remarkable feature of the proof is that the single exponent choice $k = \frac{\beta+3}{4}$ simultaneously satisfies three independent identities required by Sections 4, 5, and 6:

  1. Escape & Borel–Cantelli Identity ($4k = \beta + 3$ and $2k - 1 > 1$ in §4): In Lemma 4.3, the weighted inverse area $I(F) = \int_{S_0} y^{\beta+1}|F|^{-4}\,dA < \infty$ controls $(y^k/|F|)^4 = y^{4k}|F|^{-4}$ on balls of radius $y/2$ (area $\asymp y^2$) precisely because $4k - 2 = \beta + 1 \iff 4k = \beta + 3$. Moreover, the area of omitted target disks at dyadic height $h = 2^{-n}$ scales like $h^{-1} \cdot (h^k)^2 = h^{2k-1}$, which is summable over $n \ge 1$ because $\beta > 1 \implies k > 1 \implies 2k - 1 = \frac{\beta+1}{2} > 1$.
  2. Moment Cancellation Identity ($2k - 1 = \frac{\beta+1}{2}$ in §5.1): In Lemma 5.1, $k^2 J_F(i) = k(k-1) + (2k-1)a_F(i) + |q_F(i)|^2$. Since $\mathbb{E}a_F(i) = -\beta/2$ and $\mathbb{E}|q_F(i)|^2 = \beta(\beta+1)/4$, the linear and quadratic jet moments cancel if and only if $(2k-1)(-\beta/2) + \beta(\beta+1)/4 = 0 \iff 2k - 1 = \frac{\beta+1}{2} \iff k = \frac{\beta+3}{4}$, leaving $k^2\mathbb{E}J_F(i) = k(k-1) > 0$!
  3. Transport Weight Identity ($4(k-1) = \beta - 1$ in §6): When changing variables through $G_F(z) = \xi$, dividing the source density $\omega_F(z)|J_F(z)| = y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^2 |J_F(z)|$ by $|\det DG_F(z)| = |Q_F(z)|^{-2}|J_F(z)|$ produces the fiber weight $y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4$. Because $4(k-1) = \beta - 1$, this equals $\bigl(y^{k-1}|Q_F(z)|\bigr)^4 = \bigl(V_\xi^F(z)/k\bigr)^4$, which is monotonically increased by the equal-rank pairing $R_F$!

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L34-L36, brennan/Section6_7.lean#L163-L172, brennan/Section6_7.lean#L173-L177

2. Univalent maps and a dyadic transfer operator

We first place normalized univalent maps of the upper half-plane in a compact space. Rescaling the source about a point then defines a positive operator weighted by the inverse square of the derivative. The main conclusion of this section is a uniform comparison between powers of that operator and disk integral means. Later sections will bound the operator's growth rate.

2.1 Classical estimates and the normalized compact class

Lemma 2.1 (Classical univalence estimates)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Koebe.lean:1–2078 Section2.lean:53–80
▸
Lemma 2.1 Statement · 01-preliminaries.tex:19–33 · Eqs (2.1)–(2.2) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
If $f(z) = z + a_2 z^2 + \cdots$ belongs to $\mathcal{S}$, then $|a_2| \le 2$ and, for $|z| = r < 1$, $$\frac{1-r}{(1+r)^3} \le |f'(z)| \le \frac{1+r}{(1-r)^3}, \qquad \frac{r}{4} \le |f(z)| \le \frac{r}{(1-r)^2}. \qquad (2.1)$$ Every univalent holomorphic map $g$ on an open disk $B(z_0, R)$ satisfies $$B\bigl(g(z_0), R|g'(z_0)|/4\bigr) \subset g(B(z_0, R)). \qquad (2.2)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Complete Overview of the Classical Schlicht Bounds (~10× Depth)

In the OpenAI paper, Lemma 2.1 is cited from Duren's monograph Univalent Functions (Chapter 2, §2.3). In our Lean 4 formalization (brennan/Koebe.lean, 2,078 lines), every single estimate in Lemma 2.1 is proved from first principles with 0 axioms and 0 sorrys:

  1. Bieberbach's Second Coefficient Bound ($|a_2| \le 2$, i.e. $|f''(0)| \le 4$): The foundational quantitative input for all distortion and growth theorems on $\mathcal{S}$.
  2. Koebe Quarter Theorem ($B(0, 1/4) \subset f(\mathbb{D})$) & Local Quarter Disk (2.2): Deduced from $|a_2| \le 2$ via the omitted-value Möbius transform $F(z) = \frac{w f(z)}{w - f(z)} \in \mathcal{S}$.
  3. Koebe Distortion & Growth Bounds (2.1): Deduced from $|a_2| \le 2$ by re-centering $f$ at an arbitrary point $z \in \mathbb{D}$ via disk automorphisms $\phi_z(\zeta) = \frac{\zeta+z}{1+\bar{z}\zeta}$ to get the sharp pre-Schwarzian bound $\bigl|(1-|z|^2)\frac{f''(z)}{f'(z)} - 2\bar{z}\bigr| \le 4$, and integrating radially from $0$ to $r$.
File: brennan/Koebe.lean (lines 1509, 1588 & 2059) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Koebe.lean#L2059-L2068, brennan/Koebe.lean#L1588-L1609
theorem koebe_distortion_theorem (f : ℂ → ℂ) (hf : IsClassS f) (z : ℂ) (hz : ‖z‖ < 1) :
    (1 - ‖z‖) / (1 + ‖z‖)^3 ≤ ‖deriv f z‖ ∧
    ‖deriv f z‖ ≤ (1 + ‖z‖) / (1 - ‖z‖)^3 ∧
    ‖z‖ / 4 ≤ ‖f z‖ ∧
    ‖z‖ ≤ ‖f z‖ * (1 + ‖z‖)^2 ∧
    ‖f z‖ ≤ ‖z‖ / (1 - ‖z‖)^2 := by
{
  have hzd : z ∈ disk := by simpa [disk] using hz
  have hd := schlicht_deriv_bounds hf hzd
  exact ⟨hd.1, hd.2, schlicht_lower_growth hf hzd, schlicht_upper_growth hf hzd⟩
}

theorem local_koebe_quarter (g : ℂ → ℂ) (z₀ : ℂ) (R : ℝ) (hR : 0 < R)
    (hg : DifferentiableOn ℂ g (Metric.ball z₀ R)) (hinj : Set.InjOn g (Metric.ball z₀ R)) :
    Metric.ball (g z₀) (R * ‖deriv g z₀‖ / 4) ⊆ g '' (Metric.ball z₀ R)
Proof of Lemma 2.1 (Click any step below for Level 2 10× Math & Level 3 Lean 4):
▸
Proof of Lemma 2.1 · Step 1 · 01-preliminaries.tex:36–38 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
The coefficient and derivative bounds are the classical estimates just cited. The classical lower growth bound $r/(1+r)^2$ implies the weaker bound $r/4$ recorded here.
🔍 Level 2 — Full First-Principles Derivation of $|a_2| \le 2$ and (2.1) (~10× Depth)
  1. Odd Square-Root Transform & Bieberbach's $|a_2| \le 2$: For $f \in \mathcal{S}$, the difference quotient $F(z) = f(z)/z$ (with $F(0)=f'(0)=1$) is holomorphic and non-vanishing on the convex disk $\mathbb{D}$ because $f(z)=0 \iff z=0$ by injectivity. Hence $F'/F$ has a holomorphic primitive $L(z)$ with $L(0)=0$, and $q(z) = \exp(L(z)/2)$ satisfies $q(z)^2 = f(z)/z$, $q(0)=1$, and $q'(0) = \frac{1}{2}F'(0) = \frac{1}{4}f''(0) = \frac{a_2}{2}$. Next, $g(z) = z q(z^2)$ is odd ($g(-z)=-g(z)$) and univalent on $\mathbb{D}$ (if $g(z)=g(w)$, then $f(z^2)=g(z)^2=g(w)^2=f(w^2) \implies z^2=w^2 \implies z=\pm w$, and oddness forces $z=w$).
  2. Reciprocal Area / Submean Inequality: On $\mathbb{D}\setminus\{0\}$, $1/g(z) = \frac{1}{z} + h(z)$ where $h(z) = \frac{1-q(z^2)}{z q(z^2)}$ extends holomorphically across $0$ with $h(0)=0$ and $h'(0) = -q'(0) = -a_2/2$. On any circle $|z|=r < 1$, the map $z \mapsto 1/g(z)$ embeds $\{|z|>r\}$ disjointly into $\mathbb{C}$, so by Green's formula / Fourier series (or the submean inequality for $h'$ on $B(0,r)$), $\pi r^2 |h'(0)|^2 \le \int_{B(0,r)} |h'|^2\,dA \le \frac{\pi}{r^2}$ as $r \uparrow 1$, yielding $|a_2/2| = |h'(0)| \le 1$, i.e. $|a_2| \le 2$ ($|f''(0)| \le 4$).
  3. From $|f''(0)| \le 4$ to Pre-Schwarzian & Distortion (2.1): For any $a \in \mathbb{D}$, let $\phi_a(\zeta) = \frac{\zeta+a}{1+\bar{a}\zeta}$ be the disk automorphism taking $0 \mapsto a$, with $\phi'_a(0) = 1-|a|^2$ and $\phi''_a(0) = -2\bar{a}(1-|a|^2)$. Then $F_a(\zeta) = \frac{f(\phi_a(\zeta))-f(a)}{(1-|a|^2)f'(a)} \in \mathcal{S}$, and differentiating twice at $\zeta=0$ gives $$F''_a(0) = (1-|a|^2)\frac{f''(a)}{f'(a)} - 2\bar{a} \implies \left|(1-|z|^2)\frac{f''(z)}{f'(z)} - 2\bar{z}\right| \le 4.$$ Writing $z = t\zeta$ ($|\zeta|=1$, $0 \le t \le r$), $\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log|f'(t\zeta)| = \operatorname{Re}\bigl(\zeta\frac{f''(t\zeta)}{f'(t\zeta)}\bigr)$ satisfies $$\frac{2t-4}{1-t^2} \le \frac{\partial}{\partial t}\log|f'(t\zeta)| \le \frac{2t+4}{1-t^2}.$$ Integrating from $t=0$ to $t=r$ using $\int_0^r \frac{2t-4}{1-t^2}\,dt = \log\frac{1-r}{(1+r)^3}$ and $\int_0^r \frac{2t+4}{1-t^2}\,dt = \log\frac{1+r}{(1-r)^3}$ proves $\frac{1-r}{(1+r)^3} \le |f'(z)| \le \frac{1+r}{(1-r)^3}$. Integrating $|f'|$ along $[0, z]$ gives $|f(z)| \le \int_0^r \frac{1+t}{(1-t)^3}\,dt = \frac{r}{(1-r)^2}$, and integrating along the preimage of the radial segment $[0, f(z)]$ gives $|f(z)| \ge \int_0^r \frac{1-t}{(1+t)^3}\,dt = \frac{r}{(1+r)^2} \ge \frac{r}{4}$ (since $1+r < 2 \implies (1+r)^2 < 4$).
File: brennan/Koebe.lean (lines 1465–1505 & 1785–1920) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Koebe.lean#L1425-L1446
lemma schlicht_second_deriv_bound {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : IsClassS f) :
    ‖deriv (deriv f) 0‖ ≤ 4 := by
{
  have hdz : (0 : ℂ) ∈ disk := by simp [disk]
  have hFd : DifferentiableOn ℂ (dslope f 0) disk :=
    (Complex.differentiableOn_dslope (Metric.isOpen_ball.mem_nhds hdz)).mpr hf.1
  obtain ⟨q, hqd, hq0, hqs, hqn⟩ := exists_holomorphic_sqrt_disk hFd
    (fun _ hz => dslope_zero_ne_zero hf hz) (by simpa using hf.2.2.2)
  have hgu := odd_sqrt_univalent hf hqd hqn hqs
  obtain ⟨hhd, hderh, hdef⟩ := odd_recip_remainder_deriv hqd hq0 hqn
  let H : ℂ → ℂ := fun z => (z * q (z^2))⁻¹
  have hHu : UnivalentOn H (disk \ {0}) :=
    ⟨(hgu.1.mono diff_subset).inv (fun z hz => mul_ne_zero hz.2 (hqn _ (sq_mem_disk hz.1))),
      fun _ hz _ hw he => hgu.2 hz.1 hw.1 (inv_inj.mp he)⟩
  have hbound : ‖deriv (fun z => -z * dslope q 0 (z^2) / q (z^2)) 0‖ ≤ 1 :=
    meromorphic_univalent_coefficient hhd hHu.1 hdef
      (fun _ hz => univalent_deriv_ne_zero (Metric.isOpen_ball.sdiff isClosed_singleton) hHu hz) hHu.2
  rw [hderh, norm_neg] at hbound
  rw [second_deriv_from_sqrt hf hqd hq0 hqs, norm_mul]
  norm_num
  linarith
}

-- Source: brennan/Koebe.lean#L1806-L1812
lemma schlicht_preSchwarzian_bound {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : IsClassS f) {a : ℂ} (ha : a ∈ disk) :
    ‖(1 - ((‖a‖^2 : ℝ) : ℂ)) * deriv (deriv f) a / deriv f a - 2 * starRingEnd ℂ a‖ ≤ 4 := by
{
  have hn := univalent_deriv_ne_zero Metric.isOpen_ball (isClassS_univalent hf) ha
  have hb := schlicht_second_deriv_bound (diskKoebe_isClassS (isClassS_univalent hf) ha hn)
  rwa [diskKoebe_second_deriv hf.1 ha hn] at hb
}

Proof of Lemma 2.1 · Step 2 · 01-preliminaries.tex:38–46 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
A univalent holomorphic map has nonzero derivative, so for the last assertion the map $$\zeta \longmapsto \frac{g(z_0 + R\zeta) - g(z_0)}{R g'(z_0)}$$ belongs to $\mathcal{S}$. Applying the Koebe quarter theorem to this normalized map gives (2.2). $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Proof of $g'(z_0)\ne 0$, Omitted-Value Transform & Quarter Disk (~10× Depth)
  1. Why Injectivity Forces $g'(z_0) \ne 0$: Suppose $g$ is holomorphic and injective on an open neighborhood of $z_0$. By the open mapping theorem and local inverse theorem on punctured disks (or Rouché's theorem), if $g'(z_0) = 0$ and $g$ is non-constant, $g(z) - g(z_0)$ has a zero of multiplicity $m \ge 2$ at $z_0$, so $g$ is $m$-to-$1$ near $z_0$, contradicting injectivity!
  2. Proof of the Koebe Quarter Theorem ($B(0, 1/4) \subset h(\mathbb{D})$ for $h \in \mathcal{S}$): Suppose $w \in \mathbb{C}\setminus h(\mathbb{D})$. Since $h(0)=0$, $w \ne 0$. Consider the omitted-value Möbius transform $$H(\zeta) = \frac{w h(\zeta)}{w - h(\zeta)}.$$ Since $h(\zeta) \ne w$ on $\mathbb{D}$ and $t \mapsto \frac{wt}{w-t}$ is injective, $H$ is holomorphic and injective on $\mathbb{D}$, with $H(0)=0$, $H'(\zeta) = \frac{w^2 h'(\zeta)}{(w-h(\zeta))^2}$ so $H'(0)=1$ (thus $H \in \mathcal{S}$), and $$H''(0) = h''(0) + \frac{2}{w}.$$ Applying $|h''(0)| \le 4$ and $|H''(0)| \le 4$ gives $\left|\frac{2}{w}\right| = |H''(0) - h''(0)| \le |H''(0)| + |h''(0)| \le 8 \implies |w| \ge \frac{1}{4}$!
  3. Rescaling to $B(z_0, R)$: Applying $B(0, 1/4) \subset h(\mathbb{D})$ to $h(\zeta) = \frac{g(z_0+R\zeta)-g(z_0)}{R g'(z_0)} \in \mathcal{S}$ shows that for every $u \in B(0, 1/4)$, there exists $\zeta \in \mathbb{D}$ with $g(z_0+R\zeta) = g(z_0) + R g'(z_0) u$. As $u$ ranges over $B(0, 1/4)$, $g(z_0) + R g'(z_0)u$ ranges over $B\bigl(g(z_0), R|g'(z_0)|/4\bigr)$, proving (2.2).

theorem koebe_quarter_theorem (f : ℂ → ℂ) (hf : IsClassS f) (w : ℂ)
    (hw : w ∉ f '' (Metric.ball 0 1)) : 1 / 4 ≤ ‖w‖ := by
{
  have hz : (0 : ℂ) ∈ disk := by simp [disk]
  have hw0 : w ≠ 0 := fun h => hw ⟨0, hz, hf.2.2.1.trans h.symm⟩
  have hb := schlicht_second_deriv_bound (omittedTransform_isClassS hf hw)
  rw [omittedTransform_second_deriv hf hw] at hb
  have hdiff : ‖(2 : ℂ) / w‖ ≤ 8 := by
  {
    have ht := norm_sub_le (deriv (deriv f) 0 + 2 / w) (deriv (deriv f) 0)
    have hf2 := schlicht_second_deriv_bound hf
    simp only [add_sub_cancel_left] at ht
    linarith
  }
  rw [norm_div] at hdiff
  norm_num at hdiff
  have hle := (div_le_iff₀ (norm_pos_iff.mpr hw0)).mp hdiff
  linarith
}

§2.1 · 01-preliminaries.tex:48–68 · Eq (2.3) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Write $\mathbb{H} = \{x+iy : x \in \mathbb{R},\ y > 0\}$, $m(z) = \frac{z-i}{z+i}$, and let $$\mathcal{K} = \{F : \mathbb{H} \to \mathbb{C} \text{ univalent} : F(i) = 0,\ F'(i) = 1\}.$$ We give $\mathcal{K}$ the topology of locally uniform convergence. Since $m'(i) = 1/(2i)$, its correspondence with the disk class is exactly $$F(z) = 2i f(m(z)), \qquad f(\zeta) = \frac{F(m^{-1}(\zeta))}{2i}. \qquad (2.3)$$ For $F \in \mathcal{K}$, define the holomorphic function $Q_F(z) = \frac{1}{F'(z)}$.
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Verification of the Cayley Correspondence (2.3) (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $m : \mathbb{H} \to \mathbb{D}$ is a Biholomorphism: For $z = x+iy$, $|z-i|^2 = x^2 + (y-1)^2$ and $|z+i|^2 = x^2 + (y+1)^2$. Thus $|z+i|^2 - |z-i|^2 = 4y > 0 \iff y > 0 \iff |m(z)| < 1$. Solving $\zeta = \frac{z-i}{z+i}$ for $z$ gives $\zeta z + i\zeta = z - i \implies z(1-\zeta) = i(1+\zeta) \implies m^{-1}(\zeta) = i\frac{1+\zeta}{1-\zeta}$.
  2. Derivative Normalization at $z = i$: By the quotient rule, $m'(z) = \frac{(z+i) - (z-i)}{(z+i)^2} = \frac{2i}{(z+i)^2}$. At $z = i$, $m(i) = 0$ and $m'(i) = \frac{2i}{(2i)^2} = \frac{1}{2i}$. Therefore, for $f \in \mathcal{S}$, $F(z) = 2i f(m(z))$ satisfies $F(i) = 2i f(0) = 0$ and $$F'(z) = 2i f'(m(z)) m'(z) = \frac{-4}{(z+i)^2} f'(m(z)), \qquad F'(i) = 2i f'(0)\cdot \frac{1}{2i} = 1.$$

def H : Set ℂ := {z | 0 < z.im}

def m (z : ℂ) : ℂ := (z - I) / (z + I)

def cayley (w : ℂ) : ℂ := I * (1 + w) / (1 - w)

Lemma 2.2 (Compactness and uniform local bounds)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section2.lean:359–396 Section2_Dyadic.lean:57–205
▸
Lemma 2.2 Statement · 01-preliminaries.tex:69–87 · Eqs (2.4)–(2.5) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
The space $\mathcal{K}$ is compact and metrizable. For every integer $j \ge 0$, the evaluations $(F,z) \mapsto F^{(j)}(z)$ and $(F,z) \mapsto Q_F^{(j)}(z)$ are jointly continuous on $\mathcal{K} \times \mathbb{H}$ and bounded on $\mathcal{K} \times E$ for every compact $E \subset \mathbb{H}$. For every $C_0 \ge 0$, $$|Q_F(x+iy)| \le \frac{(C_0^2+4)^2}{y} \quad \bigl(F \in \mathcal{K},\ |x| \le C_0,\ 0 < y \le 1\bigr). \qquad (2.4)$$ In particular the bound is $64/y$ when $|x| \le 2$. Also, $$|F(z)| \ge \frac{1}{16} \quad \text{if } F \in \mathcal{K} \text{ and } |m(z)| \ge \frac{1}{2}. \qquad (2.5)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Role of Lemma 2.2 in the Architecture (~10× Depth)

Lemma 2.2 provides four essential properties used throughout Sections 2–6:

  1. Compactness of $\mathcal{K}$: Allows $C(\mathcal{K})$ to be a Banach lattice with supremum norm where positive functionals correspond to Radon/Borel measures (Lemma 3.1) and continuous functions attain their maxima.
  2. Joint Continuity & Compactness Bounds for Jets $F^{(j)}, Q_F^{(j)}$: Bounds $B_E = \max_{G\in\mathcal{K}, w\in E} |Q_G(w)| < \infty$ in Proposition 2.4 and justifies differentiating $\mathbb{E}|Q_F(x+iy)|^2$ twice under the integral sign in Lemma 3.2.
  3. Explicit Strip Bound (2.4): Gives $\|L^n\| \le 625 \cdot 4^n$ ($\rho \le 4$) at $C_0=1$ and bounds the horizontal boundary endpoints $\|A_{\pm 1+v+iv}\| \le 64^2 v^{-2}$ at $C_0=2$ in Lemma 3.1.
  4. Zero Separation (2.5): Ensures $F(z)$ is bounded away from $0$ on $S_0 = \{|m(z)| \ge 1/2\}$, so $\int_{S_0} |F'|^2 |F|^{-4}\,dA \le \int_{|w|\ge 1/16} |w|^{-4}\,dA = 256\pi$ in Lemma 4.2.

Proof of Lemma 2.2 · Step 1 · 01-preliminaries.tex:90–107 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math & Bypass 1)
The upper growth bound and (2.3) give local uniform boundedness. Montel's theorem therefore gives a locally uniformly convergent subsequence from every sequence in $\mathcal{K}$. Cauchy's formula preserves the derivative normalization, so a limit $F$ has $F'(i) = 1$ and is nonconstant. For any fixed $z_0 \in \mathbb{H}$, apply Hurwitz's theorem to $F_n(z) - F_n(z_0)$ on $\mathbb{H}\setminus\{z_0\}$... Thus $F$ is injective and belongs to $\mathcal{K}$. Cauchy's formula gives locally uniform convergence of all derivatives; the limiting derivative is nonzero, so reciprocals also converge locally uniformly.
🔍 Level 2 — Normal Families vs. Formal Bypass 1 (Arzelà–Ascoli + Uniform Koebe Separation) (~10× Depth)
Formal Bypass 1 in brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean: Instead of formalizing Montel's and Hurwitz's theorems on $\operatorname{Hol}(\mathbb{H},\mathbb{C})$, our Lean 4 proof establishes compactness of $\mathcal{S}$ (and hence $\mathcal{K}$ via the homeomorphism $F = 2i f\circ m$) directly in `UniformOnFun mathbb{D} ℂ` using three elementary steps:
  1. Equicontinuity via Koebe Lipschitz Bound: On any closed subdisk $\overline{B}(0, R) \subset \mathbb{D}$ ($0 < R < 1$), Lemma 2.1 gives $\sup_{|z|\le R} |f'(z)| \le \frac{1+R}{(1-R)^3}$. By the mean value inequality on the convex ball $\overline{B}(0,R)$, every $f \in \mathcal{S}$ is $\frac{1+R}{(1-R)^3}$-Lipschitz on $\overline{B}(0,R)$, and $|f(z)| \le \frac{R}{(1-R)^2}$. Mathlib's Arzelà–Ascoli theorem (`UniformOnFun.isCompact_of_equicontinuous`) then implies precompactness!
  2. Closedness of Holomorphicity & Normalization: By Cauchy's integral formula on circles (`Complex.hasDerivAt_of_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn`), locally uniform limits of holomorphic functions on $\mathbb{D}$ are holomorphic, and their $j$-th derivatives converge locally uniformly (`tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_iteratedDeriv_open`), preserving $f(0)=0$ and $f'(0)=1$.
  3. Bypassing Hurwitz's Theorem via Quantitative Separation: Fix any $z \ne w \in \mathbb{D}$. Set $R_z = 1 - |z| > 0$ and $r = \min(|w-z|, R_z/2) > 0$. By Lemma 2.1's local Koebe quarter theorem applied to $f$ on $B(z, r) \subset \mathbb{D}$, $B\bigl(f(z), r|f'(z)|/4\bigr) \subset f(B(z,r))$. Since $f$ is injective on $\mathbb{D}$ and $w \notin B(z, r)$, $f(w)$ cannot lie in $f(B(z,r))$, so $$|f(w) - f(z)| \ge \frac{r}{4}|f'(z)| \ge \frac{r(1-|z|)}{4(1+|z|)^3} =: c(z,w) > 0.$$ Because $c(z,w) > 0$ is independent of $f \in \mathcal{S}$, any pointwise limit $f = \lim f_n$ immediately satisfies $|f(w) - f(z)| \ge c(z,w) > 0$, proving injectivity without Hurwitz's theorem!

-- Source: brennan/Koebe.lean#L2106-L2130
lemma schlicht_uniform_separation {z w : ℂ} (hz : z ∈ disk) (hw : w ∈ disk)
    (hne : z ≠ w) : ∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ ∀ f : ℂ → ℂ, IsClassS f → c ≤ ‖f w - f z‖ := by
{
  have hz1 : ‖z‖ < 1 := by simpa [disk] using hz
  let R := min ((1 - ‖z‖) / 2) (‖w - z‖ / 2)
  have hR : 0 < R := lt_min (by linarith)
    (div_pos (norm_pos_iff.mpr (sub_ne_zero.mpr hne.symm)) (by norm_num))
  have hR1 : R ≤ (1 - ‖z‖) / 2 := min_le_left _ _
  have hR2 : R ≤ ‖w - z‖ / 2 := min_le_right _ _
  have hsub : Metric.ball z R ⊆ disk := by
  {
    intro v hv
    have hvR : ‖v - z‖ < R := by simpa [dist_eq_norm] using hv
    have ht := norm_add_le (v - z) z
    rw [sub_add_cancel] at ht
    simp only [disk, Metric.mem_ball, dist_zero_right]
    linarith
  }
  refine ⟨R * ((1 - ‖z‖) / (1 + ‖z‖)^3) / 4, by positivity, ?_⟩
  intro f hf
  have h := schlicht_separation hf hz hw hR hsub (by linarith [norm_nonneg (w - z)])
  apply le_trans _ h
  exact div_le_div_of_nonneg_right
    (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (schlicht_deriv_bounds hf hz).1 hR.le) (by norm_num)
}

Proof of Lemma 2.2 · Step 2 · 01-preliminaries.tex:109–130 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
For the explicit estimate, put $D = |x+i(y+1)|^2$ and $r = |m(x+iy)|$. Then $$1 - r^2 = \frac{4y}{D}, \qquad |Q_F(x+iy)| = \frac{D}{4|f'(m(x+iy))|}.$$ The lower derivative bound in (2.1) gives $$|Q_F(x+iy)| \le \frac{D(1+r)^3}{4(1-r)} = \frac{D^2(1+r)^4}{16y} \le \frac{D^2}{y}.$$ When $|x| \le C_0$ and $y \le 1$, this proves (2.4). Finally $|m(z)| < 1/7$ on $B(i, 1/4)$, while (2.2) gives $B(0, 1/16) \subset F(B(i, 1/4))$. Injectivity prevents any point outside $B(i, 1/4)$ from having an image of modulus less than $1/16$. This proves (2.5). $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Step-by-Step Algebraic Derivation of (2.4) and (2.5) (~10× Depth)
  1. Exact Identity $1 - r^2 = 4y/D$: For $z = x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$, $z + i = x + i(y+1)$ has squared modulus $D = |z+i|^2 = x^2 + (y+1)^2$, while $z - i = x + i(y-1)$ has squared modulus $|z-i|^2 = x^2 + (y-1)^2$. Thus $r = |m(z)| = \frac{|z-i|}{|z+i|}$ satisfies $$1 - r^2 = \frac{|z+i|^2 - |z-i|^2}{|z+i|^2} = \frac{\bigl(x^2 + y^2 + 2y + 1\bigr) - \bigl(x^2 + y^2 - 2y + 1\bigr)}{D} = \frac{4y}{D}.$$
  2. Conversion from $f'$ to $Q_F$: Since $F'(z) = \frac{-4}{(z+i)^2} f'(m(z))$, taking reciprocals and moduli gives $$|Q_F(z)| = \frac{1}{|F'(z)|} = \frac{|z+i|^2}{4|f'(m(z))|} = \frac{D}{4|f'(m(z))|}.$$
  3. Applying Lower Distortion $|f'(w)| \ge \frac{1-r}{(1+r)^3}$: Since $1 - r = \frac{1-r^2}{1+r} = \frac{4y}{D(1+r)}$, we obtain $$|Q_F(z)| \le \frac{D}{4} \cdot \frac{(1+r)^3}{1-r} = \frac{D(1+r)^3}{4} \cdot \frac{D(1+r)}{4y} = \frac{D^2(1+r)^4}{16y}.$$ Since $r < 1$, $(1+r)^4 \le 2^4 = 16$, so $\frac{(1+r)^4}{16} \le 1$ and $|Q_F(x+iy)| \le \frac{D^2}{y}$. For $|x| \le C_0$ and $0 < y \le 1$, $D = x^2 + (y+1)^2 \le C_0^2 + 2^2 = C_0^2 + 4$, giving $|Q_F(x+iy)| \le \frac{(C_0^2+4)^2}{y}$. When $C_0 = 2$, $(2^2+4)^2 = 8^2 = 64$.
  4. Zero Separation $|F(z)| \ge 1/16$ for $|m(z)| \ge 1/2$: Using $F(z) = 2i f(m(z))$ and the lower growth bound $|f(w)| \ge \frac{|w|}{4}$ (or $\frac{|w|}{(1+|w|)^2}$), for $|m(z)| \ge 1/2$ we immediately get $|F(z)| = 2|f(m(z))| \ge 2 \cdot \frac{1/2}{4} = \frac{1}{4} \ge \frac{1}{16}$.

theorem lemma_2_2_bounds (F : KClass) (x y C₀ : ℝ) (hy : 0 < y) (hy1 : y ≤ 1) (hx : |x| ≤ C₀) :
    ‖Q F.F ((x : ℂ) + (y : ℂ) * I)‖ ≤ (C₀^2 + 4)^2 / y := by
{
  set z := (x : ℂ) + (y : ℂ) * I
  have hz_re : z.re = x := by simp [z]
  have hz_im : z.im = y := by simp [z]
  have hz : z ∈ H := by dsimp [H]; rw [hz_im]; exact hy
  have hgen := norm_Q_le_general F hz
  rw [hz_re, hz_im] at hgen
  have hC0 : 0 ≤ C₀ := le_trans (abs_nonneg x) hx
  have hx2 : x^2 ≤ C₀^2 := by nlinarith [sq_abs x, (sq_le_sq₀ (abs_nonneg x) hC0).mpr hx]
  have hD_le : x^2 + (y + 1)^2 ≤ C₀^2 + 4 := by nlinarith
  have hD2_le : (x^2 + (y + 1)^2)^2 ≤ (C₀^2 + 4)^2 :=
    sq_le_sq₀ (by positivity) (by positivity) |>.mpr hD_le
  exact hgen.trans (div_le_div_of_nonneg_right hD2_le hy.le)
}

2.2 Affine rescaling and dyadic growth

▸
§2.2 · 01-preliminaries.tex:134–156 · Eqs (2.6)–(2.8) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Identify $z = x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$ with the affine map $w \mapsto x+yw$. Composition of these source maps is the product $$z \cdot w = x + yw, \qquad z^\# = \frac{-x+i}{y}, \qquad (2.6)$$ with identity $i$ and inverse $z^\#$. For $F \in \mathcal{K}$ define $$T_z F(w) = \frac{F(z\cdot w) - F(z)}{y F'(z)}. \qquad (2.7)$$ It belongs to $\mathcal{K}$. On the space $C(\mathcal{K})$ of continuous complex-valued functions, equipped with its supremum norm, define $$A_z \Phi(F) = |Q_F(z)|^2 \Phi(T_z F), \qquad L = \frac{1}{2}\bigl(A_{-1/2+i/2} + A_{1/2+i/2}\bigr). \qquad (2.8)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Group Structure of $(\mathbb{H}, \cdot)$ and Rerooting Normalization (~10× Depth)
  1. The $ax+b$ Lie Group Structure on $\mathbb{H}$: Writing $z_1 = x_1 + i y_1$ and $z_2 = x_2 + i y_2$ ($y_1, y_2 > 0$), the action $z_1 \cdot z_2 = x_1 + y_1(x_2 + i y_2) = (x_1 + y_1 x_2) + i(y_1 y_2) \in \mathbb{H}$ corresponds to matrix multiplication of upper-triangular affine matrices: $$\begin{pmatrix} y_1 & x_1 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} y_2 & x_2 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} y_1 y_2 & x_1 + y_1 x_2 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix}.$$ The identity matrix $\begin{pmatrix} 1 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix}$ corresponds to $0 + 1i = i$, and the inverse matrix $\begin{pmatrix} 1/y & -x/y \\ 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix}$ corresponds to $z^\# = \frac{-x+i}{y}$, satisfying $z \cdot z^\# = z^\# \cdot z = i$.
  2. Why $T_z F \in \mathcal{K}$: Since $w \mapsto z \cdot w = x + yw$ ($y > 0$) is a biholomorphism of $\mathbb{H}$ with derivative $\frac{d}{dw}(z\cdot w) = y$, the map $T_z F$ is holomorphic and injective on $\mathbb{H}$. At $w = i$, $z \cdot i = x + yi = z$, so $$T_z F(i) = \frac{F(z) - F(z)}{y F'(z)} = 0, \qquad (T_z F)'(w) = \frac{y F'(z\cdot w)}{y F'(z)} = \frac{F'(z\cdot w)}{F'(z)} \implies (T_z F)'(i) = 1.$$
File: brennan/Section2.lean (lines 400–534) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section2.lean#L32-L32, brennan/Section2.lean#L38-L39, brennan/Section2.lean#L518-L545
def aff (z w : ℂ) : ℂ := (z.re : ℂ) + (z.im : ℂ) * w

noncomputable def T (z : ℂ) (F : ℂ → ℂ) (w : ℂ) : ℂ :=
  (F (aff z w) - F z) / ((z.im : ℂ) * deriv F z)

lemma T_in_K (F : KClass) (z : ℂ) (hz : z ∈ H) :
    UnivalentOn (T z F.F) H ∧ T z F.F I = 0 ∧ deriv (T z F.F) I = 1 := by
{
  refine ⟨T_univalent F z hz, T_zero F z hz, T_deriv_I F z hz⟩
}
Lemma 2.3 (Affine identities and transfer growth)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section2.lean:465–534 Section2_Dyadic.lean:210–790
▸
Lemma 2.3 Statement · 01-preliminaries.tex:158–184 · Eqs (2.9)–(2.11) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
For $z, w \in \mathbb{H}$ and $F \in \mathcal{K}$, $$Q_{T_z F}(w) = \frac{Q_F(z\cdot w)}{Q_F(z)}, \qquad T_w(T_z F) = T_{z\cdot w} F, \qquad A_z A_w = A_{z\cdot w}. \qquad (2.9)$$ Each $A_z$ is bounded on $C(\mathcal{K})$, and for fixed $\Phi \in C(\mathcal{K})$ the map $z \mapsto A_z\Phi$ is continuous in the supremum norm. For $n \ge 0$, put $v_n = 2^{-n}$ and $u_{n,j} = -1 + (2j+1)v_n$, $0 \le j < 2^n$. Then $$L^n = v_n \sum_{j=0}^{2^n-1} A_{u_{n,j}+i v_n}, \qquad \|L^n\| = \max_{F \in \mathcal{K}} L^n\mathbf{1}(F). \qquad (2.10)$$ The limit $$\rho = \lim_{n\to\infty} \|L^n\|^{1/n} = \inf_{n\ge 1} \|L^n\|^{1/n} \qquad (2.11)$$ exists and satisfies $1 \le \rho \le 4$. If $\lambda > \rho$, there is $C_\lambda < \infty$ such that $\|L^n\| \le C_\lambda \lambda^n$ for all $n \ge 0$.
🔍 Level 2 — Overview of the Cocycle Semigroup & Spectral Radius $\rho$ (~10× Depth)

Equation (2.9) shows that $z \mapsto T_z$ is a right action of $(\mathbb{H}, \cdot)$ on $\mathcal{K}$ ($T_w(T_z F) = T_{z\cdot w}F$) and $z \mapsto |Q_F(z)|^2$ is a multiplicative cocycle over that action ($|Q_F(z\cdot w)|^2 = |Q_F(z)|^2 |Q_{T_z F}(w)|^2$). Consequently, $z \mapsto A_z$ is a covector representation ($A_z A_w = A_{z\cdot w}$) on $C(\mathcal{K})$. Because $L = \frac{1}{2}(A_{-1/2+i/2} + A_{1/2+i/2})$ is a convex combination of two affine operators at height $1/2$, its $n$-th power $L^n$ averages over all $2^n$ binary words of length $n$, which are precisely the $2^n$ dyadic midpoints $u_{n,j} + i 2^{-n}$ above $[-1,1]$.

File: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean (lines 220–255) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L220-L235
lemma A_weight_cocycle (F : KClass) (z w : ℂ) (hz : z ∈ H) (hw : w ∈ H) :
    A_weight z F.F * A_weight w (T z F.F) = A_weight (aff z w) F.F := by
{
  dsimp [A_weight]
  have hz_im : (z.im : ℂ) ≠ 0 := Complex.ofReal_ne_zero.mpr (ne_of_gt hz)
  have hF_diff : DifferentiableAt ℂ F.F (aff z w) :=
    (F.univalent.1 (aff z w) (aff_mem_H hz hw)).differentiableAt (isOpen_H.mem_nhds (aff_mem_H hz hw))
  rw [lemma_2_3_Q F.F z w hF_diff hz_im, norm_div, div_pow]
  have hdF_z : deriv F.F z ≠ 0 := deriv_ne_zero_of_univalentOn isOpen_H F.univalent hz
  have hQz_ne : ‖Q F.F z‖^2 ≠ 0 := by
  {
    dsimp [Q]
    exact pow_ne_zero 2 (norm_ne_zero_iff.mpr (one_div_ne_zero hdF_z))
  }
  exact mul_div_cancel₀ _ hQz_ne
}

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L242-L256
lemma lemma_2_3_A_op (z w : ℂ) (hz : z ∈ H) (hw : w ∈ H) (Phi : C_KClass) :
    A_op_val z hz (A_op_val w hw Phi) = A_op_val (aff z w) (aff_H z w hz hw) Phi := by
{
  funext F
  dsimp [A_op_val]
  rw [← mul_assoc]
  have h_wt : (A_weight z F.F : ℂ) * (A_weight w (T z F.F) : ℂ) = (A_weight (aff z w) F.F : ℂ) := by
  {
    exact_mod_cast A_weight_cocycle F z w hz hw
  }
  rw [h_wt]
  congr 2
  apply KClass_ext
  exact lemma_2_3_KClass F z w hz hw
}
Proof of Lemma 2.3 (Click any step below for Level 2 10× Math & Level 3 Lean 4):
▸
Proof of Lemma 2.3 · Step 1 · 01-preliminaries.tex:187–200 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Differentiating (2.7) proves the first identity in (2.9). Applying the normalization twice proves the second. In the operator product the weights telescope: $$|Q_F(z)|^2 |Q_{T_z F}(w)|^2 = |Q_F(z\cdot w)|^2.$$ This gives the third identity, with its displayed order of multiplication. Joint continuity of $(z,F) \mapsto T_z F$ follows from locally uniform convergence on affine images of compact source sets and the nonzero normalizing denominator. Together with Lemma 2.2, this proves boundedness of $A_z$ and supremum-norm continuity of $z \mapsto A_z\Phi$.
🔍 Level 2 — Explicit Algebraic Proof of (2.9) and Supremum-Norm Continuity (~10× Depth)
  1. First Identity $Q_{T_z F}(w) = Q_F(z\cdot w)/Q_F(z)$: Since $(T_z F)'(w) = \frac{d}{dw}\left(\frac{F(x+yw)-F(z)}{y F'(z)}\right) = \frac{y F'(x+yw)}{y F'(z)} = \frac{F'(z\cdot w)}{F'(z)}$, taking reciprocals gives $Q_{T_z F}(w) = \frac{1}{(T_z F)'(w)} = \frac{F'(z)}{F'(z\cdot w)} = \frac{Q_F(z\cdot w)}{Q_F(z)}$.
  2. Second Identity $T_w(T_z F) = T_{z\cdot w}F$: Let $z = x+iy$ and $w = u+iv$, so $z\cdot w = (x+yu) + i(yv)$ has imaginary part $yv$. For any $\zeta \in \mathbb{H}$, associativity $z\cdot(w\cdot\zeta) = (z\cdot w)\cdot\zeta$ and $z\cdot w = z\cdot(w\cdot i)$ give $$T_w(T_z F)(\zeta) = \frac{(T_z F)(w\cdot\zeta) - (T_z F)(w)}{v(T_z F)'(w)} = \frac{\frac{F(z\cdot(w\cdot\zeta))-F(z)}{y F'(z)} - \frac{F(z\cdot w)-F(z)}{y F'(z)}}{v \frac{F'(z\cdot w)}{F'(z)}} = \frac{F((z\cdot w)\cdot\zeta) - F(z\cdot w)}{(yv) F'(z\cdot w)} = T_{z\cdot w}F(\zeta).$$
  3. Third Identity $A_z A_w = A_{z\cdot w}$: Evaluating $A_z(A_w\Phi)$ at $F \in \mathcal{K}$: $$(A_z(A_w\Phi))(F) = |Q_F(z)|^2 (A_w\Phi)(T_z F) = |Q_F(z)|^2 |Q_{T_z F}(w)|^2 \Phi(T_w(T_z F)) = |Q_F(z\cdot w)|^2 \Phi(T_{z\cdot w}F) = (A_{z\cdot w}\Phi)(F).$$
  4. Supremum-Norm Continuity of $z \mapsto A_z\Phi$: For fixed $\Phi \in C(\mathcal{K})$, $(z, F) \mapsto |Q_F(z)|^2 \Phi(T_z F)$ is continuous on $\mathbb{H} \times \mathcal{K}$. Since $\mathcal{K}$ is compact, any continuous function on $\mathbb{H} \times \mathcal{K}$ is uniformly continuous on $K_0 \times \mathcal{K}$ for every compact neighborhood $K_0 \subset \mathbb{H}$, which means $z \mapsto A_z\Phi \in C(\mathcal{K})$ is continuous with respect to $\|\cdot\|_\infty$.

Proof of Lemma 2.3 · Step 2 · 01-preliminaries.tex:201–229 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
A word of length $n$ in the affine maps $-1/2+i/2$ and $1/2+i/2$ has height $2^{-n}$ and real part $\sum_{l=1}^n \sigma_l 2^{-l}$, where $\sigma_l \in \{-1,1\}$. Writing $\sigma_l = 2\epsilon_l - 1$ and $j = \sum_{l=1}^n \epsilon_l 2^{n-l}$ gives real part $-1 + (2j+1)2^{-n}$. Expansion of $L^n$ proves the grid formula (2.10). Testing with $\Phi = \mathbf{1}$ proves $\|L^n\| = \max_{F\in\mathcal{K}} L^n\mathbf{1}(F)$. At $F(z) = z-i$ every summand equals $1$, so $\|L^n\| \ge 1$. Since $|u_{n,j}| \le 1$, (2.4) gives $\|L^n\| \le 625\cdot 4^n$. Subadditivity of $\log\|L^n\|$ proves (2.11), $1 \le \rho \le 4$, and $\|L^n\| \le C_\lambda \lambda^n$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Binary Tree Induction, Fekete Subadditivity & Bounds $1 \le \rho \le 4$ (~10× Depth)
  1. Inductive Proof of the Dyadic Grid Formula: For $n=0$, $v_0=1$ and $u_{0,0}=0$, so $v_0 A_{0+i} = A_i = I = L^0$. Suppose $L^n = 2^{-n}\sum_{j=0}^{2^n-1} A_{u_{n,j}+i 2^{-n}}$. Since $L = \frac{1}{2}(A_{-1/2+i/2} + A_{1/2+i/2})$ and $A_z A_w = A_{z\cdot w}$, we have $$\left(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{i}{2}\right)\cdot(u_{n,j}+i 2^{-n}) = -\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2}\bigl(-1 + (2j+1)2^{-n}\bigr) + i 2^{-(n+1)} = -1 + (2j+1)2^{-(n+1)} + i v_{n+1} = u_{n+1, j} + i v_{n+1},$$ $$\left(+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{i}{2}\right)\cdot(u_{n,j}+i 2^{-n}) = +\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{2}\bigl(-1 + (2j+1)2^{-n}\bigr) + i 2^{-(n+1)} = -1 + (2(j+2^n)+1)2^{-(n+1)} + i v_{n+1} = u_{n+1, j+2^n} + i v_{n+1}.$$ Summing $j = 0, \dots, 2^n-1$ covers $0, \dots, 2^{n+1}-1$ once each with weight $\frac{1}{2}\cdot 2^{-n} = 2^{-(n+1)} = v_{n+1}$!
  2. Why $F_0(z) = z - i \in \mathcal{K}$ Gives $\|L^n\| \ge 1$: $F_0(z) = z - i$ is univalent on $\mathbb{H}$ with $F_0(i)=0$ and $F'_0(z) \equiv 1$ ($Q_{F_0} \equiv 1$). Under the Cayley map, $F_0(z) = 2i \frac{z-i}{2i} = 2i \frac{m(z)}{1-m(z)}$ corresponds to the half-plane Koebe-type map $f_0(\zeta) = \frac{\zeta}{1-\zeta} \in \mathcal{S}$. Thus $(L^n\mathbf{1})(F_0) = 2^{-n}\sum_{j=0}^{2^n-1} 1^2 = 1 \implies \|L^n\| \ge 1 \implies \rho \ge 1$.
  3. Upper Bound $\|L^n\| \le 625 \cdot 4^n$: Since $|u_{n,j}| \le 1$ and $v_n = 2^{-n} \le 1$, Lemma 2.2 with $C_0 = 1$ gives $|Q_F(u_{n,j}+i v_n)| \le \frac{(1^2+4)^2}{2^{-n}} = 25 \cdot 2^n$, so $|Q_F|^2 \le 625 \cdot 4^n$. Averaging over the $2^n$ points gives $(L^n\mathbf{1})(F) \le 625 \cdot 4^n$, hence $\rho = \inf_{n\ge 1}\|L^n\|^{1/n} \le (625 \cdot 4^n)^{1/n} \to 4$.

lemma rho_ge_one : 1 ≤ rho := by
{
  rw [rho, Real.one_le_exp_iff]
  exact ge_of_tendsto tendsto_log_L_norm
    (Eventually.of_forall (fun n => div_nonneg (Real.log_nonneg (one_le_L_norm n)) (Nat.cast_nonneg n)))
}

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L714-L733
lemma rho_le_four : rho ≤ 4 := by
{
  have ht : Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => Real.log 625 / (n : ℝ) + Real.log 4) atTop (𝓝 (Real.log 4)) := by
  {
    simpa using (tendsto_const_nhds.div_atTop tendsto_natCast_atTop_atTop).add_const (Real.log 4)
  }
  have hl : rho_log ≤ Real.log 4 := by
  {
    apply le_of_tendsto_of_tendsto tendsto_log_L_norm ht
    filter_upwards [eventually_gt_atTop (0 : ℕ)] with n hn
    have hnR : (0 : ℝ) < n := by exact_mod_cast hn
    have hlog := Real.log_le_log (L_norm_pos n) (L_norm_le_bound n)
    rw [Real.log_mul (by norm_num) (by positivity), Real.log_pow] at hlog
    calc
      Real.log (L_norm n) / (n : ℝ) ≤ (Real.log 625 + (n : ℝ) * Real.log 4) / (n : ℝ) :=
        div_le_div_of_nonneg_right hlog hnR.le
      _ = Real.log 625 / (n : ℝ) + Real.log 4 := by field_simp
  }
  simpa [rho, Real.exp_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4)] using Real.exp_le_exp.mpr hl
}

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L736-L739
theorem rho_bounds : 1 ≤ rho ∧ rho ≤ 4 := by
{
  exact ⟨rho_ge_one, rho_le_four⟩
}

2.3 From dyadic samples to disk integral means

Proposition 2.4 (Uniform disk comparison)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section2_Dyadic.lean:925–1472
▸
Proposition 2.4 Statement · 01-preliminaries.tex:237–247 · Eq (2.12) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
If $\rho \le 2$, then for every $\varepsilon > 0$ there is $C_\varepsilon < \infty$ such that $$M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C_\varepsilon (1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon} \quad \bigl(f \in \mathcal{S},\ 1/2 \le r < 1\bigr). \qquad (2.12)$$ The constant depends only on $\varepsilon$.
🔍 Level 2 — Geometric Intuition Behind Proposition 2.4 (~10× Depth)

The Cayley inverse $m^{-1}(w) = i\frac{1+w}{1-w}$ sends the point $w = 1 \in \partial\mathbb{D}$ to $\infty$, so a full circle $|w|=r$ in $\mathbb{D}$ maps to an unbounded circle in $\mathbb{H}$. To avoid unbounded horizontal coordinates in $\mathbb{H}$, we restrict $\theta$ to the arc $I = [3\pi/4, 5\pi/4]$ opposite $w=1$ (centered at $w = -1$). On this arc, $m^{-1}(re^{i\theta}) = x(\theta) + iy(\theta)$ stays inside the bounded strip $|x(\theta)| \le 1$ at height $y(\theta) \asymp 1-r$. We compare $x(\theta)+iy(\theta)$ to the $2^n$ dyadic grid points $u_{n,j} + i 2^{-n}$ at scale $2^{-n} \asymp 1-r$, and then cover the full circle $[-\pi,\pi]$ by applying the same bound to the four rotated functions $f_\alpha(w) = \alpha^{-1}f(\alpha w) \in \mathcal{S}$ ($\alpha \in \{1, i, -1, -i\}$).

File: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean (lines 1437–1472) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L1437-L1472
theorem prop_2_4_uniform_disk_comparison :
    rho ≤ 2 →
    ∀ ε > 0, ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 →
      integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) := by
{
  intro h_rho ε hε
  have hlam : 2 < (2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε) := by
  {
    simpa only [Real.rpow_one] using
      Real.rpow_lt_rpow_of_exponent_lt (by norm_num) (by linarith : (1 : ℝ) < 1 + ε)
  }
  obtain ⟨K, hK, hKn⟩ := L_norm_uniform_growth h_rho hlam
  let A := (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * (1600000000 * 1250) * K * (2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε)
  refine ⟨A + 1, fun f hf r ⟨hr, hr1⟩ => ?_⟩
  obtain ⟨n, hn1, hn2⟩ := exists_nat_pow_near_of_lt_one (by linarith : 0 < 1 - r)
    (by linarith : 1 - r ≤ 1) (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 1 / 2) (by norm_num : (1 / 2 : ℝ) < 1)
  have hvδ : v_n (n + 1) ≤ 1 - r := by rw [v_n_eq_half_pow]; exact hn1.le
  have hn : 1 - r ≤ 2 * v_n (n + 1) := by rw [v_n_eq_half_pow, pow_succ]; nlinarith
  have hb := full_circle_dyadic_bound hf hr hr1 (n + 1) hvδ hn
  have hc := dyadic_growth_compare (by linarith : 0 ≤ 1 + ε) (by linarith : 0 < 1 - r) hn
  have hx : 0 ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ := by positivity
  have hy : 0 ≤ (1600000000 * 1250 : ℝ) := by positivity
  have he : ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) = -(1 + ε) := by ring
  change (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * (∫ θ in -Real.pi..Real.pi, ‖deriv f (circlePoint r θ)‖ ^ (-2 : ℝ)) ≤ _
  calc
    _ ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * ((1600000000 * 1250) * L_norm (n + 1)) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hb hx
    _ ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * ((1600000000 * 1250) * (K * ((2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε)) ^ (n + 1))) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (hKn (n + 1)) hy) hx
    _ ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * ((1600000000 * 1250) * (K * ((2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε) * (1 - r) ^ (-(1 + ε))))) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hc hK.le) hy) hx
    _ = A * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) := by rw [he]; dsimp [A]; ring
    _ ≤ (A + 1) * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (by linarith) (Real.rpow_nonneg (by linarith) _)
}
Proof of Proposition 2.4 (Click any step below for Level 2 10× Math & Level 3 Lean 4):
▸
Proof of Prop 2.4 · Step 1 · 01-preliminaries.tex:250–273 · Eq (2.13) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Fix $f \in \mathcal{S}$ and $1/2 \le r < 1$. Put $\delta = 1-r$ and choose $n \ge 1$ with $v = 2^{-n} \le \delta < 2v$. On the fixed angular interval $I = [3\pi/4, 5\pi/4]$, write $m^{-1}(re^{i\theta}) = x(\theta) + iy(\theta)$ and $D(\theta) = 1 - 2r\cos\theta + r^2$. The inverse formula gives $$x(\theta) = \frac{-2r\sin\theta}{D(\theta)}, \qquad y(\theta) = \frac{1-r^2}{D(\theta)}. \qquad (2.13)$$ On $I$ we have $\cos\theta \le 0$ and hence $D \ge 1+r^2 \ge 2r$; therefore $|x| \le |\sin\theta| < 1$. Also $$\frac{\delta}{2} \le y \le 2\delta, \qquad \frac{dx}{d\theta} = \frac{4r^2 - 2r(1+r^2)\cos\theta}{D(\theta)^2} \ge \frac{1}{16}.$$
🔍 Level 2 — Complete Algebraic & Calculus Verification of (2.13) and the Arc Bounds (~10× Depth)
  1. Real and Imaginary Parts of $m^{-1}(re^{i\theta})$: Multiply numerator and denominator of $i\frac{1+re^{i\theta}}{1-re^{i\theta}}$ by $1 - re^{-i\theta}$: $$i\frac{(1+re^{i\theta})(1-re^{-i\theta})}{|1-re^{i\theta}|^2} = i\frac{1 - r^2 + r(e^{i\theta}-e^{-i\theta})}{1 - 2r\cos\theta + r^2} = i\frac{1 - r^2 + 2ir\sin\theta}{D(\theta)} = \frac{-2r\sin\theta}{D(\theta)} + i\frac{1-r^2}{D(\theta)}.$$
  2. Horizontal Bound $|x(\theta)| \le 1$: For $\theta \in [3\pi/4, 5\pi/4]$, $\cos\theta \le -1/\sqrt{2} < 0$, so $D(\theta) = 1 - 2r\cos\theta + r^2 \ge 1 + r^2 \ge 2r$. Thus $|x(\theta)| = \frac{2r|\sin\theta|}{D(\theta)} \le |\sin\theta| \le \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} < 1$.
  3. Vertical Bounds $\delta/2 \le y(\theta) \le 2\delta$: Since $1 \le D(\theta) \le (1+r)^2 \le 4$ and $\delta = 1-r \le 1-r^2 = \delta(1+r) \le 2\delta$, we have $$y(\theta) = \frac{\delta(1+r)}{D(\theta)} \ge \frac{\delta(1+r)}{(1+r)^2} = \frac{\delta}{1+r} \ge \frac{\delta}{2}, \qquad y(\theta) = \frac{1-r^2}{D(\theta)} \le \frac{2\delta}{1} = 2\delta.$$
  4. Derivative Formula & Lower Bound $\frac{dx}{d\theta} \ge \frac{1}{16}$: Using $D'(\theta) = 2r\sin\theta$, the quotient rule gives $$\frac{dx}{d\theta} = \frac{-2r\cos\theta \cdot D(\theta) - (-2r\sin\theta)(2r\sin\theta)}{D(\theta)^2} = \frac{-2r\cos\theta(1-2r\cos\theta+r^2) + 4r^2\sin^2\theta}{D(\theta)^2} = \frac{4r^2 - 2r(1+r^2)\cos\theta}{D(\theta)^2}.$$ Since $r \ge 1/2$ and $\cos\theta \le 0$ on $I$, the numerator is $\ge 4r^2 \ge 4(1/4) = 1$, while $D(\theta) \le (1+r)^2 \le 4 \implies D(\theta)^2 \le 16$, so $\frac{dx}{d\theta} \ge \frac{1}{16}$.
File: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean (lines 925–1085) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L863-L863
def arcD (r θ : ℝ) : ℝ := 1 + 2 * r * Real.cos θ + r ^ 2

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L865-L865
def arcX (r θ : ℝ) : ℝ := 2 * r * Real.sin θ / arcD r θ

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L867-L867
def arcY (r θ : ℝ) : ℝ := (1 - r ^ 2) / arcD r θ

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L926-L943
lemma arcX_hasDerivAt {r θ : ℝ} (hD : arcD r θ ≠ 0) :
    HasDerivAt (arcX r) (arcXDeriv r θ) θ := by
{
  have hn := (Real.hasDerivAt_sin θ).const_mul (2 * r)
  have hd := (((Real.hasDerivAt_cos θ).const_mul (2 * r)).const_add 1).add_const (r ^ 2)
  have h := hn.div hd hD
  change HasDerivAt (arcX r) _ θ at h
  have he : arcXDeriv r θ =
      (2 * r * Real.cos θ * arcD r θ - 2 * r * Real.sin θ * (2 * r * -Real.sin θ)) / (arcD r θ) ^ 2 := by
  {
    unfold arcXDeriv arcD
    congr 1
    have hc := Real.sin_sq_add_cos_sq θ
    nlinarith [sq_nonneg r]
  }
  rw [he]
  exact h
}
▸
Proof of Prop 2.4 · Step 2 · 01-preliminaries.tex:274–308 · Eq (2.14) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
The cells $[u_{n,j}-v, u_{n,j}+v]$ cover $[-1,1]$ with disjoint interiors. If $x(\theta)$ belongs to cell $j$, then $$w = \frac{x(\theta)-u_{n,j}}{v} + i\frac{y(\theta)}{v} \in E := \{u+i\eta : |u| \le 1,\ 1/2 \le \eta \le 4\}.$$ Let $B_E = \max_{G\in\mathcal{K},\, w\in E} |Q_G(w)|$, which is finite by Lemma 2.2. The first identity in (2.9) gives $$|Q_F(x(\theta)+iy(\theta))| \le B_E |Q_F(u_{n,j}+iv)|, \qquad F = 2i f\circ m. \qquad (2.14)$$ Since $|f'(m(z))|^{-2} = \frac{16}{|z+i|^4}|Q_F(z)|^2 \le 16|Q_F(z)|^2$ and $d\theta \le 16\,dx$, (2.14) and the cell lengths $2v$ give $$\int_I |f'(re^{i\theta})|^{-2}\,d\theta \le 256 B_E^2 \sum_j |Q_F(u_{n,j}+iv)|^2 \bigl|[u_{n,j}-v, u_{n,j}+v]\cap x(I)\bigr| \le 512 B_E^2 L^n\mathbf{1}(F).$$
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Unpacking of the Cell Comparison & Change of Variable (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $w \in E = [-1,1]\times[1/2,4]$: Since $x(\theta) \in [u_{n,j}-v, u_{n,j}+v]$, we have $\bigl|\frac{x(\theta)-u_{n,j}}{v}\bigr| \le 1$. Since $v \le \delta < 2v$ and $\frac{\delta}{2} \le y(\theta) \le 2\delta$, we have $\frac{1}{2} = \frac{v/2}{v} \le \frac{\delta/2}{v} \le \frac{y(\theta)}{v} \le \frac{2\delta}{v} < \frac{4v}{v} = 4$. Thus $w \in E$, and $(u_{n,j}+iv)\cdot w = x(\theta)+iy(\theta)$.
  2. Applying Cocycle (2.9): Setting $z_{n,j} = u_{n,j} + iv$ and $G = T_{z_{n,j}}F \in \mathcal{K}$, Lemma 2.3 gives $Q_F(x(\theta)+iy(\theta)) = Q_F(z_{n,j}\cdot w) = Q_F(z_{n,j}) Q_G(w)$. Since $G \in \mathcal{K}$ and $w \in E$, $|Q_G(w)| \le B_E$, proving (2.14).
  3. Why $|z+i| \ge 1$ on $\mathbb{H}$: For any $z = x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$ ($y > 0$), $|z+i|^2 = x^2 + (y+1)^2 > 1$, so $\frac{16}{|z+i|^4} \le 16$.
  4. Measure of Preimage of Cell $j$: Since $x : I \to [-1,1]$ is strictly increasing with $x'(\theta) \ge 1/16$, its inverse $\theta(x)$ has derivative $\theta'(x) \le 16$. Thus the angular preimage $x^{-1}([u_{n,j}-v, u_{n,j}+v])$ is an interval of length at most $16 \cdot (2v) = 32v$. Multiplying by $16 B_E^2 |Q_F(u_{n,j}+iv)|^2$ and summing over $0 \le j < 2^n$ gives $16 \cdot 32v B_E^2 \sum_j |Q_F(u_{n,j}+iv)|^2 = 512 B_E^2 (L^n\mathbf{1})(F)$.

-- Source: brennan/Section2_Dyadic.lean#L1200-L1206
lemma horizontalMass_dyadic_le (n : ℕ) (F : KClass) :
    horizontalMass (v_n n) F ≤ 1250 * L_norm n := by
{
  rw [horizontalMass_dyadic]
  exact (L_pow_real_le_mul_L_one n (horizontalMass 1) 1250 horizontalMass_one_le F).trans
    (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (le_L_norm n F) (by norm_num))
}

Proof of Prop 2.4 · Step 3 · 01-preliminaries.tex:310–327 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
For $\alpha \in \{1, i, -1, -i\}$, the map $f_\alpha(z) = \alpha^{-1}f(\alpha z)$ belongs to $\mathcal{S}$ and has derivative $f'(\alpha z)$. The four rotated copies of $I$ cover the full circle, with overlap only at endpoints. Applying the preceding estimate to these four maps therefore proves $M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C\|L^n\|$. If $\rho \le 2$, apply Lemma 2.3 with $\lambda = 2^{1+\varepsilon}$. It gives $$M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C C_\lambda v^{-1-\varepsilon} \le C C_\lambda 2^{1+\varepsilon}(1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}. \quad \square$$
🔍 Level 2 — Rotational Symmetry of $\mathcal{S}$ and Dyadic Scale Matching (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $f_\alpha \in \mathcal{S}$ for $|\alpha|=1$: Since $z \mapsto \alpha z$ is a bijection of $\mathbb{D}$ fixing $0$, $f_\alpha(z) = \alpha^{-1}f(\alpha z)$ is holomorphic and injective on $\mathbb{D}$ with $f_\alpha(0) = 0$ and $f'_\alpha(z) = \alpha^{-1}\alpha f'(\alpha z) = f'(\alpha z)$, so $f'_\alpha(0) = f'(0) = 1$.
  2. Covering $[-\pi, \pi]$ by Four Arcs of Length $\pi/2$: As $\theta$ ranges over $I = [3\pi/4, 5\pi/4]$ and $\alpha = e^{i k\pi/2}$ ($k \in \{0,1,2,3\}$), the angle $\theta + k\pi/2 \pmod{2\pi}$ covers $[-\pi,\pi]$. Thus $$2\pi M_{-2}[f'](r) = \sum_{k=0}^3 \int_{3\pi/4}^{5\pi/4} |f'_{i^k}(re^{i\theta})|^{-2}\,d\theta \le 4 \cdot 512 B_E^2 \|L^n\| = 2048 B_E^2 \|L^n\|.$$
  3. From $\|L^n\| \le C_\lambda \lambda^n$ to $(1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}$: Since $\rho \le 2$ and $\lambda = 2^{1+\varepsilon} > 2 \ge \rho$, Lemma 2.3 gives $\|L^n\| \le C_\lambda (2^{1+\varepsilon})^n = C_\lambda v^{-1-\varepsilon}$ where $v = 2^{-n}$. Since $\delta = 1-r < 2v \implies v^{-1} < 2(1-r)^{-1}$, raising to the positive power $1+\varepsilon$ gives $v^{-1-\varepsilon} \le 2^{1+\varepsilon}(1-r)^{-1-\varepsilon}$.

theorem prop_2_4_uniform_disk_comparison :
    rho ≤ 2 →
    ∀ ε > 0, ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 →
      integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) := by
{
  intro h_rho ε hε
  have hlam : 2 < (2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε) := by
  {
    simpa only [Real.rpow_one] using
      Real.rpow_lt_rpow_of_exponent_lt (by norm_num) (by linarith : (1 : ℝ) < 1 + ε)
  }
  obtain ⟨K, hK, hKn⟩ := L_norm_uniform_growth h_rho hlam
  let A := (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * (1600000000 * 1250) * K * (2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε)
  refine ⟨A + 1, fun f hf r ⟨hr, hr1⟩ => ?_⟩
  obtain ⟨n, hn1, hn2⟩ := exists_nat_pow_near_of_lt_one (by linarith : 0 < 1 - r)
    (by linarith : 1 - r ≤ 1) (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 1 / 2) (by norm_num : (1 / 2 : ℝ) < 1)
  have hvδ : v_n (n + 1) ≤ 1 - r := by rw [v_n_eq_half_pow]; exact hn1.le
  have hn : 1 - r ≤ 2 * v_n (n + 1) := by rw [v_n_eq_half_pow, pow_succ]; nlinarith
  have hb := full_circle_dyadic_bound hf hr hr1 (n + 1) hvδ hn
  have hc := dyadic_growth_compare (by linarith : 0 ≤ 1 + ε) (by linarith : 0 < 1 - r) hn
  have hx : 0 ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ := by positivity
  have hy : 0 ≤ (1600000000 * 1250 : ℝ) := by positivity
  have he : ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) = -(1 + ε) := by ring
  change (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * (∫ θ in -Real.pi..Real.pi, ‖deriv f (circlePoint r θ)‖ ^ (-2 : ℝ)) ≤ _
  calc
    _ ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * ((1600000000 * 1250) * L_norm (n + 1)) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hb hx
    _ ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * ((1600000000 * 1250) * (K * ((2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε)) ^ (n + 1))) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (hKn (n + 1)) hy) hx
    _ ≤ (2 * Real.pi)⁻¹ * ((1600000000 * 1250) * (K * ((2 : ℝ) ^ (1 + ε) * (1 - r) ^ (-(1 + ε))))) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hc hK.le) hy) hx
    _ = A * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) := by rw [he]; dsimp [A]; ring
    _ ≤ (A + 1) * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε) :=
      mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (by linarith) (Real.rpow_nonneg (by linarith) _)
}

3. A probability law from transfer growth

The disk estimate in Proposition 2.4 reduces our task to proving that $\rho \le 2$. We now suppose that $\rho > 2$ and construct a probability measure on the normalized family $\mathcal{K}$. Its covariance under affine changes of coordinates will turn geometric quantities into expectations at the single point $i$.

Lemma 3.1 (Weighted affine covariance)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section3.lean:960–1824
▸
Lemma 3.1 Statement · 02-weighted-law.tex:16–27 · Eq (3.1) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Suppose that $\rho > 2$, and put $\beta = \log_2 \rho > 1$. There is a Borel probability measure $P$ on $\mathcal{K}$ such that, for every $z = x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$ and every nonnegative Borel function $\Phi : \mathcal{K} \to [0, \infty]$, $$\mathbb{E}\!\left[|Q_F(z)|^2 \Phi(T_z F)\right] = y^{-\beta} \mathbb{E}\Phi(F), \qquad (3.1)$$ where $\mathbb{E}$ denotes expectation with respect to $P$.
🔍 Level 2 — Overview of the 4-Stage Construction of $P$ (~10× Depth)

Constructing $P$ from the discrete dyadic operator $L = \frac{1}{2}(A_{-1/2+i/2} + A_{1/2+i/2})$ proceeds through four stages:

  1. Stage 1 (Resolvent Eigenmeasure $\mu$): Use the resolvent $R_\tau = \sum_{n=0}^\infty \tau^{-n} L^n$ ($\tau \downarrow \rho$) at a maximizer $F_\tau$ of $R_\tau\mathbf{1}$ to extract a probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathcal{K}$ satisfying $\mu L = \rho\mu$.
  2. Stage 2 (Horizontal Periodicity $\mu A_{2+i} = \mu$): Use $\mu = \rho^{-n}\mu L^n$ and the dyadic grid formula for $L^n$ to telescope $\mu A_{2+i} - \mu$ to two boundary terms at $\pm 1 + v + iv$ ($v = 2^{-n}$), which decay like $C \rho^{-n} v \cdot v^{-2} = C(2/\rho)^n \to 0$ precisely because $\rho > 2$!
  3. Stage 3 (Horizontal Average $\bar\mu$ & Scale Average $\nu$): Average horizontally over $[0,2]$ ($\bar\mu = \frac{1}{2}\int_0^2 \mu A_{x+i}\,dx$) to get full translation invariance $\bar\mu A_{t+i} = \bar\mu$ and dyadic scaling $\bar\mu A_{i/2} = \rho\bar\mu$, then average logarithmically over $v \in [1,2]$ ($\nu = \int_1^2 v^\beta \bar\mu A_{iv}\,\frac{dv}{v}$) to get continuous scaling $\nu A_{iy} = y^{-\beta}\nu$.
  4. Stage 4 (Normalization & Borel Extension): Normalize $P = \nu/\nu(\mathbf{1})$ and extend from continuous $\Phi \in C(\mathcal{K})$ to Borel sets and nonnegative Borel functions via Riesz representation and monotone convergence.
File: brennan/Section3.lean (lines 1730–1824) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section3.lean#L1730-L1747
theorem exists_weighted_covariance_measure (h_rho : rho > 2) :
    ∃ P : MeasureTheory.Measure KClass, IsProbabilityMeasure P ∧
      (∀ (z : ℂ) (hz : z ∈ H) (Phi : KClass → ℝ),
        Continuous Phi →
        ∫ F, A_weight z F.F * Phi ⟨T z F.F, (T_KClass F z hz).1, (T_KClass F z hz).2.1, (T_KClass F z hz).2.2⟩ ∂P =
          (z.im : ℝ) ^ (-(Real.log rho / Real.log 2)) * ∫ F, Phi F ∂P) ∧
      (∀ (z : ℂ) (hz : z ∈ H) (Phi : KClass → ℝ≥0∞),
        Measurable Phi →
        ∫⁻ F, ENNReal.ofReal (A_weight z F.F) *
            Phi ⟨T z F.F, (T_KClass F z hz).1, (T_KClass F z hz).2.1, (T_KClass F z hz).2.2⟩ ∂P =
          ENNReal.ofReal ((z.im : ℝ) ^ (-(Real.log rho / Real.log 2))) * ∫⁻ F, Phi F ∂P) := by
{
  obtain ⟨P_disk, hP_disk⟩ := exists_weighted_continuous_law h_rho
  refine ⟨Measure.map toKClass (P_disk : Measure DiskClass),
    Measure.isProbabilityMeasure_map continuous_toKClass.measurable.aemeasurable,
    fun z hz Phi hPhi => weighted_covariance_pushforward_continuous P_disk hP_disk hz hPhi,
    fun z hz Phi hPhi => weighted_covariance_pushforward_lintegral P_disk hP_disk hz hPhi⟩
}

def P_eigenmeasure : MeasureTheory.Measure KClass :=
  if h : rho > 2 then (exists_weighted_covariance_measure h).choose else 0

lemma lemma_3_1_weighted_covariance_lintegral (h_rho : rho > 2) :

  rw [dif_pos h_rho]
  have h := (exists_weighted_covariance_measure h_rho).choose_spec

Proof of Lemma 3.1 (Click any step below for Level 2 10× Math & Level 3 Lean 4):
