Proof of Lemma 3.1 · Step 1 (The eigenmeasure) · 02-weighted-law.tex:35–99 · Eqs (3.2)–(3.3) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
The eigenmeasure. For $\tau > \rho$, $R_\tau = \sum_{n=0}^\infty \tau^{-n} L^n$ converges in operator norm. Set $g_\tau = R_\tau\mathbf{1}$ and $M_\tau = \max_{F\in\mathcal{K}} g_\tau(F)$. Since $L g_\tau = \tau(g_\tau - \mathbf{1}) \le \tau(1 - M_\tau^{-1})g_\tau$, iteration gives $\|L^n\| \le M_\tau \bigl[\tau(1 - M_\tau^{-1})\bigr]^n$, which forces $$M_\tau \longrightarrow \infty \qquad (\tau \downarrow \rho). \qquad (3.2)$$ Choose a maximizer $F_\tau$ and define $\mu_\tau = \frac{\delta_{F_\tau} R_\tau}{M_\tau}$. Then $\mu_\tau L = \tau\mu_\tau - \frac{\tau}{M_\tau}\delta_{F_\tau}$. Extracting a weak-* limit along $\tau_j \downarrow \rho$ yields a probability measure $\mu$ satisfying $$\mu L = \rho\mu. \qquad (3.3)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Proof of Resolvent Divergence (3.2) and Eigenmeasure (3.3) (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $L g_\tau = \tau(g_\tau - \mathbf{1}) \le \tau(1 - M_\tau^{-1})g_\tau$: Since $R_\tau = I + \sum_{n=1}^\infty \tau^{-n} L^n = I + \tau^{-1} L R_\tau$, applying to $\mathbf{1}$ gives $g_\tau = \mathbf{1} + \tau^{-1} L g_\tau \implies L g_\tau = \tau(g_\tau - \mathbf{1})$. Since $0 < g_\tau(F) \le M_\tau$ for all $F \in \mathcal{K}$, we have $\mathbf{1}(F) = 1 \ge M_\tau^{-1} g_\tau(F)$, so $g_\tau - \mathbf{1} \le (1 - M_\tau^{-1})g_\tau$.
  2. Why $M_\tau \to \infty$ as $\tau \downarrow \rho$: Because $L$ is a positive operator ($f \le h \implies Lf \le Lh$), induction on $n$ gives $L^n\mathbf{1} \le L^n g_\tau \le \bigl[\tau(1-M_\tau^{-1})\bigr]^n g_\tau \le M_\tau \bigl[\tau(1-M_\tau^{-1})\bigr]^n \mathbf{1}$. Taking $\|\cdot\|_\infty$ and $n$-th roots gives $\rho = \lim_{n\to\infty}\|L^n\|^{1/n} \le \tau(1 - M_\tau^{-1})$. If $M_{\tau_j} \le M < \infty$ along $\tau_j \downarrow \rho$, letting $j \to \infty$ gives $\rho \le \rho(1 - M^{-1})$, impossible since $\rho \ge 1 > 0$!
  3. Weak-* Compactness of State Space: For each $\Phi \in C(\mathcal{K})$, $|\mu_\tau(L\Phi) - \tau\mu_\tau(\Phi)| = \frac{\tau}{M_\tau}|\Phi(F_\tau)| \le \frac{\tau\|\Phi\|_\infty}{M_\tau} \to 0$ as $\tau \downarrow \rho$. By Tychonoff compactness of the closed unit ball of $C(\mathcal{K})^*$ (or diagonal selection on a countable dense subset of $C(\mathcal{K})$), any cluster point $\mu$ is a positive linear functional with $\mu(\mathbf{1})=1$ and $\mu(L\Phi) = \rho\mu(\Phi)$.
File: brennan/Section3.lean (lines 960–1220) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section3.lean#L930-L957
lemma exists_growth_eigenmeasure :
    ∃ μ : ProbabilityMeasure DiskClass, ∀ f : C(DiskClass, ℝ),
      (∫ x, transferOperator f x ∂(μ : Measure DiskClass)) =
        rho * (∫ x, f x ∂(μ : Measure DiskClass)) := by
{
  let τ : ℕ → ℝ := fun n => rho + 1 / ((n : ℝ) + 1)
  have hτ (n : ℕ) : rho < τ n := lt_add_of_pos_right _ (by positivity)
  choose μ hμ using fun n : ℕ => exists_resolvent_probability (hτ n)
  let U := Ultrafilter.of (atTop : Filter ℕ)
  let V := U.map μ
  let ν := V.lim
  have hν : Tendsto μ (U : Filter ℕ) (𝓝 ν) := V.le_nhds_lim
  have ht : Tendsto τ (U : Filter ℕ) (𝓝 rho) := by
  {
    have hh : Tendsto τ atTop (𝓝 rho) := by
    {
      simpa [τ] using tendsto_one_div_add_atTop_nhds_zero_nat.const_add rho
    }
    exact hh.mono_left (Ultrafilter.of_le atTop)
  }
  refine ⟨ν, fun f => ?_⟩
  have hi := (ProbabilityMeasure.continuous_integral_continuousMap f).tendsto ν |>.comp hν
  have hLi := (ProbabilityMeasure.continuous_integral_continuousMap (transferOperator f)).tendsto ν |>.comp hν
  have hb := le_of_tendsto_of_tendsto ((hLi.sub (ht.mul hi)).abs)
    ((ht.sub_const rho).mul_const ‖f‖) (Eventually.of_forall (hμ · f))
  simp only [sub_self, zero_mul] at hb
  exact sub_eq_zero.mp (abs_nonpos_iff.mp hb)
}
▸
Proof of Lemma 3.1 · Step 2 (A horizontal period) · 02-weighted-law.tex:101–124 · Eq (3.4) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
A horizontal period. For $v = 2^{-n}$ and $u_j = -1 + (2j+1)v$, $L^n = v\sum_{j=0}^{2^n-1} A_{u_j+iv}$. Right composition with $A_{2+i}$ replaces $u_j$ by $u_j + 2v$. Thus (3.3) gives the exact cancellation $$\mu A_{2+i} - \mu = \rho^{-n} v \mu\bigl(A_{1+v+iv} - A_{-1+v+iv}\bigr). \qquad (3.4)$$ Both remaining abscissae have absolute value at most $2$. The bound $|Q_F(u+iv)| \le C/v$ from Lemma 2.2 therefore implies $$\|\mu A_{2+i} - \mu\| \le C \rho^{-n} v\, v^{-2} = C(2/\rho)^n \longrightarrow 0.$$ The left side is independent of $n$, so $\mu A_{2+i} = \mu$.
🔍 Level 2 — Telescoping Sum & Why $\rho > 2$ is Essential Here (~10× Depth)
  1. Affine Shift by $2+i$: For each $j \in \{0, \dots, 2^n-1\}$, $(u_j + iv)\cdot(2+i) = u_j + v(2+i) = (u_j + 2v) + iv$. Since $u_j = -1 + (2j+1)v$, adding $2v$ gives $-1 + (2(j+1)+1)v = u_{j+1}$!
  2. Telescoping Cancellation: By (3.3), $\mu = \rho^{-n} \mu L^n$. Therefore, $$\mu A_{2+i} - \mu = \rho^{-n}\mu\bigl(L^n A_{2+i} - L^n\bigr) = \rho^{-n} v \mu\left(\sum_{j=0}^{2^n-1} A_{u_{j+1}+iv} - \sum_{j=0}^{2^n-1} A_{u_j+iv}\right).$$ All $2^n - 1$ interior terms $j = 1, \dots, 2^n-1$ cancel identically, leaving only $u_{2^n} + iv = (1+v) + iv$ and $u_0 + iv = (-1+v) + iv$!
  3. Why $\rho > 2$ Drives the Error to $0$: Since $|-1+v| \le 1 \le 2$ and $|1+v| \le 2$, Lemma 2.2 with $C_0 = 2$ bounds $|Q_F(\pm 1+v+iv)|^2 \le (64/v)^2 = 4096\,v^{-2}$. Multiplying by the prefactor $\rho^{-n} v = \rho^{-n} 2^{-n}$ gives $$|(\mu A_{2+i} - \mu)(\Phi)| \le 2 \cdot 4096\,\rho^{-n} v \cdot v^{-2} \|\Phi\|_\infty = 8192\,\left(\frac{2}{\rho}\right)^n \|\Phi\|_\infty.$$ Because $\rho > 2$, $2/\rho < 1$, so $(2/\rho)^n \to 0$ as $n \to \infty$! (Notice that at the true value $\rho = 2$, this bound is $O(1)$ and does not vanish.)
File: brennan/Section3.lean (lines 1225–1360) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section3.lean#L1154-L1167
lemma eigenfunctional_horizontal_period (Λ : C(DiskClass, ℝ) →L[ℝ] ℝ) (ρ : ℝ)
    (hρ : 2 < ρ) (hΛ : ∀ f, Λ (transferOperator f) = ρ * Λ f) (f : C(DiskClass, ℝ)) :
    Λ (affineOperator (horizontal 2) f) = Λ f := by
{
  have hρ0 : 0 < ρ := by linarith
  have hn := eigenfunctional_horizontal_period_step Λ ρ hρ0 hΛ f
  have ht : Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => (8192 * ‖Λ‖ * ‖f‖) * (2 / ρ) ^ n) atTop (𝓝 0) := by
  {
    simpa using (tendsto_pow_atTop_nhds_zero_of_lt_one (by positivity : 0 ≤ 2 / ρ)
      ((div_lt_one hρ0).mpr hρ)).const_mul (8192 * ‖Λ‖ * ‖f‖)
  }
  exact sub_eq_zero.mp (norm_eq_zero.mp
    (le_antisymm (ge_of_tendsto ht (Eventually.of_forall hn)) (norm_nonneg _)))
}
▸
Proof of Lemma 3.1 · Step 3 (Horizontal, scale & Borel averages) · 02-weighted-law.tex:125–211 · Eqs (3.5)–(3.6) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Horizontal and scale averages. Define $\bar\mu = \frac{1}{2}\int_0^2 \mu A_{x+i}\,dx$. Period-$2$ invariance gives $\bar\mu A_{t+i} = \bar\mu$ ($t \in \mathbb{R}$) (3.5) and expanding $\rho\bar\mu = \frac{1}{2}\int_0^2 \mu L A_{x+i}\,dx = \frac{1}{2}\int_{-1/2}^{3/2} \mu A_{s+i/2}\,ds$ gives $\rho\bar\mu = \bar\mu A_{i/2}$ (3.6). Thus $H(v) = v^\beta \bar\mu A_{iv}$ satisfies $H(2v) = H(v)$ and remains horizontally invariant. Setting $\nu = \int_1^2 H(v)\,\frac{dv}{v}$ gives $\nu A_{iy} = y^{-\beta}\nu$ and $P = \nu/\nu(\mathbf{1})$ satisfies $P A_{x+iy} = y^{-\beta}P$ on continuous tests, extending to all nonnegative Borel functions by Riesz representation and monotone convergence. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Full Derivation of (3.5), (3.6), Logarithmic Periodicity & Borel Extension (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $\rho\bar\mu = \bar\mu A_{i/2}$ (3.6): Since $(\pm 1/2 + i/2)\cdot(x+i) = (\pm 1/2 + x/2) + i/2$, we have $L A_{x+i} = \frac{1}{2}(A_{-1/2+x/2+i/2} + A_{1/2+x/2+i/2})$. Integrating over $x \in [0,2]$ and changing variables $s = -1/2 + x/2 \in [-1/2, 1/2]$ ($dx = 2\,ds$) in the first integral and $s = 1/2 + x/2 \in [1/2, 3/2]$ in the second combines them into $\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1/2}^{3/2} \mu A_{s+i/2}\,ds$. Since $(2+i)\cdot(s+i/2) = (s+2)+i/2$, $\mu A_{2+i} = \mu$ implies $s \mapsto \mu A_{s+i/2}$ has period $2$, so $\int_{-1/2}^{3/2} = \int_0^2$. Finally, $(s+i/2) = (s+i)\cdot(i/2)$, so $\frac{1}{2}\int_0^2 \mu A_{s+i} A_{i/2}\,ds = \bar\mu A_{i/2}$!
  2. Why $H(2v) = H(v)$ and $\nu A_{iy} = y^{-\beta}\nu$: Applying (3.6) to $A_{2iv}\Phi$ gives $\bar\mu A_{i/2} A_{2iv} = \bar\mu A_{iv} = \rho\bar\mu A_{2iv}$, so $\bar\mu A_{2iv} = \rho^{-1}\bar\mu A_{iv} = 2^{-\beta}\bar\mu A_{iv}$. Multiplying by $(2v)^\beta$ gives $H(2v) = (2v)^\beta \bar\mu A_{2iv} = v^\beta \bar\mu A_{iv} = H(v)$. Under $u = vy$ ($\frac{dv}{v} = \frac{du}{u}$), $$\nu A_{iy} = \int_1^2 v^\beta \bar\mu A_{ivy}\,\frac{dv}{v} = y^{-\beta} \int_y^{2y} H(u)\,\frac{du}{u} = y^{-\beta} \int_1^2 H(u)\,\frac{du}{u} = y^{-\beta}\nu$$ because $\int_y^{2y} H(u)\frac{du}{u} = \int_{\log y}^{\log y + \log 2} H(e^t)\,dt$ integrates a $(\log 2)$-periodic function over one full period!
  3. Intertwining Horizontal and Vertical Actions: Since $(x+iy) = (x+i)\cdot(iy)$, $A_{x+iy} = A_{x+i} A_{iy}$. Moreover, $(iv)\cdot(t+i) = vt + iv = (vt+i)\cdot(iv)$, so $\bar\mu A_{iv} A_{t+i} = \bar\mu A_{vt+i} A_{iv} = \bar\mu A_{iv}$ by (3.5). Thus $\nu A_{x+i} = \nu$, and $\nu A_{x+iy} = \nu A_{x+i} A_{iy} = \nu A_{iy} = y^{-\beta}\nu$.

Lemma 3.2 (Moments of the logarithmic derivative)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section3.lean:1836–2751
▸
Lemma 3.2 Statement · 02-weighted-law.tex:219–241 · Eqs (3.7)–(3.9) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Assume $\rho > 2$, and let $P$ and $\beta$ be as in Lemma 3.1. For $z = x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$, define $$q_F(z) = iy\frac{Q'_F(z)}{Q_F(z)} = -iy\frac{F''(z)}{F'(z)} = a_F(z) + i b_F(z), \qquad (3.7)$$ where $a_F, b_F$ are real. The variables $q_F(i)$ are bounded on $\mathcal{K}$, and $$\mathbb{E} a_F(i) = -\frac{\beta}{2}, \qquad \mathbb{E} b_F(i) = 0, \qquad \mathbb{E}|q_F(i)|^2 = \frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{4}. \qquad (3.8)$$ They also satisfy $q_{T_z F}(w) = q_F(z\cdot w)$ for $z, w \in \mathbb{H}$. (3.9)
🔍 Level 2 — Why the Moments of $q_F(i)$ Are Completely Determined by $\beta$ (~10× Depth)

Even though the probability measure $P$ on the infinite-dimensional space $\mathcal{K}$ is constructed non-constructively via a weak-* cluster point of resolvents, the expectation of $|Q_F(x+iy)|^2$ is known closed-form at every point $x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$: $\mathbb{E}|Q_F(x+iy)|^2 = y^{-\beta}$. Because $Q_F(i) = 1$ and $q_F(i) = i Q'_F(i)$ is the 1st-order Taylor jet of $Q_F$ at $z=i$, differentiating $y^{-\beta}$ once and twice at $(x,y)=(0,1)$ extracts $\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Re}(i Q'_F(i))] = -\beta/2$, $\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Im}(i Q'_F(i))] = 0$, and $\mathbb{E}[|Q'_F(i)|^2] = \beta(\beta+1)/4$ with zero ambiguity!

def q (F : KClass) (z : ℂ) : ℂ :=
  I * (z.im : ℂ) * (deriv (Q F.F) z) / (Q F.F z)

-- Equivalently, via F'' / F'

def a (F : KClass) (z : ℂ) : ℝ := (q F z).re

lemma E_a_eq (P : Measure KClass) [IsFiniteMeasure P] (beta : ℝ)
    (h_P_eigen : ∀ z ∈ H, ∫ F, ‖Q F.F z‖^2 ∂P = (z.im : ℝ) ^ (-beta)) :
    ∫ F, a F I ∂P = -beta / 2 := by
{
  have hz_I : I ∈ H := by dsimp [H]; simp
  have h_ev : (fun y : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F ((0 : ℝ) + y • I)‖^2 ∂P) =ᶠ[𝓝 1] (fun y : ℝ => y ^ (-beta)) := by
  {
    filter_upwards [isOpen_Ioi.mem_nhds (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 1)] with y hy
    have hyH : ((0 : ℝ) : ℂ) + y • I ∈ H := by dsimp [H]; simp; exact hy
    have h_im : (((0 : ℝ) : ℂ) + y • I).im = y := by simp [Complex.real_smul]
    rw [h_P_eigen _ hyH, h_im]
  }
  have hd_rpow : HasDerivAt (fun y : ℝ => y ^ (-beta)) (-beta * (1 : ℝ) ^ (-beta - 1)) 1 :=
    Real.hasDerivAt_rpow_const (Or.inl (by norm_num))
  have h_rhs : deriv (fun y : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F ((0 : ℝ) + y • I)‖^2 ∂P) 1 = -beta := by
  {
    rw [h_ev.deriv_eq, hd_rpow.deriv]
    simp
  }
  have h_lhs : deriv (fun y : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F ((0 : ℝ) + y • I)‖^2 ∂P) 1 = 2 * ∫ F, a F I ∂P := by
  {
    have h_dy := deriv_integral_y P I hz_I
    simp only [Complex.I_re, Complex.I_im] at h_dy
    rw [h_dy, ← integral_const_mul]
    congr 1
    ext F
    have h_pt := dy_norm_Q_sq F I hz_I
    simp only [Complex.I_re, Complex.I_im, norm_Q_at_I_sq, div_one, mul_one] at h_pt
    exact h_pt
  }
  linarith
}

-- Source: brennan/Section3.lean#L2710-L2751
lemma E_q_sq_eq (P : Measure KClass) [IsFiniteMeasure P] (beta : ℝ)
    (h_P_eigen : ∀ z ∈ H, ∫ F, ‖Q F.F z‖^2 ∂P = (z.im : ℝ) ^ (-beta)) :
    ∫ F, ‖q F I‖^2 ∂P = beta * (beta + 1) / 4 := by
{
  have hz_I : I ∈ H := by dsimp [H]; simp
  have h_ev_y : (fun y : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F ((0 : ℝ) + y • I)‖^2 ∂P) =ᶠ[𝓝 1] (fun y : ℝ => y ^ (-beta)) := by
  {
    filter_upwards [isOpen_Ioi.mem_nhds (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 1)] with y hy
    have hyH : ((0 : ℝ) : ℂ) + y • I ∈ H := by dsimp [H]; simp; exact hy
    have h_im : (((0 : ℝ) : ℂ) + y • I).im = y := by simp [Complex.real_smul]
    rw [h_P_eigen _ hyH, h_im]
  }
  have h_eq_x : (fun x : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F ((x : ℂ) + (1 : ℝ) • I)‖^2 ∂P) = (fun _ : ℝ => (1 : ℝ) ^ (-beta)) := by
  {
    ext x
    have hxH : (x : ℂ) + (1 : ℝ) • I ∈ H := by dsimp [H]; simp
    have h_im : ((x : ℂ) + (1 : ℝ) • I).im = 1 := by simp
    rw [h_P_eigen _ hxH, h_im]
  }
  have h_lap := deriv2_integral_laplacian P I hz_I
  simp only [Complex.I_re, Complex.I_im] at h_lap
  have h_d2x : deriv (deriv (fun x : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F ((x : ℂ) + (1 : ℝ) • I)‖^2 ∂P)) 0 = 0 := by
  {
    rw [h_eq_x, deriv_const', deriv_const]
  }
  have h_d2y : deriv (deriv (fun y : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F ((0 : ℝ) + y • I)‖^2 ∂P)) 1 = beta * (beta + 1) := by
  {
    rw [(h_ev_y.deriv).deriv_eq, deriv2_rpow_at_one beta]
  }
  have h_rhs : ∫ F, (deriv (deriv (fun x : ℝ => ‖Q F.F ((x : ℂ) + (1 : ℝ) • I)‖^2)) 0 +
                     deriv (deriv (fun y : ℝ => ‖Q F.F ((0 : ℝ) + y • I)‖^2)) 1) ∂P =
               4 * ∫ F, ‖q F I‖^2 ∂P := by
  {
    rw [← integral_const_mul]
    congr 1
    ext F
    have h_pt := laplacian_norm_Q_sq F I hz_I
    simp only [Complex.I_re, Complex.I_im] at h_pt
    rw [h_pt, norm_q_at_I_sq F]
  }
  linarith
}
Proof of Lemma 3.2 (Click any step below for Level 2 10× Math & Level 3 Lean 4):
▸
Proof of Lemma 3.2 · Step 1 · 02-weighted-law.tex:244–280 · Eq (3.10) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Taking $\Phi = \mathbf{1}$ in (3.1) gives $\mathbb{E}|Q_F(x+iy)|^2 = y^{-\beta}$ (3.10). On a compact neighborhood of any interior point, $Q_F, Q'_F, Q''_F$ are uniformly bounded over $\mathcal{K}$, justifying differentiation under expectation. Holomorphicity and (3.7) give the pointwise identities $$\partial_y|Q_F|^2 = \frac{2a_F}{y}|Q_F|^2, \qquad \partial_x|Q_F|^2 = \frac{2b_F}{y}|Q_F|^2, \qquad \Delta|Q_F|^2 = 4|Q'_F|^2.$$ At $i$ we have $Q_F(i) = 1$, so the $y$ derivative, the $x$ derivative, and the Euclidean Laplacian of (3.10) give $2\mathbb{E}a_F(i) = -\beta$, $2\mathbb{E}b_F(i) = 0$, and $4\mathbb{E}|q_F(i)|^2 = \beta(\beta+1)$. Differentiating $Q_{T_z F}(w) = Q_F(z\cdot w)/Q_F(z)$ gives $q_{T_z F}(w) = q_F(z\cdot w)$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Complete Cauchy–Riemann & Parametric Differentiation Proof of (3.8) (~10× Depth)
  1. Pointwise First Partial Derivatives of $|Q_F(x+iy)|^2$: Since $Q_F$ is holomorphic on $\mathbb{H}$, $\partial_x Q_F = Q'_F$ and $\partial_y Q_F = i Q'_F$. Using $\partial(|Q_F|^2) = 2\operatorname{Re}(\overline{Q_F}\,\partial Q_F)$ and $Q'_F = \frac{q_F}{iy}Q_F = -\frac{i}{y}(a_F + ib_F)Q_F = \frac{b_F - i a_F}{y}Q_F$: $$\partial_x |Q_F|^2 = 2\operatorname{Re}\bigl(\overline{Q_F} Q'_F\bigr) = 2\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{b_F - i a_F}{y}\right)|Q_F|^2 = \frac{2b_F}{y}|Q_F|^2,$$ $$\partial_y |Q_F|^2 = 2\operatorname{Re}\bigl(\overline{Q_F} (i Q'_F)\bigr) = 2\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{a_F + i b_F}{y}\right)|Q_F|^2 = \frac{2a_F}{y}|Q_F|^2.$$
  2. Pointwise Euclidean Laplacian $\Delta|Q_F|^2 = 4|Q'_F|^2$: Differentiating a second time with $\partial_{xx}Q_F = Q''_F$ and $\partial_{yy}Q_F = -Q''_F$: $$\partial_{xx}|Q_F|^2 = 2|Q'_F|^2 + 2\operatorname{Re}(\overline{Q_F} Q''_F), \qquad \partial_{yy}|Q_F|^2 = 2|i Q'_F|^2 + 2\operatorname{Re}(\overline{Q_F}(-Q''_F)) = 2|Q'_F|^2 - 2\operatorname{Re}(\overline{Q_F} Q''_F).$$ Adding the two cancels $\pm 2\operatorname{Re}(\overline{Q_F}Q''_F)$, giving $\Delta|Q_F|^2 = 4|Q'_F|^2$.
  3. Uniform Cauchy Bounds on $\overline{B}(z, y/4)$: By Cauchy's integral inequality on the circle $\partial B(w, y/4) \subset \mathbb{H}$, $\|Q_F^{(n)}(w)\| \le \frac{n!}{(y/4)^n} \sup_{\zeta \in \overline{B}(z, y/2)} \|Q_F(\zeta)\|$, which is uniformly bounded across all $F \in \mathcal{K}$ by Lemma 2.2. Mathlib's dominated differentiation theorem (`hasDerivAt_integral_of_dominated_loc_of_deriv_le`) then allows passing $\partial_x$, $\partial_y$, and $\Delta = \partial_{xx}+\partial_{yy}$ inside $\int_{\mathcal{K}} \cdots dP(F)$.
  4. Evaluating at $z = i$ ($x=0, y=1$): Since $Q_F(i)=1$, at $z=i$ we have $|Q_F(i)|^2 = 1$ and $|Q'_F(i)|^2 = |q_F(i)|^2$. Thus:
    • $\left.\partial_y\right|_{y=1} \mathbb{E}|Q_F(i y)|^2 = \left.\partial_y(y^{-\beta})\right|_{y=1} \implies 2\mathbb{E}a_F(i) = -\beta \implies \mathbb{E}a_F(i) = -\beta/2$.
    • $\left.\partial_x\right|_{x=0} \mathbb{E}|Q_F(x + i)|^2 = \left.\partial_x(1)\right|_{x=0} = 0 \implies 2\mathbb{E}b_F(i) = 0 \implies \mathbb{E}b_F(i) = 0$.
    • $\left.\Delta\right|_{(0,1)} \mathbb{E}|Q_F(x+iy)|^2 = \left.(0 + \beta(\beta+1)y^{-\beta-2})\right|_{y=1} \implies 4\mathbb{E}|q_F(i)|^2 = \beta(\beta+1) \implies \mathbb{E}|q_F(i)|^2 = \frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{4}$.

-- Source: brennan/Section3.lean#L2603-L2614
theorem deriv2_integral_laplacian (P : Measure KClass) [IsFiniteMeasure P] (z : ℂ) (hz : z ∈ H) :
    deriv (deriv (fun x : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F (x + z.im • I)‖^2 ∂P)) z.re +
    deriv (deriv (fun y : ℝ => ∫ F, ‖Q F.F (z.re + y • I)‖^2 ∂P)) z.im =
    ∫ F, (deriv (deriv (fun x : ℝ => ‖Q F.F (x + z.im • I)‖^2)) z.re +
          deriv (deriv (fun y : ℝ => ‖Q F.F (z.re + y • I)‖^2)) z.im) ∂P := by
{
  have hx := deriv2_integral_along_line P hz norm_one (fun x => hasDerivAt_line_x x z.im)
    (fun _ h => line_x_mem_closedBall h)
  have hy := deriv2_integral_along_line P hz Complex.norm_I (hasDerivAt_line_y z.re)
    (fun _ h => line_y_mem_closedBall h)
  rw [hx.2, hy.2, ← integral_add hx.1 hy.1]
}

4. Escape estimates and compact superlevels

Throughout this section assume $\rho > 2$, let $\beta = \log_2 \rho > 1$, and let $P$ be the probability from Lemma 3.1. Set $$k = \frac{\beta+3}{4} > 1, \qquad V_\xi^F(x+iy) = \frac{y^k}{|F(x+iy)-\xi|} \quad (F \in \mathcal{K},\ \xi \in \mathbb{C}), \qquad (4.2)$$ with value $+\infty$ at the unique possible point where $F(z) = \xi$.

Proposition 4.1 (Compact positive superlevels)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section4_5.lean:1600–2073
▸
Proposition 4.1 Statement · 03-escape.tex:27–35 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
For $P$-almost every $F \in \mathcal{K}$, for planar area-almost every $\xi \in \mathbb{C}$, the set $$\{z \in \mathbb{H} : V_\xi^F(z) \ge \delta\}$$ is compact in $\mathbb{H}$ for every $\delta > 0$.
🔍 Level 2 — Why Compact Superlevels Are the Geometric Keystone (~10× Depth)

To count critical points of $V_\xi^F$ above level $h > 0$ using Poincaré–Hopf degree theory (Lemma 5.5), we must know that $\{z \in \mathbb{H} : V_\xi^F(z) \ge \delta\}$ is compact inside the open upper half-plane $\mathbb{H}$ (i.e., bounded in $\mathbb{C}$ and bounded away from the real axis $y = 0$). Proposition 4.1 proves this happens for $P$-almost every $F \in \mathcal{K}$ (specifically, every $F$ with $I(F) < \infty$, Lemma 4.2) and Lebesgue-almost every target $\xi \in \mathbb{C}$.

4.1 A weighted area integral

Lemma 4.2 (Weighted inverse area)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section4_5.lean:57–595
▸
Lemma 4.2 Statement & Proof · 03-escape.tex:44–106 · Eqs (4.3)–(4.5) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Define $S_0 = \{z \in \mathbb{H} : |m(z)| \ge \tfrac{1}{2}\}$ and $I(F) = \int_{S_0} y^{\beta+1}|F(x+iy)|^{-4}\,dA(x+iy)$ (4.3). The function $I : \mathcal{K} \to [0,\infty]$ is Borel and satisfies $\mathbb{E}I(F) \le 256\pi$. In particular, $I(F) < \infty$ almost surely.
Proof. For $z \ne i$, normalization gives $(T_z F)(z^\#) = -\frac{F(z)Q_F(z)}{y}$. Applying (4.1) with $\Phi(H) = |H(z^\#)|^{-4}$ gives $$\mathbb{E}\!\left[|F'(z)|^2 |F(z)|^{-4}\right] = y^{-\beta-4}\mathbb{E}|F(z^\#)|^{-4}. \qquad (4.4)$$ The involution $(x,y) \mapsto (u,Y) = (-x/y, 1/y)$ has Jacobian determinant $y^{-3}$ ($dx\,dy = Y^{-3}du\,dY$) and preserves $S_0$ since $|m(z^\#)|^2 = |m(z)|^2$. Since $|F| \ge 1/16$ on $S_0$ (Lemma 2.2), injective conformal change of variables gives $$\int_{S_0} |F'(z)|^2 |F(z)|^{-4}\,dA(z) = \int_{F(S_0)} |w|^{-4}\,dA(w) \le \int_{|w|\ge 1/16} |w|^{-4}\,dA(w) = 256\pi. \qquad (4.5)$$ Integrating (4.4) over $S_0$ using Tonelli's theorem and the involution yields $\mathbb{E}I(F) = \mathbb{E}\int_{S_0} |F'|^2|F|^{-4}\,dA \le 256\pi$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Step-by-Step Verification of the Involution $z^\#$ Trick & $256\pi$ (~10× Depth)
  1. Evaluating $T_z F$ at $z^\# = (-x+i)/y$: By definition, $z \cdot z^\# = x + y\left(\frac{-x+i}{y}\right) = i$. Since $F(i) = 0$ for every $F \in \mathcal{K}$, $$(T_z F)(z^\#) = \frac{F(z\cdot z^\#) - F(z)}{y F'(z)} = \frac{0 - F(z)}{y F'(z)} = -\frac{F(z) Q_F(z)}{y}.$$ Taking $|\cdot|^{-4}$ and multiplying by $|Q_F(z)|^2 = |F'(z)|^{-2}$ gives $$|Q_F(z)|^2 |(T_z F)(z^\#)|^{-4} = |F'(z)|^{-2} \cdot y^4 |F(z)|^{-4} |F'(z)|^4 = y^4 |F'(z)|^2 |F(z)|^{-4}.$$ Taking expectations via Lemma 3.1 ($\mathbb{E}[|Q_F(z)|^2 \Phi(T_z F)] = y^{-\beta}\mathbb{E}\Phi(F)$ with $\Phi(H) = |H(z^\#)|^{-4}$) and dividing by $y^4$ yields (4.4)!
  2. Why $|m(z^\#)|^2 = |m(z)|^2$: For $z^\# = -\frac{x}{y} + i\frac{1}{y}$, we have $$|m(z^\#)|^2 = \frac{(-x/y)^2 + (1/y - 1)^2}{(-x/y)^2 + (1/y + 1)^2} = \frac{x^2 + (1-y)^2}{x^2 + (1+y)^2} = |m(z)|^2.$$
  3. Jacobian of $(x,y) \mapsto (u,Y) = (-x/y, 1/y)$: The partial derivatives are $\partial_x u = -1/y$, $\partial_y u = x/y^2$, $\partial_x Y = 0$, $\partial_y Y = -1/y^2$, with determinant $(-1/y)(-1/y^2) = y^{-3} = Y^3$. Thus $y^{-\beta-4}\,dx\,dy = Y^{\beta+4} Y^{-3}\,du\,dY = Y^{\beta+1}\,du\,dY$, which transforms $\int_{S_0} y^{-\beta-4}\mathbb{E}|F(z^\#)|^{-4}\,dx\,dy$ into $\int_{S_0} Y^{\beta+1}\mathbb{E}|F(u+iY)|^{-4}\,du\,dY = \mathbb{E}I(F)$!
File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 236–285 & 501–595) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L236-L240, brennan/Section4_5.lean#L501-L529

4.2 Escape and horizontal preimage length

Lemma 4.3 (Escape and trace bounds)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section4_5.lean:600–1527
▸
Lemma 4.3 Statement & Step 1 · 03-escape.tex:110–159 · Eqs (4.6), (4.8) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Let $F \in \mathcal{K}$ satisfy $I(F) < \infty$. Then $\frac{y^k}{|F(x+iy)|} \to 0$ as $|x|+y \to \infty$ (4.6). For every compact $K \subset \mathbb{C}$ and $\epsilon > 0$, $F^{-1}(K)\cap\{\operatorname{Im}z \ge \epsilon\}$ is compact in $\mathbb{H}$, $H_{F,K} := \sup\{\operatorname{Im}z : F(z) \in K\} < \infty$, and there is $C_{F,K} < \infty$ such that $\int_{\mathbb{R}} \mathbf{1}_K(F(x+i\epsilon))\,dx \le C_{F,K}$ ($\epsilon > 0$) (4.7).
Proof (Part 1: Escape at Infinity). Write $R(z) = |x|+y$. If $z' = x'+iy' \in B(z, y/2)$, then $y/2 < y' < 3y/2$ and $R(z) \le 3R(z')$. On $B(z, y/2) \subset S_0$, $1/F$ is holomorphic, so submean for $|1/F|^4$ gives $$y^{\beta+3}|F(z)|^{-4} \le C_\beta \int_{S_0\cap\{R(z')\ge R(z)/3\}} (\operatorname{Im}z')^{\beta+1}|F(z')|^{-4}\,dA(z') \longrightarrow 0. \qquad (4.8)$$ Since $4k = \beta+3$, this proves (4.6) and boundedness of $H_{F,K}$.
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Subharmonic Mean-Value & Tail-Integral Proof of (4.8) (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $B(z, y/2) \subset S_0$ for Large $R(z)$: Since $\mathbb{H}\setminus S_0 = \{|m(z)| < 1/2\}$ is a bounded disk in $\mathbb{H}$, there is $R_0 > 0$ such that $R(z') \ge R_0 \implies z' \in S_0$. For any $z' \in B(z, y/2)$, $|x-x'| < y/2$ and $|y-y'| < y/2$, so $y' > y/2 \implies y < 2y'$ and $|x| < |x'| + y/2 < |x'| + y'$. Thus $R(z) = |x|+y \le |x'| + 3y' \le 3(|x'|+y') = 3R(z')$. Hence for $R(z) \ge 3R_0$, $B(z, y/2) \subset S_0 \cap \{R(z') \ge R(z)/3\}$.
  2. $L^2$ Submean Applied to $g = F^{-2}$: Since $F$ is non-vanishing on $S_0$, $g(w) = (F(w))^{-2}$ is holomorphic on $B(z, y/2)$, so $|F(z)|^{-4} = |g(z)|^2 \le \frac{1}{\pi(y/2)^2} \int_{B(z,y/2)} |F(z')|^{-4}\,dA(z')$.
  3. Matching Powers of $y$: Since $y < 2y'$ and $\frac{2}{3}y < y' < \frac{3}{2}y$, we have $y^{\beta+1} \le 2^{\beta+1}(y')^{\beta+1}$. Multiplying the submean inequality by $y^{\beta+3} = y^2 \cdot y^{\beta+1}$ cancels $y^{-2}$ in $\frac{4}{\pi y^2}$, giving $$\left(\frac{y^k}{|F(z)|}\right)^4 = y^{\beta+3}|F(z)|^{-4} \le \frac{2^{\beta+3}}{\pi} \int_{S_0 \cap \{R(z')\ge R(z)/3\}} (y')^{\beta+1}|F(z')|^{-4}\,dA(z').$$ Because $I(F) = \int_{S_0} (y')^{\beta+1}|F(z')|^{-4}\,dA(z') < \infty$, dominated convergence forces the integral over $\{R(z') \ge R/3\}$ to vanish as $R \to \infty$!

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L850-L889
theorem lemma_4_3_escape (beta : ℝ) (h_beta : beta > 1) (F : KClass) (hI : I_integrable beta F) :
  Filter.Tendsto (fun z : ℂ => V_target beta F 0 z)
    (Filter.comap (fun z : ℂ => abs z.re + z.im) Filter.atTop) (𝓝 0) := by
{
  have ht : HasQuarticTail F.F (4 * k_val beta) := by
  {
    convert KClass_quartic_tail F h_beta hI using 1
    unfold k_val; ring
  }
  rw [ENNReal.tendsto_nhds_zero]
  intro ε hε
  obtain ⟨δ, hδ, hδ_le⟩ : ∃ δ : ℝ, 0 < δ ∧ ENNReal.ofReal δ ≤ ε := by
  {
    rcases eq_or_ne ε ⊤ with rfl | htop
    · exact ⟨1, zero_lt_one, le_top⟩
    · exact ⟨ε.toReal, ENNReal.toReal_pos hε.ne' htop, by simp [htop]⟩
  }
  obtain ⟨R, hR⟩ := quartic_tail_iff_power ht δ hδ
  rw [Filter.Eventually, Filter.mem_comap]
  refine ⟨Ici (2 * max R 4), Filter.mem_atTop _, fun z hz => ?_⟩
  by_cases hzH : z ∈ H
  · have hy : 0 < z.im := hzH
    have hnorm : max R 4 ≤ ‖z‖ := by
    {
      have hre : |z.re| ≤ ‖z‖ := Complex.abs_re_le_norm z
      have him : z.im ≤ ‖z‖ := (le_abs_self _).trans (Complex.abs_im_le_norm z)
      change 2 * max R 4 ≤ |z.re| + z.im at hz
      linarith
    }
    have hFz : F.F z ≠ 0 := F_ne_zero_S0 F (S0_of_norm hzH (by linarith [le_max_right R 4]))
    have hpow := hR z hy ((le_max_left R 4).trans hnorm)
    have hV : V_target beta F 0 z = ENNReal.ofReal (z.im ^ k_val beta * ‖F.F z‖⁻¹) := by
    {
      simp only [V_target, hzH, ite_true, sub_zero]
      rw [← ENNReal.ofReal_div_of_pos (norm_pos_iff.mpr hFz), div_eq_mul_inv]
    }
    rw [Set.mem_setOf_eq, hV]
    exact (ENNReal.ofReal_le_ofReal hpow.le).trans hδ_le
  · simp [V_target, hzH]
}

Proof of Lemma 4.3 · Step 2 · 03-escape.tex:160–194 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math & Bypass 2)
Proof (Part 2: Horizontal Preimage Trace Bound). Choose a nonnegative $\psi \in C_c^\infty(\mathbb{C})$ with $\psi \ge 1$ on $K$, and fix $\epsilon > 0$. Set $g = \psi \circ F$ and $h(y) = \int_{\mathbb{R}} g(x,y)\,dx$. Since $\partial_{xx} g$ integrates to zero, $$\int_{y>\epsilon} (y-\epsilon)\Delta g(x,y)\,dx\,dy = \int_\epsilon^\infty (y-\epsilon)h''(y)\,dy = \bigl[(y-\epsilon)h'(y)\bigr]_\epsilon^\infty - \bigl[h(y)\bigr]_\epsilon^\infty = h(\epsilon).$$ Since $\Delta(\psi\circ F) = |F'|^2 (\Delta\psi)\circ F$, conformal change of variables gives $$\int_{\mathbb{R}} \mathbf{1}_K(F(x+i\epsilon))\,dx \le h(\epsilon) = \int_{\Omega\cap\{Y>\epsilon\}} (Y(w)-\epsilon)\Delta\psi(w)\,dA(w) \le H_{F,\operatorname{supp}\psi}\|\Delta\psi\|_{L^1(\mathbb{C})}. \quad \square$$
🔍 Level 2 — Formal Bypass 2: Rectangular Divergence Theorem on Trace Flux (~10× Depth)
Formal Bypass 2 in brennan/Section4_5.lean: Instead of differentiating the improper horizontal integral $h(y) = \int_{-\infty}^\infty g(x,y)\,dx$ twice under the integral sign, our Lean 4 formalization introduces the explicit 2D vector field (trace flux): $$\mathbf{V}(x,y) = \Bigl((y-\epsilon)\partial_x g(x,y),\; (y-\epsilon)\partial_y g(x,y) - g(x,y)\Bigr).$$ By the product rule, its divergence is identically: $$\operatorname{div}\mathbf{V} = (y-\epsilon)\partial_{xx}g + \bigl[\partial_y g + (y-\epsilon)\partial_{yy}g - \partial_y g\bigr] = (y-\epsilon)\Delta g(x,y)!$$ On a large rectangle $[-L, L] \times [\epsilon, M]$ containing the compact support of $g$ in $\{y \ge \epsilon\}$, $\mathbf{V}$ vanishes on the left ($x=-L$), right ($x=L$), and top ($y=M$) edges, while on the bottom edge $y = \epsilon$ the factor $(y-\epsilon)$ vanishes, leaving $\mathbf{V}(x,\epsilon) = (0, -g(x,\epsilon))$. Applying Mathlib's rectangular divergence theorem (`integral_divergence_prod_Icc_of_hasFDerivAt_of_le`) immediately gives $$\int_{-L}^L g(x,\epsilon)\,dx = \int_\epsilon^M \int_{-L}^L (y-\epsilon)\Delta g(x,y)\,dx\,dy!$$ Furthermore, instead of a non-constructive $C_c^\infty$ bump, we use the explicit algebraic quartic bump $\psi(w) = \bigl(\max(0, 1 - |w|^2/(4R^2))\bigr)^4$, which is $C^2$ everywhere on $\mathbb{C}$ with $|\Delta\psi| \le \frac{32}{R^2}$!

def traceFluxX (g : ℝ × ℝ → ℝ) (ε : ℝ) (p : ℝ × ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (p.2 - ε) * fderiv ℝ g p (1, 0)

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L962-L963
def traceFluxY (g : ℝ × ℝ → ℝ) (ε : ℝ) (p : ℝ × ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (p.2 - ε) * fderiv ℝ g p (0, 1) - g p

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L1036-L1068
lemma rectangle_trace_identity {g : ℝ × ℝ → ℝ} {R ε Y : ℝ}
    (hR : 0 ≤ R) (hεY : ε ≤ Y)
    (hg : ∀ p ∈ Icc (-R, ε) (R, Y), ContDiffAt ℝ 2 g p)
    (htop : ∀ x ∈ Icc (-R) R, g (x, Y) = 0 ∧ fderiv ℝ g (x, Y) (0, 1) = 0)
    (hside : ∀ y ∈ Icc ε Y,
      fderiv ℝ g (-R, y) (1, 0) = 0 ∧ fderiv ℝ g (R, y) (1, 0) = 0) :
    (∫ p in Icc (-R, ε) (R, Y), (p.2 - ε) * pairLaplacian g p) =
      ∫ x in -R..R, g (x, ε) := by
{
  have hb := rectangle_divergence_integral hR hεY hg
  have ht : (∫ x in -R..R, traceFluxY g ε (x, Y)) = 0 := by
  {
    rw [← intervalIntegral.integral_zero]
    refine intervalIntegral.integral_congr (fun x hx => ?_)
    have hx' : x ∈ Icc (-R) R := by simpa only [uIcc_of_le (by linarith : -R ≤ R)] using hx
    simp [traceFluxY, (htop x hx').1, (htop x hx').2]
  }
  have hl : (∫ y in ε..Y, traceFluxX g ε (-R, y)) = 0 := by
  {
    rw [← intervalIntegral.integral_zero]
    refine intervalIntegral.integral_congr (fun y hy => ?_)
    simp [traceFluxX, (hside y (by simpa only [uIcc_of_le hεY] using hy)).1]
  }
  have hr : (∫ y in ε..Y, traceFluxX g ε (R, y)) = 0 := by
  {
    rw [← intervalIntegral.integral_zero]
    refine intervalIntegral.integral_congr (fun y hy => ?_)
    simp [traceFluxX, (hside y (by simpa only [uIcc_of_le hεY] using hy)).2]
  }
  rw [ht, hl, hr] at hb
  simpa only [traceFluxY, sub_self, zero_mul, zero_sub,
    intervalIntegral.integral_neg, sub_neg_eq_add, zero_add, add_zero, sub_zero] using hb
}

4.3 Targets near the real axis & Measurability

Proof of Proposition 4.1 & Lemma 4.4 (Measurability of compact superlevels)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section4_5.lean:1600–2198

Proof of Proposition 4.1 · 03-escape.tex:204–297 · Eqs (4.9)–(4.13) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proof of Proposition 4.1. Fix $F$ with $I(F) < \infty$ and put $\Omega = F(\mathbb{H})$. For omitted targets $\xi \notin \Omega$ with $|\xi| \le R$ and dyadic $h \le 1$, call $I_j = [jh, (j+1)h)$ a witness cell if there is $x \in I_j$, $h \le y \le 2h$ with $V_\xi^F(x+iy) \ge \delta$, so $|F(z)-\xi| \le (2h)^k/\delta$. Since $B(z,y) \subset \mathbb{H}$ omits $\xi$, Lemma 2.1 gives $y|F'(z)| \le 4|F(z)-\xi| \le 4(2h)^k/\delta$ (4.10), and boundedness of $T_z F$ on $[-1,1]+i[1/2,1]$ gives $|F(x'+ih)-\xi| \le (1+4M)(2h)^k/\delta =: C_\delta h^k$ for all $x' \in I_j$ (4.11). By (4.7), there are at most $C_{F,R,\delta}/h$ witness cells, so the union $E_h$ of target disks of radius $C_\delta h^k$ around cell midpoint images has area $$|E_h| \le C_{F,R,\delta}\, h^{2k-1}. \qquad (4.12)$$ Since $2k - 1 > 1$, $\sum_{n=1}^\infty |E_{2^{-n}}| < \infty$, so $\limsup E_{2^{-n}}$ has area zero. For interior targets $\xi \in \Omega$, $F^{-1}(\overline{B}(\xi,a))$ has positive minimum height. Combining with escape at infinity (4.9) shows $\{V_\xi^F \ge \delta\}$ is compact in $\mathbb{H}$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Derivation of Witness Cell Distortion (4.11) & Borel–Cantelli (4.12) (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $y|F'(z)| \le 4|F(z)-\xi|$ for Omitted Targets $\xi \notin F(\mathbb{H})$: The open disk $B(z, y)$ lies inside $\mathbb{H}$. By Lemma 2.1's local Koebe quarter theorem (2.2), $B\bigl(F(z), y|F'(z)|/4\bigr) \subset F(B(z,y)) \subset F(\mathbb{H})$. Since $\xi \notin F(\mathbb{H})$, $\xi$ cannot lie in $B\bigl(F(z), y|F'(z)|/4\bigr)$, so $|F(z)-\xi| \ge \frac{1}{4}y|F'(z)|$.
  2. Why the Entire Horizontal Segment $I_j + ih$ Maps Near $\xi$: For any $x' \in I_j = [jh, (j+1)h)$ and $x \in I_j$, $|x'-x| \le h \le y$, and $1/2 \le h/y \le 1$. Thus the relative point $w = \frac{x'-x}{y} + i\frac{h}{y}$ belongs to the fixed compact rectangle $E_0 = [-1,1]\times[1/2,1] \subset \mathbb{H}$. By Lemma 2.2, $M = \sup_{G\in\mathcal{K},\, w\in E_0} |G(w)| < \infty$. Since $F(x'+ih) - F(z) = y F'(z) (T_z F)(w)$, the triangle inequality gives $$|F(x'+ih) - \xi| \le |F(x'+ih) - F(z)| + |F(z) - \xi| \le y|F'(z)| M + |F(z)-\xi| \le (4M + 1)\frac{(2h)^k}{\delta} = C_\delta h^k.$$
  3. Counting Witness Cells via Lemma 4.3: Since $|\xi| \le R$, every $x' \in I_j$ has $|F(x'+ih)| \le R + C_\delta h^k \le R + C_\delta$, so $F(x'+ih)$ belongs to the fixed compact disk $K_{R,\delta} = \overline{B}(0, R+C_\delta)$. Thus each witness cell $I_j$ contributes $\int_{I_j} \mathbf{1}_{K_{R,\delta}}(F(x'+ih))\,dx' = |I_j| = h$. Since the cells $I_j$ are disjoint, Lemma 4.3(4.7) gives $$(\#\text{witness cells}) \cdot h \le \int_{\mathbb{R}} \mathbf{1}_{K_{R,\delta}}(F(x'+ih))\,dx' \le C_{F,K_{R,\delta}} \implies \#\text{witness cells} \le \frac{C_{F,K_{R,\delta}}}{h}.$$
  4. Summability over $h = 2^{-n}$: Each witness cell's midpoint $m_j + ih$ determines a disk $\overline{B}(F(m_j+ih), C_\delta h^k)$ of area $\pi C_\delta^2 h^{2k}$ that covers all omitted targets $\xi$ witnessed in $I_j$. Thus $|E_h| \le \frac{C_{F,K_{R,\delta}}}{h} \cdot \pi C_\delta^2 h^{2k} = C'_{F,R,\delta} h^{2k-1}$. Since $\beta > 1 \implies k = \frac{\beta+3}{4} > 1 \implies 2k-1 = \frac{\beta+1}{2} > 1 > 0$, $\sum_{n=1}^\infty (2^{-n})^{2k-1} < \infty$!

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L1583-L1620
lemma KClass_omitted_cell_bound {M : ℝ} (hM : 0 ≤ M)
    (hb : ∀ (F : KClass) (w : ℂ), |w.re| ≤ 1 → (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ w.im → w.im ≤ 1 → ‖F.F w‖ ≤ M)
    (F : KClass) {h x y x' : ℝ} {ξ : ℂ} (hh : 0 < h)
    (hy : h ≤ y) (hyy : y ≤ 2 * h) (hxx : |x' - x| ≤ h)
    (hξ : ξ ∉ F.F '' H) :
    ‖F.F (x' + h * I) - ξ‖ ≤ (1 + 4 * M) * ‖F.F (x + y * I) - ξ‖ := by
{
  let z : ℂ := x + y * I
  let w : ℂ := ((x' - x) / y : ℝ) + (h / y : ℝ) * I
  have hy0 : 0 < y := hh.trans_le hy
  have hz : z ∈ H := by simpa [z, H] using hy0
  have hwy : w.im = h / y := by simp [w]
  have hwx : w.re = (x' - x) / y := by simp [w]
  have hwr : |w.re| ≤ 1 := by rw [hwx, abs_div, abs_of_pos hy0]; exact (div_le_one hy0).mpr (hxx.trans hy)
  have hwl : (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ w.im := by rw [hwy, le_div_iff₀ hy0]; linarith
  have hwu : w.im ≤ 1 := by rw [hwy]; exact (div_le_one hy0).mpr hy
  have hwa : aff z w = x' + h * I := by
  {
    apply Complex.ext
    · simp [aff, z, w, Complex.mul_re, Complex.mul_im]
      field_simp
      ring
    · simp [aff, z, w, Complex.mul_re, Complex.mul_im]
      field_simp
  }
  have hd : 0 < ‖deriv F.F z‖ := norm_pos_iff.mpr (deriv_ne_zero_of_univalentOn isOpen_H F.univalent hz)
  have hm := hb ⟨T z F.F, (T_KClass F z hz).1, (T_KClass F z hz).2.1, (T_KClass F z hz).2.2⟩ w hwr hwl hwu
  have hzim : z.im = y := by simp [z]
  simp only [T, hwa, norm_div, norm_mul, Complex.norm_real, hzim, Real.norm_eq_abs, abs_of_pos hy0] at hm
  have hm' := (div_le_iff₀ (mul_pos hy0 hd)).mp hm
  have hq := omitted_quarter_bound F.univalent hz hξ
  rw [hzim] at hq
  have hsum := norm_add_le (F.F (x' + h * I) - F.F z) (F.F z - ξ)
  simp only [sub_add_sub_cancel] at hsum
  have hhq := mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hq hM
  dsimp only [z] at *
  nlinarith
}

Lemma 4.4 Statement & Proof · 03-escape.tex:299–324 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Lemma 4.4 (Measurability of compact superlevels). The set of pairs $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{K}\times\mathbb{C}$ for which every closed positive superlevel of $V_\xi^F$ is compact in $\mathbb{H}$ is Borel.
Proof. Use the compact exhaustion $C_m = \{x+iy : |x| \le m,\ 1/m \le y \le m\}$, $m \ge 1$. Compactness for all $\delta > 0$ is equivalent to: for every $n \ge 1$ there is $m \ge 1$ such that $\{V_\xi^F > 1/n\} \subset C_m$, which is equivalent to $$|F(q)-\xi| \ge n(\operatorname{Im}q)^k \quad \text{for every } q \in (\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q})\cap(\mathbb{H}\setminus C_m).$$ Each inequality is closed in $(F,\xi)$ by continuity of evaluation, so the countable intersection-union is Borel. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Countable Rational Grid Reduction for Borel Measurability (~10× Depth)

For each fixed rational point $q \in (\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q})\cap(\mathbb{H}\setminus C_m)$, the evaluation map $(F,\xi) \mapsto |F(q)-\xi| - n(\operatorname{Im}q)^k$ is continuous on $\mathcal{K}\times\mathbb{C}$, so $A_{n,m,q} = \{(F,\xi) : |F(q)-\xi| \ge n(\operatorname{Im}q)^k\}$ is closed. Because $\mathbb{H}\setminus C_m$ is open in $\mathbb{C}$ and $\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q}$ is dense in every open subset of $\mathbb{C}$, a continuous function $z \mapsto |F(z)-\xi| - n(\operatorname{Im}z)^k$ is $\ge 0$ on $\mathbb{H}\setminus C_m$ if and only if it is $\ge 0$ on $(\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q})\cap(\mathbb{H}\setminus C_m)$. Thus the set of pairs with compact superlevels equals the $\Pi^0_3$ ($F_{\sigma\delta}$) Borel set $$\bigcap_{n=1}^\infty \bigcup_{m=1}^\infty \bigcap_{q \in (\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q})\cap(\mathbb{H}\setminus C_m)} A_{n,m,q}.$$

File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2185–2198) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2185-L2198
theorem lemma_4_4_measurability (beta : ℝ) :
    MeasurableSet {p : KClass × ℂ |
      ∀ delta > 0, IsCompact {z ∈ H | V_target beta p.1 p.2 z ≥ delta}} := by
{
  rw [compact_superlevels_eq_countable_iInter_iUnion beta]
  refine MeasurableSet.iInter (fun m => MeasurableSet.iUnion (fun n => ?_))
  exact isClosed_superlevel_box_condition beta m n |>.measurableSet

5. Critical points and an equivariant pairing

5.1 The critical map and its real Jacobian

▸
Section 5.1 Intro · 04-critical-pairing.tex:1–25 · Eqs (5.1)–(5.2) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Fix $\rho > 2$, $\beta = \log_2 \rho > 1$, and $k = (\beta+3)/4 > 1$. For $F \in \mathcal{K}$ and $z = x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$, define $$G_F(z) = F(z) - \frac{iy}{k}F'(z). \qquad (5.1)$$ Writing $DG_F(z)$ for the real derivative of $G_F$ with respect to $(x,y)$ and identifying $\mathbb{C}$ with $\mathbb{R}^2$, define the normalized signed Jacobian $$J_F(z) = \frac{\det DG_F(z)}{|F'(z)|^2}. \qquad (5.2)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Geometric Meaning of the Critical Map $G_F(z)$ and Normalized Jacobian $J_F(z)$ (~10× Depth)

Why does $G_F(z) = F(z) - \frac{iy}{k}F'(z)$ govern the critical points of $V^F_\xi(z) = y^k/|F(z)-\xi|$? Take the logarithmic gradient of $V^F_\xi(z)$: since $\ell(z) = \log V^F_\xi(z) = k\log y - \log|F(z)-\xi|$, a critical point $z = x+iy$ of $V^F_\xi$ satisfies $$0 = (\partial_x - i\partial_y)\ell(z) = \frac{k}{iy} - \frac{F'(z)}{F(z)-\xi} \iff F(z) - \xi = \frac{iy}{k}F'(z) \iff G_F(z) = \xi.$$ Thus, $z \in \mathbb{H}\setminus F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$ is a critical point of $V^F_\xi$ if and only if $G_F(z) = \xi$. Notice that $G_F(x+iy)$ depends explicitly on $y = \operatorname{Im}z$, so $G_F : \mathbb{H} \to \mathbb{C} \cong \mathbb{R}^2$ is a smooth real map that is not holomorphic, and its normalized real $2\times 2$ Jacobian determinant $J_F(z) = \det DG_F(z)/|F'(z)|^2$ (defined everywhere since $F'(z) \ne 0$ for univalent $F \in \mathcal{K}$) takes both positive and negative values.

File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2218–2240) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2220-L2223
def G (beta : ℝ) (F : KClass) (z : ℂ) : ℂ :=
  F.F z - I / (k_val beta : ℂ) * (z.im : ℂ) * deriv F.F z

-- The normalized signed Jacobian: 2x2 real determinant of dG_F divided by |F'(z)|^2

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2224-L2226
def J (beta : ℝ) (F : KClass) (z : ℂ) : ℝ :=
  (((fderiv ℝ (G beta F) z 1).re * (fderiv ℝ (G beta F) z I).im -
    (fderiv ℝ (G beta F) z I).re * (fderiv ℝ (G beta F) z 1).im) / ‖deriv F.F z‖^2)
Lemma 5.1 (Jacobian formula and mean at $i$)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section4_5.lean:2241–2341
▸
Lemma 5.1 Statement · 04-critical-pairing.tex:27–40 · Eqs (5.3)–(5.4) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Recall $q_F = -iy F''/F' = a_F + ib_F$ from (3.7). For every $F \in \mathcal{K}$ and $z \in \mathbb{H}$, $$k^2 J_F(z) = k(k-1) + (2k-1)a_F(z) + |q_F(z)|^2. \qquad (5.3)$$ Under the probability measure $P$ of Section 3, $$\mathbb{E}J_F(i) = \frac{k-1}{k} = \frac{\beta-1}{\beta+3} > 0. \qquad (5.4)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Why $k = (\beta+3)/4$ Produces Exact Moment Cancellation (~10× Depth)

Look at the right-hand side of (5.3) at $z=i$: it has the deterministic constant $k(k-1)$ plus the linear-plus-quadratic combination $(2k-1)a_F(i) + |q_F(i)|^2$. By Lemma 3.2(3.8), $\mathbb{E}a_F(i) = -\frac{\beta}{2}$ and $\mathbb{E}|q_F(i)|^2 = \frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{4}$. Therefore, $$\mathbb{E}\bigl[(2k-1)a_F(i) + |q_F(i)|^2\bigr] = (2k-1)\left(-\frac{\beta}{2}\right) + \frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{4} = \frac{\beta}{4}\bigl(\beta + 3 - 4k\bigr).$$ Setting $k = \frac{\beta+3}{4}$ makes $2k-1 = \frac{\beta+1}{2}$ and $\beta + 3 - 4k = 0$ identically. Thus $k = \frac{\beta+3}{4}$ is the unique exponent for which the expected first and second moments of $q_F(i)$ cancel completely, leaving $$\mathbb{E}J_F(i) = \frac{k(k-1)}{k^2} = \frac{k-1}{k} = \frac{(\beta-1)/4}{(\beta+3)/4} = \frac{\beta-1}{\beta+3} > 0$$ because $\rho > 2 \implies \beta = \log_2\rho > 1$.

File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2284–2341) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2285-L2299
lemma J_identity (beta : ℝ) (hk : k_val beta ≠ 0) (F : KClass) {z : ℂ} (hz : z ∈ H) :
    (k_val beta)^2 * J beta F z =
      (k_val beta) * ((k_val beta) - 1) + (2 * (k_val beta) - 1) * a F z + ‖q F z‖^2 := by
{
  have hd : ‖deriv F.F z‖ ≠ 0 :=
    norm_ne_zero_iff.mpr (deriv_ne_zero_of_univalentOn isOpen_H F.univalent hz)
  obtain ⟨hc₁, hc₂⟩ := G_columns beta hk F hz
  unfold J a
  rw [hc₁, hc₂, critical_columns_det, norm_div, Complex.norm_real,
    Real.norm_eq_abs, div_pow, sq_abs]
  field_simp
}

-- Crucially, the expectation of the Jacobian is STRICTLY POSITIVE at the root i
-- We prove this algebraically by integrating the Jacobian identity and substituting the moments

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2300-L2317
lemma E_J_positive (beta : ℝ) (h_beta : beta > 1) (P : Measure KClass)
    (h_J_integral : ∫ F, (k_val beta)^2 * J beta F I ∂P =
      (k_val beta) * ((k_val beta) - 1) +
      (2 * (k_val beta) - 1) * (∫ F, a F I ∂P) + (∫ F, ‖q F I‖^2 ∂P))
    (h_E_a : ∫ F, a F I ∂P = -beta / 2)
    (h_E_q_sq : ∫ F, ‖q F I‖^2 ∂P = beta * (beta + 1) / 4) :
    ∫ F, (k_val beta)^2 * J beta F I ∂P > 0 := by
{
  have hk : k_val beta = (beta + 3) / 4 := rfl
  rw [h_J_integral, h_E_a, h_E_q_sq, hk]
  have h1 : (beta + 3) / 4 > 0 := by linarith
  have h2 : (beta + 3) / 4 - 1 > 0 := by linarith
  have h_eq : (beta + 3) / 4 * ((beta + 3) / 4 - 1) +
      (2 * ((beta + 3) / 4) - 1) * (-beta / 2) + beta * (beta + 1) / 4 =
      (beta + 3) / 4 * ((beta + 3) / 4 - 1) := by ring
  rw [h_eq]
  positivity
}
▸
Proof of Lemma 5.1 · 04-critical-pairing.tex:42–60 · Eq (5.5) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proof. Because $F$ is holomorphic, $\partial_x F = F'$ and $\partial_y F = iF'$. Differentiating (5.1) gives the columns of $DG_F(z)$ as complex numbers: $$\partial_x G_F = F'\left(1 + \frac{q_F}{k}\right), \qquad \partial_y G_F = iF'\left(\frac{k-1}{k} + \frac{q_F}{k}\right). \qquad (5.5)$$ For complex columns $u, v$, the real determinant is $\operatorname{Im}(\bar u v)$. Thus $$J_F(z) = \operatorname{Im}\left[i\left(1 + \frac{\overline{q_F}}{k}\right)\left(\frac{k-1}{k} + \frac{q_F}{k}\right)\right] = \frac{k(k-1) + (2k-1)a_F + |q_F|^2}{k^2},$$ which is (5.3). Evaluating at $z = i$, taking expectations, and inserting (3.8) together with $2k-1 = (\beta+1)/2$ gives $$k^2 \mathbb{E}J_F(i) = k(k-1) - \frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{4} + \frac{\beta(\beta+1)}{4} = k(k-1),$$ which is (5.4). $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Full Real $2\times 2$ Determinant Computation $\operatorname{Im}(\overline{\partial_x G_F}\,\partial_y G_F)$ (~10× Depth)
  1. Partial Derivatives of $G_F(x+iy) = F(x+iy) - \frac{iy}{k}F'(x+iy)$:
    • $\partial_x G_F = F'(z) - \frac{iy}{k}F''(z) = F'(z)\left(1 - \frac{iy F''(z)}{k F'(z)}\right) = F'(z)\left(1 + \frac{q_F(z)}{k}\right)$.
    • $\partial_y G_F = iF'(z) - \frac{i}{k}F'(z) - \frac{iy}{k}(iF''(z)) = iF'(z)\left(\frac{k-1}{k} + \frac{q_F(z)}{k}\right)$.
  2. Complex Determinant Formula $\det_{\mathbb{R}}(u, v) = \operatorname{Im}(\bar u v)$: Identifying $u = u_1 + i u_2 \leftrightarrow \binom{u_1}{u_2}$ and $v = v_1 + i v_2 \leftrightarrow \binom{v_1}{v_2}$, $\det\begin{pmatrix} u_1 & v_1 \\ u_2 & v_2 \end{pmatrix} = u_1 v_2 - u_2 v_1 = \operatorname{Im}(\bar u v)$.
  3. Expanding $\operatorname{Im}(\overline{\partial_x G_F}\,\partial_y G_F)/|F'(z)|^2$: $$\frac{\overline{\partial_x G_F}\,\partial_y G_F}{|F'(z)|^2} = \frac{i}{k^2}\bigl(k + \overline{q_F}\bigr)\bigl(k - 1 + q_F\bigr) = \frac{i}{k^2}\Bigl(k(k-1) + k q_F + (k-1)\overline{q_F} + |q_F|^2\Bigr).$$ Taking the imaginary part (which extracts the real part of the bracketed factor) and using $\operatorname{Re}\bigl(k q_F + (k-1)\overline{q_F}\bigr) = (2k-1)a_F$ yields (5.3).
File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2241–2282) & brennan/Section6_Transport.lean (line 1058) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2251-L2264
lemma G_columns (beta : ℝ) (hk : k_val beta ≠ 0) (F : KClass) {z : ℂ} (hz : z ∈ H) :
    fderiv ℝ (G beta F) z 1 = deriv F.F z / (k_val beta : ℂ) * ((k_val beta : ℂ) + q F z) ∧
    fderiv ℝ (G beta F) z I =
      I * (deriv F.F z / (k_val beta : ℂ)) * ((k_val beta : ℂ) - 1 + q F z) := by
{
  have han : AnalyticAt ℂ F.F z := F.univalent.1.analyticOnNhd isOpen_H z hz
  have hd : deriv F.F z ≠ 0 := deriv_ne_zero_of_univalentOn isOpen_H F.univalent hz
  have hq : q F z = -I * (z.im : ℂ) * (deriv (deriv F.F) z) / (deriv F.F z) :=
    q_eq_F_deriv2 F z hd han.deriv.differentiableAt
  have hk' : (k_val beta : ℂ) ≠ 0 := Complex.ofReal_ne_zero.mpr hk
  constructor <;> rw [fderiv_G_apply beta F hz, hq] <;>
    simp only [Complex.one_im, Complex.I_im, Complex.ofReal_zero, Complex.ofReal_one,
      zero_mul, one_mul, mul_one, zero_add] <;> field_simp <;> ring
}

-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2266-L2276
lemma critical_columns_det (u q_val : ℂ) (k : ℝ) :
    (u * ((k : ℂ) + q_val)).re * (I * u * ((k : ℂ) - 1 + q_val)).im -
      (I * u * ((k : ℂ) - 1 + q_val)).re * (u * ((k : ℂ) + q_val)).im =
    ‖u‖^2 * (k * (k - 1) + (2 * k - 1) * q_val.re + ‖q_val‖^2) := by
{
  simp only [Complex.mul_re, Complex.mul_im, Complex.add_re, Complex.add_im,
    Complex.sub_re, Complex.sub_im, Complex.ofReal_re, Complex.ofReal_im,
    Complex.I_re, Complex.I_im, Complex.one_re, Complex.one_im,
    Complex.sq_norm, Complex.normSq_apply]
  ring
}

5.2 Critical points of the potential

Lemma 5.2 (Critical points and Hessian of $\log V^F_\xi$)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section4_5.lean:2345–2460
▸
Lemma 5.2 Statement · 04-critical-pairing.tex:66–94 · Eqs (5.6)–(5.8) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Fix $F \in \mathcal{K}$ and $\xi \in \mathbb{C}$, and write $\ell(z) = \log V^F_\xi(z) = k\log y - \log|F(z)-\xi|$ on $\mathbb{H}\setminus F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$.
  1. A point $z \in \mathbb{H}\setminus F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$ is a critical point of $V^F_\xi$ if and only if $G_F(z) = \xi$. At such a point, $$V^F_\xi(z) = k\,y^{k-1}|Q_F(z)|. \qquad (5.6)$$
  2. On $\mathbb{H}\setminus F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$, $\Delta\ell(z) = -k/y^2 < 0$. At a critical point $z$ of $V^F_\xi$, $$\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z) = -\frac{k}{y^2}\begin{pmatrix} k + a_F(z) & -b_F(z) \\ -b_F(z) & 1 - k - a_F(z) \end{pmatrix}, \qquad (5.7)$$ and therefore $$\det\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z) = -\frac{k^4}{y^4}J_F(z). \qquad (5.8)$$
In particular, a nondegenerate critical point $z$ has $J_F(z) > 0$ if and only if it is a saddle point of $V^F_\xi$, and $J_F(z) < 0$ if and only if it is a strict local maximum of $V^F_\xi$; $V^F_\xi$ has no local minima on $\mathbb{H}\setminus F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$.
🔍 Level 2 — Sign Reversal Between $\det\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z)$ and $J_F(z)$ (~10× Depth)

Look closely at (5.7) and (5.8): the trace of the Hessian matrix is $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z) = -\frac{k}{y^2}\bigl((k+a_F) + (1-k-a_F)\bigr) = -\frac{k}{y^2} < 0$, and $\det\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z)$ and $J_F(z)$ have opposite signs:

  • At a saddle point $z$ of $\ell = \log V^F_\xi$, the two real eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \lambda_2$ of $\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z)$ have opposite signs ($\lambda_1 < 0 < \lambda_2$), so $\det\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z) = \lambda_1\lambda_2 < 0$. By (5.8), this means $J_F(z) > 0$ at every nondegenerate saddle.
  • At a nondegenerate critical point with $J_F(z) < 0$, (5.8) gives $\det\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z) = \lambda_1\lambda_2 > 0$. Since $\lambda_1 + \lambda_2 = \Delta\ell(z) = -k/y^2 < 0$, both eigenvalues $\lambda_1, \lambda_2$ are strictly negative, so every nondegenerate critical point with $J_F(z) < 0$ is a strict local maximum.
File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2800–2815) & brennan/Section5_LevelIndex.lean (line 1203) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2676-L2677
def criticalHeight (beta : ℝ) (F : KClass) (z : ℂ) : ℝ :=
  k_val beta * z.im ^ (k_val beta - 1) * ‖Q F.F z‖

-- Source: brennan/Section5_LevelIndex.lean#L1240-L1261
lemma not_isLocalMin_logPotential {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : UnivalentOn f H)
    {k : ℝ} (hk : 0 < k) {z ξ : ℂ} (hz : z ∈ H) (hp : f z ≠ ξ) :
    ¬ IsLocalMin (logPotential f k ξ) z := by
{
  intro hmin
  have hd := (hf.1 z hz).differentiableAt (isOpen_H.mem_nhds hz)
  have hu : ∀ᶠ w in 𝓝 z, DifferentiableAt ℝ (logPotential f k ξ) w := by
  {
    filter_upwards [isOpen_H.mem_nhds hz, hd.continuousAt.eventually_ne hp] with w hw hne
    exact differentiableAt_logPotential hf hw hne k
  }
  have h1 := second_directional_nonneg_at_min hmin hu
    (differentiableAt_fderiv_logPotential_apply hf hz hp k 1)
  have hI := second_directional_nonneg_at_min hmin hu
    (differentiableAt_fderiv_logPotential_apply hf hz hp k Complex.I)
  have ht := trace_realHessian_negative hf hk hz hp
  simp only [realHessian, Matrix.trace, Matrix.diag, Fin.sum_univ_two,
    Matrix.of_apply, Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one] at ht
  change secondDirectional _ z 1 1 + secondDirectional _ z Complex.I Complex.I < 0 at ht
  unfold secondDirectional at ht
  linarith
}
▸
Proof of Lemma 5.2 · 04-critical-pairing.tex:96–130 · Eq (5.9) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proof. Put $g = F'/(F-\xi)$. Since $\log|F-\xi| = \operatorname{Re}\log(F-\xi)$, one has $\partial_x\log|F-\xi| = \operatorname{Re}g$ and $\partial_y\log|F-\xi| = -\operatorname{Im}g$, so $(\partial_x - i\partial_y)\ell = -\frac{ik}{y} - g$. Thus $\nabla\ell(z) = 0$ if and only if $g(z) = -ik/y$, which rearranges to $G_F(z) = \xi$. Taking absolute values gives $|F(z)-\xi| = (y/k)|F'(z)| = y/(k|Q_F(z)|)$, and (5.6) follows. Since $\log|F-\xi|$ is harmonic away from $F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$, $\Delta\ell = k\Delta\log y = -k/y^2 < 0$. Moreover, $\partial_{xx}\log|F-\xi| = -\partial_{yy}\log|F-\xi| = \operatorname{Re}(g')$ and $\partial_{xy}\log|F-\xi| = -\operatorname{Im}(g')$. At a critical point, $g = -ik/y$, so $$g' = \frac{F''}{F'}g - g^2 = \left(i\frac{q_F}{y}\right)\left(-\frac{ik}{y}\right) + \frac{k^2}{y^2} = \frac{k(k+q_F)}{y^2}. \qquad (5.9)$$ Taking real and imaginary parts gives (5.7). Its determinant is $-\frac{k^2}{y^4}\bigl[k(k-1) + (2k-1)a_F + a_F^2 + b_F^2\bigr] = -\frac{k^4}{y^4}J_F(z)$ by (5.3). Since $\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Hess}\ell = \Delta\ell < 0$, a positive determinant forces both eigenvalues of $\operatorname{Hess}\ell$ to be negative. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Step-by-Step Algebraic Verification of (5.7), (5.8), and (5.9) (~10× Depth)
  1. Quotient Rule for $g = F'/(F-\xi)$: Differentiating $g$ gives $g' = \frac{F''(F-\xi) - (F')^2}{(F-\xi)^2} = \frac{F''}{F'}g - g^2$. At $G_F(z) = \xi$, we have $g = -ik/y$ and $F''/F' = i q_F/y$, so $g' = \frac{k(k+q_F)}{y^2} = \frac{k(k+a_F)}{y^2} + i\frac{k b_F}{y^2}$.
  2. Entries of $\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z)$: Since $\ell = k\log y - \log|F-\xi|$,
    • $\partial_{xx}\ell = -\operatorname{Re}(g') = -\frac{k}{y^2}(k + a_F)$,
    • $\partial_{xy}\ell = \operatorname{Im}(g') = \frac{k}{y^2}b_F = -\frac{k}{y^2}(-b_F)$,
    • $\partial_{yy}\ell = -\frac{k}{y^2} + \operatorname{Re}(g') = -\frac{k}{y^2}(1 - k - a_F)$,
    which is the exact $2\times 2$ matrix in (5.7).
  3. Determinant of (5.7): $$\det\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z) = \frac{k^2}{y^4}\Bigl[(k+a_F)(1-k-a_F) - b_F^2\Bigr] = -\frac{k^2}{y^4}\Bigl[k(k-1) + (2k-1)a_F + a_F^2 + b_F^2\Bigr] = -\frac{k^4}{y^4}J_F(z).$$
File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2345–2460) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2895-L2933
lemma criticalHeight_count (beta : ℝ) (h_beta : beta > 1) (F : KClass) (xi : ℂ)
    (hg : is_good_pair beta F xi) {h : ℝ} (hh : 0 < h) :
    (positiveFiber beta F xi ∩ {z | h ≤ criticalHeight beta F z}).Finite ∧
    (negativeFiber beta F xi ∩ {z | h ≤ criticalHeight beta F z}).Finite ∧
    (positiveFiber beta F xi ∩ {z | h ≤ criticalHeight beta F z}).ncard ≤
      (negativeFiber beta F xi ∩ {z | h ≤ criticalHeight beta F z}).ncard := by
{
  have hk : 0 < k_val beta := by unfold k_val; linarith
  have hc := lemma_5_5_count beta h_beta F xi hg h hh
  have hp : positiveFiber beta F xi ∩ {z | h ≤ criticalHeight beta F z} =
      {z ∈ H | is_saddle beta F xi z ∧ V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal h} := by
  {
    ext z
    simp only [positiveFiber, is_saddle, mem_inter_iff, mem_setOf_eq, ge_iff_le]
    refine ⟨fun ⟨⟨hz, hG, hJ⟩, hle⟩ => ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, ?_⟩,
      fun ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, hV⟩ => ⟨⟨hz, hG, hJ⟩, ?_⟩⟩
    · rw [V_target_eq_criticalHeight beta hk F hz hG]
      exact ENNReal.ofReal_le_ofReal hle
    · rw [V_target_eq_criticalHeight beta hk F hz hG] at hV
      exact (ENNReal.ofReal_le_ofReal_iff (criticalHeight_pos beta hk F hz).le).mp hV
  }
  have hn : negativeFiber beta F xi ∩ {z | h ≤ criticalHeight beta F z} =
      {z ∈ H | is_max beta F xi z ∧ V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal h} := by
  {
    ext z
    simp only [negativeFiber, is_max, mem_inter_iff, mem_setOf_eq, ge_iff_le]
    refine ⟨fun ⟨⟨hz, hG, hJ⟩, hle⟩ => ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, ?_⟩,
      fun ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, hV⟩ => ⟨⟨hz, hG, hJ⟩, ?_⟩⟩
    · rw [V_target_eq_criticalHeight beta hk F hz hG]
      exact ENNReal.ofReal_le_ofReal hle
    · rw [V_target_eq_criticalHeight beta hk F hz hG] at hV
      exact (ENNReal.ofReal_le_ofReal_iff (criticalHeight_pos beta hk F hz).le).mp hV
  }
  rw [hp, hn]
  refine ⟨hc.1.subset (fun z hz => ⟨hz.1, hz.2.2, hz.2.1.1⟩),
    hc.1.subset (fun z hz => ⟨hz.1, hz.2.2, hz.2.1.1⟩), ?_⟩
  rw [Set.ncard_def, Set.ncard_def]
  exact hc.2
}

5.3 Good pairs and affine covariance

Definition 5.3 & Lemma 5.4 (Good pairs and affine covariance)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section4_5.lean:2465–2615
▸
Def 5.3 & Lemma 5.4 · 04-critical-pairing.tex:134–188 · Eqs (5.10)–(5.11) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Definition 5.3. A pair $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{K}\times\mathbb{C}$ is good, written $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$, if:
  1. for every $\delta > 0$, the superlevel set $\{z \in \mathbb{H} : V^F_\xi(z) \ge \delta\}$ is compact in $\mathbb{H}$ (with $V^F_\xi = +\infty$ on $F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$);
  2. every $z \in \mathbb{H}$ with $G_F(z) = \xi$ satisfies $J_F(z) \ne 0$.
Lemma 5.4 (Good pairs and affine covariance). The set $\mathcal{G}$ is Borel in $\mathcal{K}\times\mathbb{C}$. For $P$-almost every $F \in \mathcal{K}$, the complement $\{\xi \in \mathbb{C} : (F,\xi) \notin \mathcal{G}\}$ has Lebesgue measure $0$. Moreover, for every $t = x_0 + iy_0 \in \mathbb{H}$, $w \in \mathbb{H}$, and $\xi \in \mathbb{C}$, with $\widetilde\xi = (\xi - F(t))/(y_0 F'(t))$, $$G_{T_t F}(w) = \frac{G_F(t\cdot w) - F(t)}{y_0 F'(t)}, \qquad J_{T_t F}(w) = J_F(t\cdot w), \qquad (5.10)$$ $$V^{T_t F}_{\widetilde\xi}(w) = y_0^{1-k}|F'(t)|\,V^F_\xi(t\cdot w), \qquad (5.11)$$ and consequently $(T_t F, \widetilde\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$ if and only if $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$.
Proof. Exhaust $\mathbb{H}$ by compact sets $K_m = \{|x| \le m,\ 1/m \le y \le m\}$. Condition (1) holds iff for every $n \ge 1$ there exists $m \ge 1$ such that $\sup_{z \in \mathbb{H}\setminus K_m} V^F_\xi(z) < 1/n$; Condition (2) holds iff for every $m \ge 1$, $\inf_{z \in K_m}(|G_F(z)-\xi| + |J_F(z)|) > 0$. Both are countable intersections/unions of open conditions, so $\mathcal{G}$ is Borel. For fixed $F \in \mathcal{K}$, the critical value set $G_F(\{J_F = 0\})$ has Lebesgue measure $0$ by the 2D Sard theorem, and by Proposition 4.1 the set of $\xi$ violating (1) has Lebesgue measure $0$ for $P$-a.e. $F$. Finally, since $(T_t F)(w) = (F(t\cdot w)-F(t))/(y_0 F'(t))$, $(T_t F)'(w) = F'(t\cdot w)/F'(t)$, and $\operatorname{Im}(t\cdot w) = y_0\operatorname{Im}w$, direct substitution yields (5.10)–(5.11). $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Exact Scale Invariance $J_{T_t F}(w) = J_F(t\cdot w)$ & 2D Sard Covering (~10× Depth)
  1. Why $J_{T_t F}(w) = J_F(t\cdot w)$ Has No $|F'(t)|$ Prefactor: Because $J_F(z) = \det DG_F(z)/|F'(z)|^2$ is already normalized by $|F'(z)|^2$ in (5.2), and $q_{T_t F}(w) = q_F(t\cdot w)$ by (3.7), formula (5.3) immediately gives $J_{T_t F}(w) = J_F(t\cdot w)$!
  2. Derivation of (5.11) for $V^{T_t F}_{\widetilde\xi}(w)$: With $w = u+iv$ and $\operatorname{Im}(t\cdot w) = y_0 v$, $$V^{T_t F}_{\widetilde\xi}(w) = \frac{v^k}{|(T_t F)(w) - \widetilde\xi|} = \frac{v^k}{|F(t\cdot w) - \xi|/(y_0|F'(t)|)} = y_0^{1-k}|F'(t)|\frac{(y_0 v)^k}{|F(t\cdot w)-\xi|} = y_0^{1-k}|F'(t)|\,V^F_\xi(t\cdot w).$$
  3. Elementary 2D Sard Covering on $K_m$: On each compact $K_m$, $DG_F$ is Lipschitz. At any critical point $z_0 \in K_m$ where $\det DG_F(z_0) = 0$, the image of $DG_F(z_0)$ lies in a 1D line, so Taylor's theorem maps a square of side $r$ around $z_0$ into a thin rectangle of length $O(r)$ and width $O(r^2)$ (area $O(r^3)$), forcing $|G_F(\{J_F = 0\}\cap K_m)| = 0$.
File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2465–2615) & brennan/Section6_Transport.lean (lines 172–284) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2342-L2344
def is_good_pair (beta : ℝ) (F : KClass) (xi : ℂ) : Prop :=
  (∀ delta > 0, IsCompact {z ∈ H | V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal delta}) ∧
  (∀ z ∈ H, G beta F z = xi → J beta F z ≠ 0)

-- Source: brennan/Section6_Transport.lean#L172-L187
lemma G_T_elem (beta : ℝ) (F : KClass) {t w : ℂ} (ht : t ∈ H) (hw : w ∈ H) :
    G beta (T_elem F t ht) w = rerootTarget F t (G beta F (aff t w)) := by
{
  have hy : (t.im : ℂ) ≠ 0 := Complex.ofReal_ne_zero.mpr (ne_of_gt ht)
  have hd : deriv F.F t ≠ 0 := deriv_ne_zero_of_univalentOn isOpen_H F.univalent ht
  have hF_aff : DifferentiableAt ℂ F.F (aff t w) :=
    (F.univalent.1 _ (aff_mem_H ht hw)).differentiableAt (isOpen_H.mem_nhds (aff_mem_H ht hw))
  have him : ((aff t w).im : ℂ) = (t.im : ℂ) * (w.im : ℂ) := by simp [aff]
  change G beta (T_elem F t ht) w = _
  unfold G rerootTarget
  change T t F.F w - I / (k_val beta : ℂ) * (w.im : ℂ) * deriv (T t F.F) w = _
  rw [deriv_T F.F t w hF_aff hy]
  simp only [T, him]
  field_simp
  ring
}

-- Source: brennan/Section6_Transport.lean#L197-L205
lemma J_T_elem (beta : ℝ) (hk : k_val beta ≠ 0) (F : KClass) {t w : ℂ}
    (ht : t ∈ H) (hw : w ∈ H) :
    J beta (T_elem F t ht) w = J beta F (aff t w) := by
{
  apply mul_left_cancel₀ (pow_ne_zero 2 hk)
  rw [J_identity beta hk (T_elem F t ht) hw, J_identity beta hk F (aff_mem_H ht hw)]
  have hq : q (T_elem F t ht) w = q F (aff t w) := q_T_eq F t w ht hw
  simp only [a, hq]
}

-- Source: brennan/Section6_Transport.lean#L261-L284
lemma is_good_pair_T_elem (beta : ℝ) (hk : 0 < k_val beta) (F : KClass)
    {t : ℂ} (ht : t ∈ H) (xi : ℂ) :
    is_good_pair beta (T_elem F t ht) (rerootTarget F t xi) ↔ is_good_pair beta F xi := by
{
  have hc := heightScaleInverse_pos beta F ht
  constructor
  · rintro ⟨hcompact, hregular⟩
    refine ⟨fun delta hdelta => ?_, fun w hw hG => ?_⟩
    · have h := hcompact (delta / heightScaleInverse beta F t) (div_pos hdelta hc)
      rw [superlevel_V_target_T_elem beta F ht xi (div_pos hdelta hc),
        div_mul_cancel₀ _ hc.ne'] at h
      exact (affineHome t ht).isCompact_preimage.mp h
    · let z := (affineHome t ht).symm w
      have he : aff t z = w := (affineHome t ht).apply_symm_apply w
      have hz : z ∈ H := (aff_mem_H_iff ht z).mp (he.symm ▸ hw)
      have hr := hregular z hz ((G_T_elem_fiber beta F ht hz xi).mpr (he.symm ▸ hG))
      rwa [J_T_elem beta hk.ne' F ht hz, he] at hr
  · rintro ⟨hcompact, hregular⟩
    refine ⟨fun delta hdelta => ?_, fun z hz hG => ?_⟩
    · rw [superlevel_V_target_T_elem beta F ht xi hdelta]
      exact (affineHome t ht).isCompact_preimage.mpr (hcompact _ (mul_pos hdelta hc))
    · rw [J_T_elem beta hk.ne' F ht hz]
      exact hregular _ (aff_mem_H ht hz) ((G_T_elem_fiber beta F ht hz xi).mp hG)
}

5.4 Counting above a level and pairing

Lemma 5.5 (Counting above a level)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) ⚡ Bypasses 3, 4 & 5: Regularized Gradient, Zero Surgery & Winding Interior Section5_LevelIndex.lean:450–1944
▸
Lemma 5.5 Statement · 04-critical-pairing.tex:190–208 · Eq (5.12) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Let $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$. For every $\delta > 0$, the sets $$S_\delta(F,\xi) = \bigl\{z \in \mathbb{H} : G_F(z)=\xi,\ J_F(z)>0,\ V^F_\xi(z)>\delta\bigr\},$$ $$M_\delta(F,\xi) = \bigl\{z \in \mathbb{H} : G_F(z)=\xi,\ J_F(z)<0,\ V^F_\xi(z)>\delta\bigr\}$$ are finite, and $$\# S_\delta(F,\xi) \le \# M_\delta(F,\xi). \qquad (5.12)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Why Every Superlevel Component Has At Least One More Maximum Than Saddle (~10× Depth)

Why does $\# S_\delta(F,\xi) \le \# M_\delta(F,\xi)$ hold for every good pair $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$ and every $\delta > 0$? Consider the superlevel set $U_\lambda = \{z \in \mathbb{H} : V^F_\xi(z) > \lambda\}$ at a regular level $\lambda$ slightly above $\delta$:

  1. Because $\ell = \log V^F_\xi$ is strictly subharmonic ($\Delta\ell = -k/y^2 < 0$) outside $F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$, the minimum principle forbids $\mathbb{H}\setminus\overline{U_\lambda}$ from having any bounded connected components (holes). Indeed, a bounded component $H$ of $\mathbb{H}\setminus\overline{U_\lambda}$ would have $V^F_\xi = \lambda$ on $\partial H$ and $V^F_\xi < \lambda$ in $H$ (so $F \ne \xi$ on $\overline{H}$), forcing $\ell$ to attain an interior minimum in $H$, impossible since $\Delta\ell < 0$.
  2. With no holes, each connected component $W$ of $U_\lambda$ is a topological disk bounded by a single smooth simple closed curve $\Gamma = \partial W$ ($C_\lambda = 1$ boundary curve per component).
  3. By the Poincaré–Hopf index theorem on $W$, the outward-pointing vector field $-\nabla\ell$ has boundary winding number $+1$ on $\Gamma$, and its interior indices are $+1$ at each strict local maximum ($J_F < 0$), $-1$ at each saddle ($J_F > 0$), and $+1$ at $p = F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$ if $p \in W$. Summing over all components gives $$C_\lambda = \# M_\delta(F,\xi) - \# S_\delta(F,\xi) + B \implies \# S_\delta(F,\xi) = \# M_\delta(F,\xi) + B - C_\lambda \le \# M_\delta(F,\xi)$$ since $B = \#(F^{-1}(\{\xi\})\cap U_\lambda) \in \{0,1\}$ and $B \le 1 \le C_\lambda$ whenever $U_\lambda \ne \emptyset$.
File: brennan/Section4_5.lean (lines 2631–2640) & brennan/Section5_LevelIndex.lean (lines 1918–1944) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section4_5.lean#L2626-L2661
theorem lemma_5_5_count (beta : ℝ) (h_beta : beta > 1) (F : KClass) (xi : ℂ)
    (h_good : is_good_pair beta F xi) (h : ℝ) (hh : h > 0) :
  {z ∈ H | V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal h ∧ G beta F z = xi}.Finite ∧
  Nat.card {z ∈ H | is_max beta F xi z ∧ V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal h} ≥
  Nat.card {z ∈ H | is_saddle beta F xi z ∧ V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal h} := by
{
  have hk : 0 < k_val beta := by
  {
    unfold k_val; linarith
  }
  have hc := (critical_count F.univalent hk
    (is_good_pair_to_GoodPair beta hk.le F xi h_good) hh).2
  refine ⟨finite_critical_superlevel_KClass beta F xi h_good h hh, ?_⟩
  have hmax : {z ∈ H | is_max beta F xi z ∧ V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal h} =
      {z ∈ {z | z ∈ H ∧ criticalMap F.F (k_val beta) z = xi ∧
        ENNReal.ofReal h ≤ potential F.F (k_val beta) xi z} |
        normalizedJacobian F.F (k_val beta) z < 0} := by
  {
    ext z
    simp only [mem_setOf_eq, is_max, G_eq_criticalMap, J_eq_normalizedJacobian, ge_iff_le]
    exact ⟨fun ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, hV⟩ => ⟨⟨hz, hG, V_target_eq_potential beta hk.le F xi hz ▸ hV⟩, hJ⟩,
      fun ⟨⟨hz, hG, hV⟩, hJ⟩ => ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, (V_target_eq_potential beta hk.le F xi hz).symm ▸ hV⟩⟩
  }
  have hsad : {z ∈ H | is_saddle beta F xi z ∧ V_target beta F xi z ≥ ENNReal.ofReal h} =
      {z ∈ {z | z ∈ H ∧ criticalMap F.F (k_val beta) z = xi ∧
        ENNReal.ofReal h ≤ potential F.F (k_val beta) xi z} |
        0 < normalizedJacobian F.F (k_val beta) z} := by
  {
    ext z
    simp only [mem_setOf_eq, is_saddle, G_eq_criticalMap, J_eq_normalizedJacobian, gt_iff_lt, ge_iff_le]
    exact ⟨fun ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, hV⟩ => ⟨⟨hz, hG, V_target_eq_potential beta hk.le F xi hz ▸ hV⟩, hJ⟩,
      fun ⟨⟨hz, hG, hV⟩, hJ⟩ => ⟨hz, ⟨hG, hJ⟩, (V_target_eq_potential beta hk.le F xi hz).symm ▸ hV⟩⟩
  }
  rw [ge_iff_le, hmax, hsad]
  exact hc
}
Proof of Lemma 5.5 (Click any step below for Level 2 10× Math & Level 3 Lean 4):

Proof of Lemma 5.5 · Step 1 · 04-critical-pairing.tex:210–225 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proof (Finiteness and regular level curves). Every critical point in $\{V^F_\xi \ge \delta\}$ is nondegenerate by Definition 5.3(2) and Lemma 5.2, hence isolated; since $\{V^F_\xi \ge \delta\}$ is compact by Definition 5.3(1), $S_\delta(F,\xi)$ and $M_\delta(F,\xi)$ are finite. If $S_\delta(F,\xi)$ is empty, (5.12) is immediate. Assume $S_\delta(F,\xi) \ne \emptyset$. Because $\{V^F_\xi \ge \delta\}$ contains only finitely many critical points, we may choose $\lambda > \delta$ smaller than the minimum of $V^F_\xi$ on $S_\delta(F,\xi) \cup M_\delta(F,\xi)$. Then $\lambda$ is a regular value of $V^F_\xi$, $S_\lambda(F,\xi) = S_\delta(F,\xi)$, and $M_\lambda(F,\xi) = M_\delta(F,\xi)$. Put $U_\lambda = \{z \in \mathbb{H} : V^F_\xi(z) > \lambda\}$; it is open, nonempty, and has compact closure $\overline{U_\lambda} \subset \{V^F_\xi \ge \lambda\} \subset \mathbb{H}$. Since $\lambda$ is regular, $\partial U_\lambda = \{V^F_\xi = \lambda\}$ is a finite disjoint union of smooth simple closed curves in $\mathbb{H}$.
🔍 Level 2 — Desingularizing $F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$ via $\Phi = (V^F_\xi)^{-2}$ in Lean 4 (~10× Depth)

If $\xi \in F(\mathbb{H})$, say $F(p) = \xi$, then $V^F_\xi(z) = y^k/|F(z)-\xi|$ blows up to $+\infty$ at $z = p$ and $\ell(z) = \log V^F_\xi(z)$ has a $-\log|z-p|$ singularity. In our Lean 4 formalization (`Section5_LevelIndex.lean`), we work equivalently with the smooth reciprocal squared potential $$\Phi(z) = \bigl(V^F_\xi(z)\bigr)^{-2} = y^{-2k}|F(z)-\xi|^2,$$ which is $C^\infty$ across all of $\mathbb{H}$, satisfies $\{V^F_\xi \ge \lambda\} = \{\Phi \le \lambda^{-2}\}$, and turns the pole $p = F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$ into a smooth isolated local minimum $\Phi(p) = 0$ with positive Hessian determinant.

def squaredReciprocalPotential (f : ℂ → ℂ) (k : ℝ) (ξ z : ℂ) : ℝ :=
  ‖f z - ξ‖^2 * z.im ^ (-2 * k)

def logPotential (f : ℂ → ℂ) (k : ℝ) (ξ z : ℂ) : ℝ :=
  k * Real.log z.im - Real.log ‖f z - ξ‖

Proof of Lemma 5.5 · Steps 2–3 · 04-critical-pairing.tex:226–282 · Eq (5.13) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math + Bypass 5)
No bounded complement components & Turning tangent. We first show that $\mathbb{H}\setminus\overline{U_\lambda}$ has no bounded connected component. Indeed, if $H$ were a bounded connected component of $\mathbb{H}\setminus\overline{U_\lambda}$, then $\partial H \subset \partial U_\lambda \subset \mathbb{H}$, so $\overline{H} \subset \mathbb{H}$ is compact, $V^F_\xi < \lambda$ on $H$, and $V^F_\xi = \lambda$ on $\partial H$. In particular $F \ne \xi$ on $\overline{H}$, so $\ell = \log V^F_\xi$ is smooth on $H$, continuous on $\overline{H}$, equals $\log\lambda$ on $\partial H$, and satisfies $\ell < \log\lambda$ in $H$. Its minimum on $\overline{H}$ is therefore attained at an interior point $z_0 \in H$, where $\Delta\ell(z_0) \ge 0$, contradicting $\Delta\ell = -k/y^2 < 0$.
Let $\Gamma$ be a connected component of $\partial U_\lambda$, oriented positively with respect to $U_\lambda$, so that the interior of $U_\lambda$ lies to the left of $\Gamma$ and the outward normal is $-i\gamma'(s)$ when $\Gamma$ is parametrized by arc length $\gamma : [0,L] \to \mathbb{H}$. Because $\mathbb{H}\setminus\overline{U_\lambda}$ has no bounded connected components, $\Gamma$ is the outer boundary of the connected component of $U_\lambda$ that it bounds. Choose the starting point $\gamma(0) = \gamma(L)$ to be a leftmost point of $\Gamma$, so that $\Gamma$ lies in the half-plane $\{\operatorname{Re}z \ge \operatorname{Re}\gamma(0)\}$ and the initial tangent points down: $\gamma'(0) = -i$. On the triangle $T = \{(s_1,s_2) : 0 \le s_1 \le s_2 \le L\}$, define $$\sigma(s_1, s_2) = \begin{cases} \dfrac{\gamma(s_2) - \gamma(s_1)}{s_2 - s_1}, & 0 \le s_1 < s_2 \le L,\ (s_1,s_2) \ne (0,L), \\ \gamma'(s_1), & s_1 = s_2, \\ -\gamma'(0), & (s_1,s_2) = (0,L). \end{cases} \qquad (5.13)$$ Since $\gamma$ is $C^1$ and simple, $\sigma : T \to \mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}$ is continuous. As $T$ is convex, $\sigma/|\sigma|$ lifts to a continuous angle $\vartheta : T \to \mathbb{R}$ with $\vartheta(0,0) = -\pi/2$. For $0 < s < L$, $\operatorname{Re}(\gamma(s)-\gamma(0)) \ge 0$ and $\gamma(s) \ne \gamma(0)$, so $\sigma(0,s)$ never points into the negative real axis; hence $\vartheta(0,s) \in (-\pi, \pi)$, and since $\sigma(0,L) = -\gamma'(0) = i$ we get $\vartheta(0,L) = \pi/2$. Likewise, for $0 < s < L$, $\sigma(s,L) = (\gamma(0)-\gamma(s))/(L-s)$ has $\operatorname{Re}\sigma(s,L) \le 0$ and never points into the positive real axis; hence $\vartheta(s,L) \in (0, 2\pi)$, and since $\sigma(L,L) = \gamma'(L) = -i$ we obtain $\vartheta(L,L) = 3\pi/2$. Restricted to the diagonal $s_1 = s_2$, this says that the unit tangent $\gamma'(s)$ winds once counterclockwise as $s$ runs from $0$ to $L$ (Hopf [1935]). Along $\Gamma$, $\ell = \log\lambda$ is constant and $U_\lambda = \{\ell > \log\lambda\}$ lies to the left of $\gamma'$, so $-\nabla\ell$ is a positive scalar multiple of the outward normal $-i\gamma'$. Therefore $-\nabla\ell/|\nabla\ell|$ also winds once counterclockwise along each boundary component $\Gamma$.
🔍 Level 2 — Hopf's 1935 Secant-Triangle Proof & Constructive Bypass 5 (`windingInterior`) (~10× Depth)
  1. Hopf's Secant-Triangle Proof of the Turning Tangent Theorem: Why does the continuous angle $\vartheta : T \to \mathbb{R}$ increase by exactly $2\pi$ along the diagonal $(s,s)$? Because the triangle $T = \{0 \le s_1 \le s_2 \le L\}$ is convex and $\sigma : T \to \mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}$ is nonvanishing, the change in $\vartheta$ along the hypotenuse $(0,0) \to (L,L)$ equals the sum of the changes along the two legs $(0,0) \to (0,L)$ and $(0,L) \to (L,L)$:
    • At the leftmost point $\gamma(0)$ ($\operatorname{Re}\gamma(s) \ge \operatorname{Re}\gamma(0)$), the interior of $U_\lambda$ lies to the right ($+1$), so positive orientation ($i\gamma'(0)$ pointing inward) gives $\gamma'(0) = -i$, i.e. $\vartheta(0,0) = -\pi/2$.
    • Along the vertical leg $(0,s)$ ($0 < s < L$), $\operatorname{Re}(\gamma(s)-\gamma(0)) \ge 0$ and $\gamma(s) \ne \gamma(0)$, so $\sigma(0,s)$ omits $(-\infty, 0]$. Since $\sigma(0,0) = -i$ ($\vartheta = -\pi/2$) and $\sigma(0,L) = -\gamma'(0) = +i$, the continuous angle must reach $\vartheta(0,L) = +\pi/2$ (an increase of $+\pi$).
    • Along the horizontal leg $(s,L)$ ($0 < s < L$), $\sigma(s,L) = \frac{\gamma(0)-\gamma(s)}{L-s}$ has $\operatorname{Re}\sigma(s,L) \le 0$, omitting $[0, +\infty)$. Since $\sigma(0,L) = +i$ ($\vartheta = \pi/2$) and $\sigma(L,L) = \gamma'(L) = -i$, the continuous angle must reach $\vartheta(L,L) = 3\pi/2$ (a second increase of $+\pi$).
    Thus $\vartheta(L,L) - \vartheta(0,0) = \frac{3\pi}{2} - (-\frac{\pi}{2}) = 2\pi$.
  2. Constructive Bypass 5 (Winding-Number Interior) In our Lean 4 formalization (`Section5_LevelIndex.lean`), we define the interior of any regular level curve $\gamma$ analytically via the Cauchy winding number: $$\operatorname{windingInterior}(\gamma) := \bigl\{w \in \mathbb{C}\setminus\operatorname{range}(\gamma) : \operatorname{windingNumber}(\gamma, w) \ne 0\bigr\},$$ and prove `reciprocalPotential_lt_on_windingInterior` directly from `not_isLocalMin_logPotential`.
