Proof of Lemma 5.5 · Step 4 (Index sum) · 04-critical-pairing.tex:284–316 · Eq (5.14) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math + Bypasses 3 & 4)
Poincaré–Hopf index sum. Inside $U_\lambda$, the vector field $-\nabla\ell$ is smooth and nonvanishing away from the finitely many critical points $S_\lambda(F,\xi) \cup M_\lambda(F,\xi)$ and the set $F^{-1}(\{\xi\})\cap U_\lambda$, which consists of at most one point $p$ because $F$ is univalent. Excising small disks around these isolated points and comparing boundary winding numbers gives:
  • at a nondegenerate critical point $z$, the index of $-\nabla\ell$ is $\operatorname{sgn}\det(-\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z)) = \operatorname{sgn}\det\operatorname{Hess}\ell(z) = -\operatorname{sgn}J_F(z)$ by (5.8), hence $+1$ at $z \in M_\lambda(F,\xi)$ and $-1$ at $z \in S_\lambda(F,\xi)$;
  • if $p \in F^{-1}(\{\xi\})\cap U_\lambda$, then near $p$ we have $F(z)-\xi = F'(p)(z-p) + O(|z-p|^2)$ with $F'(p) \ne 0$, so $-\nabla\ell(z) = \frac{z-p}{|z-p|^2} + O(1)$, which has index $+1$ on a small circle around $p$.
Summing over the $C_\lambda \ge 1$ connected components of $\partial U_\lambda$ yields $$C_\lambda = \# M_\lambda(F,\xi) - \# S_\lambda(F,\xi) + B, \qquad B = \#\bigl(F^{-1}(\{\xi\})\cap U_\lambda\bigr) \in \{0,1\}. \qquad (5.14)$$ Since $B \le 1 \le C_\lambda$, we obtain $\# S_\lambda(F,\xi) = \# M_\lambda(F,\xi) + B - C_\lambda \le \# M_\lambda(F,\xi)$, which is (5.12). $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Constructive Bypasses 3 & 4: Regularized Complex Gradient & Multiplicative Zero Surgery (~10× Depth)

To formalize the Poincaré–Hopf index sum (5.14) in Lean 4 without puncturing the domain at critical points or developing 2D manifold orientation theory, we combine two constructive algebraic bypasses:

  1. Constructive Bypass 3 (Regularized Complex Gradient) Define the globally smooth, non-singular complex vector field $$W_F(z) := 2iy\,|F(z)-\xi|^2\,\partial_{\bar z}\log V^F_\xi(z) = k|F(z)-\xi|^2 - iy\overline{F'(z)}\bigl(F(z)-\xi\bigr).$$
    • Away from $p = F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$, $W_F(z) = 0 \iff G_F(z) = \xi$, and its real Jacobian determinant is $\det DW_F(z) = -k^2|F(z)-\xi|^2|F'(z)|^2 J_F(z)$, which has sign $-\operatorname{sgn}J_F(z)$ ($+1$ at maxima in $M_\lambda$, $-1$ at saddles in $S_\lambda$).
    • Near $p = F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$, $F(z) - \xi = F'(p)(z-p) + O(|z-p|^2)$, so $W_F(z) = -iy_p|F'(p)|^2(z-p) + O(|z-p|^2)$ has a simple regular zero of real Jacobian determinant $+y_p^2|F'(p)|^4 > 0$ (index $+1$).
  2. Constructive Bypass 4 (Multiplicative Zero Surgery) Given a vector field $W : \mathbb{C} \to \mathbb{C}$ with finitely many regular zeros $z_1, \dots, z_m \in D$, we divide out each zero $z_0$ by $z - z_0$ (if $\det DW(z_0) > 0$) or $\overline{z - z_0}$ (if $\det DW(z_0) < 0$). Since $z - z_0$ has winding number $+1$ and $\overline{z - z_0}$ has winding number $-1$, logarithmic differentiation of products gives $$\operatorname{windingNumber}(W\circ\gamma, 0) = \sum_{j=1}^m \operatorname{sgn}\det DW(z_j)$$ because the final quotient $W_m$ is nowhere vanishing on $\overline{D}$ and therefore has winding number $0$.
File: brennan/Section5_Winding.lean (lines 894–960) & brennan/Section5_LevelIndex.lean (lines 1360–1700) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section5_LevelIndex.lean#L1353-L1354
def regularizedGradient (f : ℂ → ℂ) (k : ℝ) (ξ z : ℂ) : ℂ :=
  ((k : ℂ) * Complex.I / (z.im : ℂ)) * (f z - ξ) * conj (criticalMap f k z - ξ)

-- Source: brennan/Section5_LevelIndex.lean#L1734-L1783
lemma regular_potential_level_index_sum {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : UnivalentOn f H)
    {k : ℝ} (hk : 0 < k) {ξ : ℂ} (hg : GoodPair f k ξ) {c : ℝ} (hc : 0 < c)
    (hreg : ∀ z ∈ H, squaredReciprocalPotential f k ξ z = c → regularizedGradient f k ξ z ≠ 0) :
    ∃ (s : Finset ℂ) (N : ℕ), (∀ z ∈ s, z ∈ H ∧ regularizedGradient f k ξ z = 0) ∧
      (∀ z ∈ H, squaredReciprocalPotential f k ξ z < c → (z ∈ s ↔ regularizedGradient f k ξ z = 0)) ∧
      (∑ z ∈ s, if squaredReciprocalPotential f k ξ z < c then
        planeIndex (fderiv ℝ (regularizedGradient f k ξ) z) else 0) = (N : ℤ) ∧
      ((∃ z ∈ H, f z = ξ) → 1 ≤ N) := by
{
  let u := squaredReciprocalPotential f k ξ
  have hcd : c < c + 1 := by linarith
  obtain ⟨K, F, hK, hKcv, hKH, hPK, hFc, hFs, hFeq, hFzero, hFlt, hlevel, hFreg, hL⟩ :=
    extend_potential_sublevel hf hk hg hc hreg
  obtain ⟨C, hC, hdis, hcurves⟩ := compact_regular_level_oriented_curves hFc hFs hFreg hL
  let ι := {S : Set ℂ // S ∈ C}
  choose γ hγrange hγc hγp hγi hγd hγm hγr hγdown using (fun S : ι => hcurves S.1 S.2)
  have hγlevel (i : ι) (t : ℝ) : F (γ i t) = c := by
    have hh : γ i t ∈ ⋃ S ∈ C, S := mem_iUnion₂.mpr ⟨i.1, i.2, hγrange i ▸ mem_range_self t⟩; rwa [hC] at hh
  have hcover (z) : F z = c ↔ ∃ i : ι, z ∈ range (γ i) :=
    ⟨fun hz => by obtain ⟨S, hS, hzS⟩ := mem_iUnion₂.mp (hC.symm ▸ (show z ∈ {z | F z = c} from hz)); exact ⟨⟨S, hS⟩, (hγrange ⟨S, hS⟩).symm ▸ hzS⟩,
      by rintro ⟨i, t, rfl⟩; exact hγlevel i t⟩
  have hbelow (i : ι) (w : ℂ) (hw : w ∉ range (γ i)) (hind : loopIndex (periodicLoop (hγc i).continuous 0) w ≠ 0) : F w < c :=
    (hFlt w).mpr (squaredReciprocalPotential_lt_windingInterior hf hk hc ξ (periodicLoop (hγc i).continuous 0)
      (periodicLoop_closed _ (hγp i) 0) (fun t => (hlevel _ (hγlevel i ((t : ℝ) + 0))).1)
      (fun t => (hlevel _ (hγlevel i ((t : ℝ) + 0))).2) ⟨by rwa [periodicLoop_range _ (hγp i) 0], hind⟩)
  obtain ⟨s, X, hs, hXeq, hXzero, hD⟩ := regularizedGradient_cutoff hf hk hg hK hKH
  have hγK (i : ι) (t : ℝ) : γ i t ∈ K := hPK ⟨(hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).1, (hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).2.le.trans hcd.le⟩
  have horth (i : ι) (t : ℝ) : (X (γ i t) * conj (deriv (γ i) t)).re = 0 := by
    have he0 : fderiv ℝ F (γ i t) (deriv (γ i) t) = 0 :=
      ((((hFc.differentiable (by norm_num)) _).hasFDerivAt.comp_hasDerivAt t (((hγc i).differentiable (by norm_num)) t).hasDerivAt).congr_of_eventuallyEq
        (Filter.Eventually.of_forall (fun q => (hγlevel i q).symm))).unique (hasDerivAt_const t c)
    rw [(hFeq _ (hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).1 ((hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).2.le.trans hcd.le)).fderiv_eq,
      fderiv_squaredReciprocalPotential_apply hf (ne_of_gt hk) (hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).1, ← (hXeq _ (hγK i t)).self_of_nhds] at he0
    exact (mul_eq_zero.mp he0).resolve_left (mul_ne_zero (by norm_num) (ne_of_gt (Real.rpow_pos_of_pos (hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).1 (-2 * k))))
  have hsum := regular_level_index_sum hFc.continuous γ hγc hγp hγi hγd hγm hγr hγdown hγlevel
    (fun z hz => ⟨hFc.contDiffAt.of_le (by norm_num), hFreg z hz⟩) hcover
    (fun i j hij => by rw [hγrange, hγrange]; exact hdis i.1 i.2 j.1 j.2 (Subtype.val_injective.ne hij))
    hbelow ⟨0, hFzero ▸ hcd⟩ hKcv hγK s X (fderiv ℝ (regularizedGradient f k ξ)) (fun z hz => ((hs z).mp hz).1) hXzero hD
    (fun i t => by rw [(hXeq _ (hγK i t)).self_of_nhds]; exact hreg _ (hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).1 (hlevel _ (hγlevel i t)).2) horth
  refine ⟨s, Fintype.card ι, fun z hz => ⟨hKH ((hs z).mp hz).1, ((hs z).mp hz).2⟩,
    fun z hzH hzu => (hs z).trans ⟨fun h => h.2, fun hx => ⟨hPK ⟨hzH, hzu.le.trans hcd.le⟩, hx⟩⟩, ?_, ?_⟩
  · refine Eq.trans (Finset.sum_congr rfl (fun z hz => ?_)) hsum
    simp only [show (F z < c) ↔ u z < c from (hFlt z).trans (and_iff_right (hKH ((hs z).mp hz).1)), u]
  · rintro ⟨z, hzH, hpole⟩
    have huz : u z = 0 := by simp [u, squaredReciprocalPotential, hpole]
    have hFz : F z = 0 := (hFeq z hzH (huz.le.trans (hc.trans hcd).le)).self_of_nhds.trans huz
    obtain ⟨w, hw⟩ := intermediate_value_univ z 0 hFc.continuous (show c ∈ Icc (F z) (F 0) by rw [hFz, hFzero]; exact ⟨hc.le, hcd.le⟩)
    obtain ⟨i, _⟩ := (hcover w).mp hw
    exact Nat.succ_le_iff.mpr (Fintype.card_pos_iff.mpr ⟨i⟩)
}

Above every level $\delta$, Lemma 5.5 gives at least as many maxima as saddles, so matching the $r$-th saddle $s_r$ to the $r$-th maximum $m_r$ ($r = 0, 1, 2, \dots$) never lowers $V^F_\xi$.
Proposition 5.6 (Measurable equivariant pairing)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) ⚡ Bypass 6: Countable Borel Inverse Branches Section6_Transport.lean:380–1022

Proposition 5.6 Statement · 04-critical-pairing.tex:322–353 · Eqs (5.15)–(5.16) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Define the Borel set $$\mathcal{D} = \bigl\{(F,z) \in \mathcal{K}\times\mathbb{H} : (F, G_F(z)) \in \mathcal{G} \text{ and } J_F(z) > 0\bigr\}.$$ There exists a Borel map $R_F(z) : \mathcal{D} \to \mathbb{H}$ such that for every $(F,z) \in \mathcal{D}$, with $\xi = G_F(z)$ and $z' = R_F(z) = x' + iy'$:
  1. $G_F(z') = \xi$ and $J_F(z') < 0$;
  2. for fixed $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$, the map $z \mapsto R_F(z)$ is injective on $\{z \in \mathbb{H} : G_F(z) = \xi,\ J_F(z) > 0\}$;
  3. $V^F_\xi(z') \ge V^F_\xi(z)$, and hence $$(y')^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z')|^4 \ge y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4; \qquad (5.15)$$
  4. for every $t \in \mathbb{H}$ and $w \in \mathbb{H}$ with $(T_t F, w) \in \mathcal{D}$, $$R_{T_t F}(w) = t^{-1}\cdot R_F(t\cdot w). \qquad (5.16)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Equal-Rank Pairing, Fourth-Power Weight (5.15), and Affine Equivariance (5.16) (~10× Depth)
  1. Canonical Ordering (`heightPrecedes` in `Section4_5.lean:2803`): For fixed $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$, order both the saddle fiber $S(F,\xi) = \{G_F = \xi,\ J_F > 0\}$ and the maximum fiber $M(F,\xi) = \{G_F = \xi,\ J_F < 0\}$ by decreasing value of $V^F_\xi$, breaking ties by increasing lexicographic order of $(x,y) = (\operatorname{Re}z, \operatorname{Im}z)$. By Lemma 5.5, only finitely many points precede any given point, so each $z \in S(F,\xi)$ has a well-defined finite rank $r \in \{0, 1, 2, \dots\}$.
  2. Why $V^F_\xi(m_r) \ge V^F_\xi(s_r)$ Implies (5.15): If $V^F_\xi(m_r) < V^F_\xi(s_r)$, choosing $\delta \in (V^F_\xi(m_r), V^F_\xi(s_r))$ would give $\# S_\delta(F,\xi) \ge r+1 > r \ge \# M_\delta(F,\xi)$, contradicting Lemma 5.5(5.12). Thus $V^F_\xi(z') \ge V^F_\xi(z)$. Since $k = (\beta+3)/4 \implies 4(k-1) = \beta-1$, raising the critical-point identity (5.6) ($V^F_\xi(z) = k\,y^{k-1}|Q_F(z)|$) to the fourth power gives $$y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4 = \bigl(V^F_\xi(z)/k\bigr)^4, \qquad (5.17)$$ which immediately yields (5.15).
  3. Why Affine Transformations Preserve the Ranking Order: For $t = x_0 + iy_0 \in \mathbb{H}$ ($y_0 > 0$), the map $w \mapsto t\cdot w = (x_0 + y_0\operatorname{Re}w) + i(y_0\operatorname{Im}w)$ scales $V^F_\xi$ by the positive constant $y_0^{k-1}|F'(t)|^{-1} > 0$ (5.11) and preserves the lexicographic order of $(\operatorname{Re}w, \operatorname{Im}w)$. Hence $t\cdot m_r(T_t F, \widetilde\xi) = m_r(F,\xi)$, which is (5.16).

def heightPrecedes (h_fn : ℂ → ℝ) (z w : ℂ) : Prop :=
  h_fn w < h_fn z ∨ h_fn z = h_fn w ∧ (z.re < w.re ∨ z.re = w.re ∧ z.im < w.im)

theorem prop_5_6_equivariant_pairing (beta : ℝ) (h_beta : beta > 1) (F : KClass) (z : ℂ)
    (hz : z ∈ H) (hz_saddle : J beta F z > 0) (h_good : is_good_pair beta F (G beta F z)) :
  R_pair beta F z ∈ H ∧
  is_max beta F (G beta F z) (R_pair beta F z) ∧
  V_target beta F (G beta F z) (R_pair beta F z) ≥ V_target beta F (G beta F z) z := by
{
  have hk : 0 < k_val beta := by unfold k_val; linarith
  obtain ⟨hH, hmax, hh⟩ := R_pair_spec_height beta h_beta F hz hz_saddle h_good
  refine ⟨hH, hmax, ?_⟩
  rw [ge_iff_le, V_target_eq_criticalHeight beta hk F hz rfl,
    V_target_eq_criticalHeight beta hk F hH hmax.1]
  exact ENNReal.ofReal_le_ofReal hh
}

Proof of Proposition 5.6 · 04-critical-pairing.tex:355–425 · Eq (5.17) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math + Bypass 6)
Proof of Proposition 5.6. Fix $(F,\xi) \in \mathcal{G}$. Order both fibers $S(F,\xi) = \{z \in \mathbb{H} : G_F(z)=\xi,\ J_F(z)>0\}$ and $M(F,\xi) = \{z \in \mathbb{H} : G_F(z)=\xi,\ J_F(z)<0\}$ by decreasing value of $V^F_\xi$, breaking ties by increasing lexicographic order of $(x,y)$. For $z \in S(F,\xi)$ of rank $r \in \{0,1,2,\dots\}$, Lemma 5.5 ensures $\# M_\delta(F,\xi) \ge \# S_\delta(F,\xi) \ge r+1$ for every $0 < \delta < V^F_\xi(z)$; letting $\delta \uparrow V^F_\xi(z)$ shows that the $r$-th element $z'$ of $M(F,\xi)$ exists and satisfies $V^F_\xi(z') \ge V^F_\xi(z)$. Since $4(k-1) = \beta-1$, (5.6) gives $y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4 = (V^F_\xi(z)/k)^4$ (5.17) at every critical point, yielding (5.15), and (5.10)–(5.11) give (5.16).
To see that $R_F$ is Borel, let $\{B_m\}_{m\ge 1}$ be an enumeration of all closed disks $B_m = \overline{D(c_m, r_m)} \subset \mathbb{H}$ with center $c_m \in \mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q}$ and radius $r_m \in \mathbb{Q}\cap(0,\infty)$. For each $m, j \ge 1$, let $U_{m,j} \subset \mathcal{K}\times\mathbb{C}$ be the open set of pairs $(F,\xi)$ such that setting $D = DG_F(c_m)$, $\det D \ne 0$, $\sup_{w \in B_m} \|I - D^{-1}DG_F(w)\| < 1/2$, and $|D^{-1}(G_F(c_m)-\xi)| < (1/2 - 1/j)r_m$. On $U_{m,j}$, the map $w \mapsto w - D^{-1}(G_F(w)-\xi)$ is a $1/2$-contraction of $B_m$ into itself, so $G_F(w)=\xi$ has a unique solution $s_{m,j}(F,\xi) \in B_m$, depending continuously on $(F,\xi)$. Every nondegenerate solution of $G_F(z)=\xi$ lies in $s_{m,j}(F,\xi)$ for some $(m,j)$, so the rank of $z$ and the point $R_F(z)$ are selected from the countable family $\{s_{m,j}(F,G_F(z))\}$ by Borel conditions on $V^F_{G_F(z)}$, $J_F$, and the coordinates. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Constructive Bypass 6: Quantitative Newton Contraction & Countable Borel Inverse Branches (`BorelBranches`) (~10× Depth)

Constructive Bypass 6 Why does the Newton contraction $T(w) = w - D^{-1}(G_F(w)-\xi)$ on rational closed disks $B_m = \overline{D(c_m, r_m)}$ completely avoid abstract Polish-space measurable selection theorems?

  1. Self-Map & $1/2$-Contraction on $B_m$: For $w_1, w_2 \in B_m$, the fundamental theorem of calculus along the segment $[w_1, w_2] \subset B_m$ gives $|T(w_1) - T(w_2)| \le \sup_{w \in B_m}\|I - D^{-1}DG_F(w)\|\,|w_1 - w_2| \le \frac{1}{2}|w_1 - w_2|$. Moreover, for $w \in B_m$, $$|T(w) - c_m| \le |T(w) - T(c_m)| + |T(c_m) - c_m| \le \frac{1}{2}r_m + |D^{-1}(G_F(c_m)-\xi)| < r_m,$$ so $T(B_m) \subset B_m$, and Banach's contraction mapping theorem produces the unique root $s_{m,j}(F,\xi) = \lim_{n\to\infty} T^n(c_m) \in B_m$ as a pointwise limit of continuous functions of $(F,\xi)$.
  2. Countable Branch Selection (`BorelBranches` in `Section6_Transport.lean:615–1022`): Since every critical point in $\mathcal{D}$ is covered by the countable family of Borel branches $g_m(F,\xi)$, both the rank of $z$ in $S(F,\xi)$ and the $r$-th maximum $R_F(z) \in M(F,\xi)$ are expressed by countable boolean combinations of Borel predicates.

-- Source: brennan/Section6_Transport.lean#L615-L626
structure BorelBranches {X Y : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X] [MeasurableSpace Y]
    (f : X → Y) (s : Set X) where
  part : ℕ → Set X
  branch : ℕ → Y → X
  measurable_part : ∀ n, MeasurableSet (part n)
  disjoint : Pairwise (Disjoint on part)
  cover : (⋃ n, part n) = s
  injective : ∀ n, InjOn f (part n)
  measurable_branch : ∀ n, Measurable (branch n)
  measurable_image : ∀ n, MeasurableSet (f '' part n)
  right_inv : ∀ n y, y ∈ f '' part n → branch n y ∈ part n ∧ f (branch n y) = y
  left_inv : ∀ n x, x ∈ part n → branch n (f x) = x

lemma measurable_R_pair {beta : ℝ} (h_beta : beta > 1) :
    Measurable (fun q : pairingDomain beta => R_pair beta q.val.1 q.val.2) := by
{
  let phi : pairingDomain beta → pairingDomainDisk beta := fun q =>
    ⟨(ofKClass q.val.1, q.val.2), by
      have heq := (toKClass_ofKClass_eqOn q.val.1).symm
      refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩
      · rw [← (G_J_congr_eqOn beta heq q.val.2.2).2]
        exact q.2.1
      · rw [← (G_J_congr_eqOn beta heq q.val.2.2).1]
        exact (is_good_pair_congr_eqOn beta heq _).mp q.2.2⟩
  have hphi : Measurable phi :=
    ((continuous_ofKClass.comp (continuous_fst.comp continuous_subtype_val)).prodMk
      (continuous_snd.comp continuous_subtype_val)).measurable.subtype_mk
  have heq : (fun q : pairingDomain beta => R_pair beta q.val.1 q.val.2) =
      (fun r : pairingDomainDisk beta => R_pair beta (toKClass r.val.1) r.val.2) ∘ phi := by
  {
    funext q
    exact R_pair_congr_eqOn beta h_beta (toKClass_ofKClass_eqOn q.val.1).symm
      q.val.2.2 q.2.1 q.2.2
  }
  rw [heq]
  exact (measurable_R_pair_diskClass h_beta).comp hphi
}

6. Transport between saddles and maxima

Section 6 Intro · Area Formula · 05-transport.tex:1–25 · Eq (6.1) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Keep the standing assumption $\rho > 2$, and set $$J_{F,+}(z) = \max\bigl(J_F(z), 0\bigr), \qquad J_{F,-}(z) = \max\bigl(-J_F(z), 0\bigr), \qquad \omega_F(x+iy) = y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(x+iy)|^2.$$ Since $|\det DG_F(z)| = |F'(z)|^2 |J_F(z)| = |Q_F(z)|^{-2}|J_F(z)|$, the area formula for the $C^1$ map $G_F : \mathbb{H} \to \mathbb{C}$ (Federer [1969, Theorem 3.2.3]) gives, for every nonnegative Borel function $h : \mathbb{H} \to [0,\infty]$, $$\int_{\mathbb{H}} \omega_F(z) J_{F,\pm}(z) h(z)\,dA(z) = \int_{\mathbb{C}} \sum_{\substack{z \in \mathbb{H} : G_F(z)=\xi \\ \pm J_F(z) > 0}} y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4 h(z)\,dA(\xi). \qquad (6.1)$$
🔍 Level 2 — How the Weight $\omega_F(z) = y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^2$ Bridges the Area Formula and Fourth-Power Potential Height (~10× Depth)

Why do we multiply $J_{F,\pm}(z)$ by the specific weight $\omega_F(z) = y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^2$? Watch how two different powers of $|Q_F(z)| = |F'(z)|^{-1}$ combine:

  1. Relating $J_{F,\pm}(z)$ to $|\det DG_F(z)|$: By definition (5.2), $|\det DG_F(z)| = |F'(z)|^2 |J_F(z)| = |Q_F(z)|^{-2}|J_F(z)|$. Therefore, on $\{ \pm J_F > 0 \}$, $$\omega_F(z) J_{F,\pm}(z) = y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^2 \cdot |Q_F(z)|^2 |\det DG_F(z)| = \bigl(y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4\bigr)\,|\det DG_F(z)|.$$
  2. Applying Federer's Area Formula to $\psi(z) = y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4 h(z) \mathbf{1}_{\{\pm J_F > 0\}}$: Integrating $\psi(z)|\det DG_F(z)|$ over $\mathbb{H}$ equals the integral over $\xi \in \mathbb{C}$ of the fiber sum $\sum_{G_F(z)=\xi,\, \pm J_F(z)>0} y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4 h(z)$, which is (6.1).
  3. Why $y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4$ Inside the Fiber Sum Is Monotone Under $R_F$: By Proposition 5.6(5.15) (and (5.17), $y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4 = (V^F_\xi(z)/k)^4$), the fiber weight $y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4$ is nondecreasing along $z \mapsto z' = R_F(z)$ on every good fiber $G_F^{-1}(\{\xi\})$.

lemma transport_area (beta σ : ℝ) (F : KClass) (hσ : |σ| = 1)
    {h : ℂ → ℝ≥0∞} (hm : Measurable h) :
    (∫⁻ z in H, transportDensity beta F z * ENNReal.ofReal (σ * J beta F z) * h z) =
    ∫⁻ xi, fiberSum (G beta F) (jacobianRegion beta F σ)
      (fun z => targetDensity beta F z * h z) xi := by
{
  have hs := measurableSet_jacobianRegion beta F σ
  have he := (weighted_area_formula_regular hs
    (fun z hz => contDiffAt_G beta F hz.1)
    (fun z hz => jacobianRegion_regular beta F σ hz)
    ((measurable_targetDensity beta F).mul hm)).2
  refine Eq.trans ?_ he
  have hzero : (fun z => transportDensity beta F z * ENNReal.ofReal (σ * J beta F z) * h z) =ᶠ[ae (volume.restrict H)]
      (jacobianRegion beta F σ).indicator (fun z => transportDensity beta F z *
        ENNReal.ofReal (σ * J beta F z) * h z) := by
  {
    filter_upwards [ae_restrict_mem isOpen_H.measurableSet] with z hz
    by_cases hj : 0 < σ * J beta F z
    · rw [indicator_of_mem (show z ∈ jacobianRegion beta F σ from ⟨hz, hj⟩)]
    · rw [indicator_of_notMem (show z ∉ jacobianRegion beta F σ from fun h => hj h.2),
        ENNReal.ofReal_eq_zero.mpr (le_of_not_gt hj), mul_zero, zero_mul]
  }
  rw [lintegral_congr_ae hzero, lintegral_indicator hs, Measure.restrict_restrict hs]
  have hinter : jacobianRegion beta F σ ∩ H = jacobianRegion beta F σ :=
    inter_eq_left.mpr (fun z hz => hz.1)
  rw [hinter]
  apply setLIntegral_congr_fun hs
  intro z hz
  dsimp only
  rw [transportDensity_jacobian beta σ F hσ hz, mul_assoc]
}
Lemma 6.1 (Almost-everywhere goodness at $i$)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section6_Transport.lean:1475–1868
▸
Lemma 6.1 Statement · 05-transport.tex:27–35 · Eq (6.2) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
One has $$\mathbb{E}\Bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\,\mathbf{1}_{\{(F, G_F(i)) \notin \mathcal{G}\}}\Bigr] = 0. \qquad (6.2)$$
🔍 Level 2 — How Affine Covariance Converts a Single Basepoint $z = i$ into a 2D Target Integral (~10× Depth)

At the basepoint $z = i$, every normalized map $F \in \mathcal{K}$ has $F(i)=0$ and $F'(i)=1$, so $G_F(i) = -i/k$ is a single deterministic point in $\mathbb{C}$. Although Lemma 5.4 shows that $E_F = \{\xi \in \mathbb{C} : (F,\xi) \notin \mathcal{G}\}$ has 2D Lebesgue measure zero for $P$-almost every $F$, a null set can contain a single point $-i/k$. Why must the $J_{F,+}(i)$-weighted probability that $-i/k \in E_F$ vanish?

Because Lemma 3.1 equates $\mathbb{E}\Phi(F)$ (up to the positive factor $\int_{B_0} y^{-1}\,dA(z) = 2\log 2$) with the box integral $\mathbb{E}\int_{B_0} \omega_F(z)\Phi(T_z F)\,dA(z)$ over $B_0 = \{|x|\le 1,\ 1\le y\le 2\}$. By affine covariance (Lemma 5.4), $(T_z F, G_{T_z F}(i)) \notin \mathcal{G} \iff (F, G_F(z)) \notin \mathcal{G}$, and as $z$ varies over $B_0$ where $J_{F,+}(z) > 0$, the area formula (6.1) pushes the integral onto the null target set $E_F$, where it is identically zero.

-- Source: brennan/Section6_Transport.lean#L1559-L1581
lemma exceptional_transport_zero (h_rho : rho > 2) (beta : ℝ)
    (h_beta : beta = Real.log rho / Real.log 2) (F : KClass)
    (hg : ∀ᵐ xi : ℂ, is_good_pair beta F xi) :
    (∫⁻ z in H, transportDensity beta F z * ENNReal.ofReal (J beta F z) *
      {z : ℂ | z ∈ H ∧ ¬is_good_pair beta F (G beta F z)}.indicator 1 z) = 0 := by
{
  have hs := measurableSet_exceptionSlice h_rho beta h_beta F
  have he := transport_area beta 1 F (by norm_num)
    (measurable_const.indicator hs : Measurable ({z : ℂ | z ∈ H ∧ ¬is_good_pair beta F (G beta F z)}.indicator (1 : ℂ → ℝ≥0∞)))
  simp only [one_mul] at he
  refine he.trans ?_
  apply lintegral_eq_zero_of_ae_eq_zero
  filter_upwards [hg] with xi hxi
  unfold fiberSum
  apply ENNReal.tsum_eq_zero.mpr
  intro z
  have hn : z.val ∉ {z : ℂ | z ∈ H ∧ ¬is_good_pair beta F (G beta F z)} := by
  {
    rintro ⟨_, hbad⟩
    exact hbad (z.property.2.symm ▸ hxi)
  }
  exact (congrArg (targetDensity beta F z * ·) (indicator_of_notMem (f := (1 : ℂ → ℝ≥0∞)) hn)).trans (mul_zero _)
}

Proof of Lemma 6.1 · 05-transport.tex:37–59 · Eq (6.3) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proof. Put $\Phi(F) = J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{\{(F,G_F(i))\notin\mathcal{G}\}}$. By Lemma 5.4, for every $z \in \mathbb{H}$, $$\Phi(T_z F) = J_{F,+}(z)\,\mathbf{1}_{\{(F,G_F(z))\notin\mathcal{G}\}}. \qquad (6.3)$$ Let $B_0 = \{x+iy \in \mathbb{H} : |x| \le 1,\ 1 \le y \le 2\}$. For each fixed $F \in \mathcal{K}$, applying (6.1) with $h(z) = \mathbf{1}_{B_0}(z)\mathbf{1}_{\{(F,G_F(z))\notin\mathcal{G}\}}$ bounds $\int_{B_0} \omega_F(z)\Phi(T_z F)\,dA(z)$ by an integral over $\{\xi \in \mathbb{C} : (F,\xi) \notin \mathcal{G}\}$. By Lemma 5.4, the latter set has Lebesgue measure $0$ for $P$-almost every $F$, so $\int_{B_0} \omega_F(z)\Phi(T_z F)\,dA(z) = 0$ almost surely. Taking expectations and using Tonelli's theorem and Lemma 3.1 gives $$0 = \int_{B_0} y^{\beta-1}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[|Q_F(z)|^2\Phi(T_z F)\bigr]\,dA(z) = \left(\int_{B_0} y^{-1}\,dA(z)\right)\mathbb{E}\Phi(F) = 2\log 2\,\mathbb{E}\Phi(F). \quad \square$$
🔍 Level 2 — Complete Algebraic Chain of Lemma 6.1 (~10× Depth)
  1. Pulling Back $\Phi(T_z F)$: At $w = i$, $z\cdot i = z$. By Lemma 5.4(5.10), $J_{T_z F,+}(i) = J_{F,+}(z)$ and $G_{T_z F}(i) = \frac{G_F(z) - F(z)}{y F'(z)} = \widetilde\xi$ for $\xi = G_F(z)$. Thus $(T_z F, G_{T_z F}(i)) \notin \mathcal{G} \iff (F, G_F(z)) \notin \mathcal{G}$, proving (6.3).
  2. Why the Weight $y^{\beta-1}$ Produces the Hyperbolic Vertical Factor $y^{-1}$: By Lemma 3.1, $\mathbb{E}\bigl[|Q_F(z)|^2 \Phi(T_z F)\bigr] = y^{-\beta}\mathbb{E}\Phi(F)$. Multiplying by $y^{\beta-1}$ cancels $y^\beta$ down to $y^{\beta-1}y^{-\beta} = y^{-1}$, whose integral over $B_0 = [-1,1]\times[1,2]$ is $\int_{-1}^1 dx \int_1^2 y^{-1}\,dy = 2\log 2 > 0$.

lemma averagedDensity_window {beta : ℝ} (h_beta : beta > 1) (N : ℝ) (F : KClass) (z : H) :
    averagedDensity beta (windowTest beta N) (F, z) =
      transportDensity beta F z * ENNReal.ofReal (J beta F z) *
        (transportSlice beta N F).indicator 1 z := by

    simp
  · rw [indicator_of_notMem (fun h => hz (he.mp h)), indicator_of_notMem hz]
    simp

Proposition 6.2 (Transfer bound $\rho \le 2$)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section6_7.lean:51–180

Section 6 · Truncated Transport Box · 05-transport.tex:61–96 · Eqs (6.4)–(6.7) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
For each integer $N \ge 2$, define the truncated domain $$D_N = \left\{(F,z) \in \mathcal{D} : \text{with } z = x+iy \text{ and } R_F(z) = x'+iy',\ \frac{1}{N} \le \frac{y'}{y} \le N \text{ and } |x'-x| \le Ny\right\}. \qquad (6.4)$$ Since $R_F$ is Borel on $\mathcal{D}$, each $D_N$ is Borel, and $D_N \uparrow \mathcal{D}$ as $N \to \infty$. By (5.16), for every $z \in \mathbb{H}$, $R_{T_z F}(i) = z^{-1}\cdot R_F(z)$, and therefore $$\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,z) = \mathbf{1}_{D_N}(T_z F, i). \qquad (6.5)$$ For $R > 0$, $Y > 1$, and $N \ge 2$, set $$B_{R,Y} = \{x+iy \in \mathbb{H} : |x| \le R,\ 1 \le y \le Y\}, \qquad B'_{R,Y,N} = \left\{x+iy \in \mathbb{H} : |x| \le R+NY,\ \frac{1}{N} \le y \le NY\right\}. \qquad (6.6)$$ If $z \in B_{R,Y}$ and $(F,z) \in D_N$, then $z' = R_F(z) \in B'_{R,Y,N}$. Indeed, $1/N \le y' = (y'/y)y \le NY$ and $|x'| \le |x| + |x'-x| \le R + Ny \le R + NY$.
Fix $F \in \mathcal{K}$. Applying (6.1) with the sign $+$ and $h(z) = \mathbf{1}_{B_{R,Y}}(z)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,z)$, then bounding each fiber sum via Proposition 5.6(1)–(3) and applying (6.1) with the sign $-$ and $h(z') = \mathbf{1}_{B'_{R,Y,N}}(z')$, we obtain $$\begin{aligned} \int_{B_{R,Y}} \omega_F(z) J_{F,+}(z)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,z)\,dA(z) &= \int_{\mathbb{C}} \sum_{\substack{z \in B_{R,Y} : G_F(z)=\xi \\ (F,z) \in D_N}} y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4\,dA(\xi) \\ &\le \int_{\mathbb{C}} \sum_{\substack{z' \in B'_{R,Y,N} : G_F(z')=\xi \\ J_F(z') < 0}} (y')^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z')|^4\,dA(\xi) \\ &= \int_{B'_{R,Y,N}} \omega_F(z') J_{F,-}(z')\,dA(z'). \qquad (6.7) \end{aligned}$$
🔍 Level 2 — Why (6.7) Holds Pointwise for Every $F \in \mathcal{K}$ (~10× Depth)
  1. Hyperbolic Displacement Invariance (6.5): For $z = x+iy \in \mathbb{H}$ and $z' = x'+iy' = R_F(z)$, Proposition 5.6(5.16) at $t = z$, $w = i$ ($t\cdot i = z$) gives $$R_{T_z F}(i) = z^{-1}\cdot R_F(z) = \frac{x'-x}{y} + i\frac{y'}{y}.$$ Therefore, the condition $(T_z F, i) \in D_N$ ($\frac{1}{N} \le \operatorname{Im}R_{T_z F}(i) \le N$ and $|\operatorname{Re}R_{T_z F}(i)| \le N$) is identical to $(F,z) \in D_N$ ($\frac{1}{N} \le \frac{y'}{y} \le N$ and $|x'-x| \le Ny$).
  2. Injective Fiberwise Domination: Fix $\xi \in \mathbb{C}$. For every $z \in B_{R,Y}$ with $G_F(z) = \xi$ and $(F,z) \in D_N$, its paired point $z' = R_F(z)$ lies in $B'_{R,Y,N}$, satisfies $G_F(z') = \xi$ and $J_F(z') < 0$, and obeys $(y')^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z')|^4 \ge y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^4$ by (5.15). Because $z \mapsto R_F(z)$ is injective on the fiber $S(F,\xi)$, distinct $z$ map to distinct $z' \in B'_{R,Y,N} \cap M(F,\xi)$, so the sum over $z$ is bounded by the sum over $z'$.

lemma transport_box (h_rho : rho > 2) (beta : ℝ)
    (h_beta : beta = Real.log rho / Real.log 2) {N R Y : ℝ} (hN : 1 ≤ N) (F : KClass) :
    (∫⁻ z in H, transportDensity beta F z * ENNReal.ofReal (J beta F z) *
      (transportSlice beta N F ∩ transportBox R 1 Y).indicator 1 z) ≤
    ∫⁻ z in H, transportDensity beta F z * ENNReal.ofReal (-J beta F z) *
      (transportBox (R + N * Y) (1 / N) (N * Y)).indicator 1 z := by
{
  have h_beta_gt : beta > 1 := by
  {
    rw [h_beta]
    exact (one_lt_div (Real.log_pos (by norm_num))).mpr (Real.log_lt_log (by norm_num) h_rho)
  }
  let S := transportSlice beta N F ∩ transportBox R 1 Y
  let T := transportBox (R + N * Y) (1 / N) (N * Y)
  have hs : MeasurableSet S :=
    (measurableSet_transportSlice h_rho beta h_beta N F).inter (measurableSet_transportBox R 1 Y)
  have ht : MeasurableSet T := measurableSet_transportBox _ _ _
  have hp := transport_area beta 1 F (by norm_num) (measurable_const.indicator hs : Measurable (S.indicator (1 : ℂ → ℝ≥0∞)))
  have hn := transport_area beta (-1) F (by norm_num) (measurable_const.indicator ht : Measurable (T.indicator (1 : ℂ → ℝ≥0∞)))
  simp only [one_mul] at hp
  simp only [neg_one_mul] at hn
  apply le_trans hp.le
  apply le_trans ?_ hn.symm.le
  apply lintegral_mono
  intro xi
  apply le_trans (fiberSum_mul_indicator (G beta F) (jacobianRegion beta F 1) S (targetDensity beta F) xi).le
  apply le_trans ?_ (fiberSum_mul_indicator (G beta F) (jacobianRegion beta F (-1)) T (targetDensity beta F) xi).symm.le
  apply fiberSum_mono_injective (R_pair beta F) xi
  · intro z hz hG
    have hsz := hz.2.1
    have hpair := R_pair_spec_height beta h_beta_gt F hsz.1 hsz.2.1 hsz.2.2.1
    exact ⟨⟨⟨hpair.1, by simpa only [neg_one_mul] using neg_pos.mpr hpair.2.1.2⟩,
      pair_mem_enlargedBox hN hz.2.2 hsz.2.2.2⟩, hpair.2.1.1.trans hG⟩
  · apply (R_pair_injective beta h_beta_gt F).mono
    intro z hz
    exact ⟨hz.1.2.1.1, hz.1.2.1.2.1, hz.1.2.1.2.2.1⟩
  · intro z hz _
    exact targetDensity_le_pair h_beta_gt F hz.2.1.1 hz.2.1.2.1 hz.2.1.2.2.1
}

Proposition 6.2 Statement & Proof · 05-transport.tex:98–137 · Eq (6.8) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proposition 6.2 (Transfer bound). One has $\rho \le 2$.
Proof. Suppose for contradiction that $\rho > 2$. For fixed $N \ge 2$, apply Lemma 3.1 to $\Phi_N(F) = J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,i)$ and to $\Psi(F) = J_{F,-}(i)$. Using $J_{T_z F,\pm}(i) = J_{F,\pm}(z)$ and (6.5), we get $$\mathbb{E}\bigl[\omega_F(z) J_{F,+}(z)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,z)\bigr] = y^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,i)\bigr], \qquad \mathbb{E}\bigl[\omega_F(z) J_{F,-}(z)\bigr] = y^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,-}(i)\bigr].$$ Taking expectations in (6.7) and using Tonelli's theorem therefore yields $$2R\log Y\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,i)\bigr] \le 2(R+NY)\log(N^2 Y)\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,-}(i)\bigr]. \qquad (6.8)$$ First let $R \to \infty$ at fixed $Y, N$, dividing by $2R$, to obtain $$(\log Y)\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,i)\bigr] \le \log(N^2 Y)\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,-}(i)\bigr].$$ Next divide by $\log Y$ and let $Y \to \infty$ at fixed $N$; since $\log(N^2 Y)/\log Y \to 1$, this gives $\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,i)\bigr] \le \mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,-}(i)\bigr]$. Finally let $N \to \infty$. By monotone convergence and Lemma 6.1, $$\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\bigr] = \mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{D}}(F,i)\bigr] \le \mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,-}(i)\bigr],$$ so $\mathbb{E}J_F(i) = \mathbb{E}J_{F,+}(i) - \mathbb{E}J_{F,-}(i) \le 0$. This contradicts Lemma 5.1, which gave $\mathbb{E}J_F(i) > 0$. Hence $\rho \le 2$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Exact Box Integral Evaluation (6.8) & Double Limit $R \to \infty$, $Y \to \infty$ (~10× Depth)

Watch how cleanly the weight $\omega_F(z) = y^{\beta-1}|Q_F(z)|^2$ makes the expected box integrals in (6.8) elementary:

  1. Why $\mathbb{E}[\omega_F(z)J_{F,\pm}(z)] = y^{-1}\mathbb{E}[J_{F,\pm}(i)]$: Since $J_{T_z F,\pm}(i) = J_{F,\pm}(z)$ by (5.10), Lemma 3.1 gives $$\mathbb{E}\bigl[\omega_F(z)J_{F,\pm}(z)\bigr] = y^{\beta-1}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[|Q_F(z)|^2 J_{T_z F,\pm}(i)\bigr] = y^{\beta-1}\cdot y^{-\beta}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,\pm}(i)\bigr] = y^{-1}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,\pm}(i)\bigr].$$
  2. Evaluating $\int_{B_{R,Y}} y^{-1}\,dA(z)$ and $\int_{B'_{R,Y,N}} y^{-1}\,dA(z)$:
    • On $B_{R,Y} = [-R,R]\times[1,Y]$: $\int_{-R}^R dx \int_1^Y y^{-1}\,dy = 2R\log Y$.
    • On $B'_{R,Y,N} = [-(R+NY), R+NY]\times[1/N, NY]$: $\int_{-(R+NY)}^{R+NY} dx \int_{1/N}^{NY} y^{-1}\,dy = 2(R+NY)\bigl(\log(NY) - \log(1/N)\bigr) = 2(R+NY)\log(N^2 Y)$.
    This gives (6.8) in one line.
  3. Amenability of the Iterated Limit $R \to \infty$, then $Y \to \infty$: Dividing (6.8) by $2R\log Y$ gives $$\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,i)\bigr] \le \left(1 + \frac{NY}{R}\right)\left(1 + \frac{2\log N}{\log Y}\right)\mathbb{E}\bigl[J_{F,-}(i)\bigr].$$ Letting $R \to \infty$ kills $\frac{NY}{R} \to 0$; then letting $Y \to \infty$ kills $\frac{2\log N}{\log Y} \to 0$ (or along the diagonal $Y = \sqrt{R}$ as in `transport_ratio_tendsto` in `Section6_Transport.lean:1995`), leaving $\mathbb{E}[J_{F,+}(i)\mathbf{1}_{D_N}(F,i)] \le \mathbb{E}[J_{F,-}(i)]$.
File: brennan/Section6_Transport.lean (lines 1951–2147) & brennan/Section6_7.lean (lines 51–180) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section6_Transport.lean#L1951-L1972
lemma expected_transport_box (h_rho : rho > 2) (beta : ℝ)
    (h_beta : beta = Real.log rho / Real.log 2) {N R Y : ℝ}
    (hN : 1 ≤ N) (hR : 0 ≤ R) (hY : 1 ≤ Y) :
    ENNReal.ofReal (2 * R * Real.log Y) * (∫⁻ F, windowTest beta N F ∂P_eigenmeasure) ≤
      ENNReal.ofReal (2 * (R + N * Y) * Real.log (N ^ 2 * Y)) *
        (∫⁻ F, rootJac beta (-1) F ∂P_eigenmeasure) := by
{
  have hn : 0 < N := lt_of_lt_of_le zero_lt_one hN
  have hny : 1 / N ≤ N * Y := by
  {
    rw [div_le_iff₀ hn]
    have hc := mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_right (mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left hY hn.le) hn.le
    nlinarith [sq_nonneg (N - 1)]
  }
  have hp := averagedDensity_box h_rho beta h_beta (measurable_windowTest h_rho beta h_beta N) hR zero_lt_one hY
  have hm := averagedDensity_box h_rho beta h_beta (measurable_rootJac beta (-1))
    (add_nonneg hR (mul_nonneg hn.le (zero_le_one.trans hY))) (one_div_pos.mpr hn) hny
  simp only [div_one] at hp
  rw [show N * Y / (1 / N) = N ^ 2 * Y from by field_simp] at hm
  rw [← hp, ← hm]
  exact lintegral_mono (fun F => transport_box_averaged h_rho beta h_beta hN F)
}

7.1 Inverse-square means and negative powers

Proof of $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$, Corollary 7.1 & Negative Derivative Powers
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section6_7.lean:196–1230
▸
Proof of $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2)=1$ in Theorem 1.1 · 06-integrability.tex:1–35 · Eq (7.1) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proof of the integral-means assertion in Theorem 1.1. By Proposition 6.2, $\rho \le 2$. Proposition 2.4 therefore shows that for every $\epsilon > 0$ there is $C_\epsilon < \infty$ such that $$M_{-2}[f'](r) \le C_\epsilon(1-r)^{-1-\epsilon} \qquad \left(f \in \mathcal{S},\ \frac{1}{2} \le r < 1\right). \qquad (7.1)$$ Hence $\beta_f(-2) \le 1$ for every $f \in \mathcal{S}$, so $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) \le 1$.
For the reverse inequality, let $\kappa(z) = z/(1-z)^2 \in \mathcal{S}$ be the Koebe function. Then $\kappa'(z) = (1+z)/(1-z)^3$. For $1/2 \le r < 1$ and $|\theta - \pi| \le 1-r$, one has $|1+re^{i\theta}| \le 2(1-r)$ and $|1-re^{i\theta}| \ge 1$, so $|\kappa'(re^{i\theta})|^{-2} \ge \frac{1}{4}(1-r)^{-2}$. Integrating over $|\theta - \pi| \le 1-r$ gives $$M_{-2}[\kappa'](r) \ge \frac{1}{2\pi}\cdot 2(1-r)\cdot \frac{1}{4}(1-r)^{-2} = \frac{1}{4\pi}(1-r)^{-1}.$$ Thus $\beta_\kappa(-2) \ge 1$, and consequently $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$.
🔍 Level 2 — Detailed Koebe Lower Bound $M_{-2}[\kappa'](r) \ge \frac{1}{4\pi}(1-r)^{-1}$ (~10× Depth)

Why does the Koebe function $\kappa(z) = \frac{z}{(1-z)^2}$ achieve $\beta_\kappa(-2) = 1$? Near the boundary critical point $z = -1$ (where $\kappa'(-1) = 0$ corresponds to the tip $-1/4$ of the slit $(-\infty, -1/4]$), write $z = re^{i\theta}$ with $\theta = \pi + \phi$, $|\phi| \le 1-r$:

  1. By the triangle inequality, $|1 + re^{i(\pi+\phi)}| = |1 - re^{i\phi}| \le (1-r) + r|1 - e^{i\phi}| \le (1-r) + |\phi| \le 2(1-r)$.
  2. Meanwhile, $\operatorname{Re}(re^{i\theta}) = -r\cos\phi \le 0$ for $|\phi| \le 1-r \le 1/2 < \pi/2$, so $|1 - re^{i\theta}| \ge 1 - \operatorname{Re}(re^{i\theta}) \ge 1$.
  3. Hence $|\kappa'(re^{i\theta})| = \frac{|1+re^{i\theta}|}{|1-re^{i\theta}|^3} \le 2(1-r)$, so $|\kappa'(re^{i\theta})|^{-2} \ge \frac{1}{4}(1-r)^{-2}$ on an arc of length $2(1-r)$, giving $M_{-2}[\kappa'](r) \ge \frac{1}{4\pi}(1-r)^{-1}$.
File: brennan/Section6_7.lean (lines 196–250) Open in Editor ↗

Corollary 7.1 & Negative Powers · 06-integrability.tex:37–94 · Eqs (7.2)–(7.4) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Corollary 7.1 (Negative integral means past the critical point). For every $t \le -2$ and $\epsilon > 0$ there is $C_{t,\epsilon} < \infty$ such that $$M_t[f'](r) \le C_{t,\epsilon}(1-r)^{-(|t|-1)-\epsilon} \qquad \left(f \in \mathcal{S},\ \frac{1}{2} \le r < 1\right). \qquad (7.2)$$ In particular, $B_{\mathcal{S}}(t) = |t| - 1$ for all $t \le -2$.
Proof. For $f \in \mathcal{S}$ and $|z| = r$, the Koebe distortion lower bound $|f'(z)| \ge (1-r)/(1+r)^3 \ge (1-r)/8$ gives $|f'(z)|^t = |f'(z)|^{-2}|f'(z)|^{-(|t|-2)} \le 8^{|t|-2}(1-r)^{-(|t|-2)}|f'(z)|^{-2}$. Averaging over $|z| = r$ and applying (7.1) gives (7.2). On $|\theta - \pi| \le 1-r$, the Koebe function satisfies $|\kappa'(re^{i\theta})|^t \ge c_t(1-r)^{-|t|}$, so $M_t[\kappa'](r) \ge c'_t(1-r)^{-(|t|-1)}$ and hence $B_{\mathcal{S}}(t) = |t| - 1$. $\square$
For $-2 < t < 0$, Hölder's inequality on $|z| = r$ with exponents $2/|t|$ and $2/(2-|t|)$ gives $$M_t[f'](r) \le \bigl(M_{-2}[f'](r)\bigr)^{|t|/2}. \qquad (7.3)$$ Choose $\epsilon > 0$ so that $\frac{|t|}{2}(1+\epsilon) < 1$. On $0 \le r \le 1/2$, the distortion bound gives $|f'(re^{i\theta})| \ge (1-1/2)/(1+1/2)^3 = 4/27$, while on $1/2 \le r < 1$, (7.1) and (7.3) give $M_t[f'](r) \le C_\epsilon^{|t|/2}(1-r)^{-|t|(1+\epsilon)/2}$. Integrating in polar coordinates yields, for every $-2 < t < 0$, $$\sup_{f \in \mathcal{S}} \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^t\,dA(z) = 2\pi \sup_{f \in \mathcal{S}} \int_0^1 M_t[f'](r)\,r\,dr < \infty. \qquad (7.4)$$
🔍 Level 2 — Why $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2)=1$ Unlocks Both $t \le -2$ and $-2 < t < 0$ (~10× Depth)
  1. Phase-Transition Elbow at $t = -2$: For $t < -2$, writing $|f'|^t = |f'|^{-2}\cdot |f'|^{-(|t|-2)}$ and bounding the second factor pointwise via Koebe distortion $|f'(re^{i\theta})|^{-1} \le \frac{8}{1-r}$ shows $M_t[f'](r) \le 8^{|t|-2}(1-r)^{-(|t|-2)} M_{-2}[f'](r) \le 8^{|t|-2}C_\epsilon(1-r)^{-(|t|-1)-\epsilon}$. Together with the Koebe lower bound $M_t[\kappa'](r) \ge c'_t(1-r)^{-(|t|-1)}$, this pins down the entire ray $B_{\mathcal{S}}(t) = |t| - 1$ for $t \le -2$.
  2. Hölder Interpolation on $(-2, 0)$: For $-2 < t < 0$, $p = \frac{2}{|t|} > 1$ and $q = \frac{2}{2-|t|} > 1$ satisfy $\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q} = 1$. Hölder's inequality on $[-\pi,\pi]$ with the probability measure $\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}$ gives (7.3). Since $\frac{|t|}{2} < 1$, choosing $\epsilon = \frac{2-|t|}{2|t|} > 0$ makes the radial exponent $-\frac{|t|}{2}(1+\epsilon) = -\frac{2+|t|}{4} > -1$, which is integrable on $[1/2, 1)$.
File: brennan/Section6_7.lean (lines 1080–1230) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L1191-L1230
lemma schlicht_negative_area
    (h_means : ∀ ε > (0 : ℝ), ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 →
      integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε))
    {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) {t : ℝ}
    (ht : -2 < t) (ht0 : t < 0) : IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t) disk := by
{
  let q : ℝ := -t / 2
  let α : ℝ := (1 - q) / (2 * q)
  have hq : 0 < q := by dsimp [q]; linarith
  have hq1 : q < 1 := by dsimp [q]; linarith
  have hα : 0 < α := div_pos (by linarith) (by positivity)
  let A : ℂ → ℝ := fun z => (1 - ‖z‖) ^ α * ‖deriv f z‖ ^ (-2 : ℝ)
  let B : ℂ → ℝ := fun z => (1 - ‖z‖) ^ (-1 / 2 : ℝ)
  apply integrableOn_geometric_product Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet
    (A := A) (B := B) (weighted_inverse_integrable h_means hf hα)
    (integrableOn_disk_boundary_power (by norm_num))
    ((continuousOn_deriv_norm_rpow ⟨hf.1, hf.2.1⟩ t).aestronglyMeasurable Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet)
    hq.le hq1.le
  · intro z hz
    have hn : ‖z‖ < 1 := by simpa [disk, Brennan.Koebe.disk] using hz
    exact mul_nonneg (Real.rpow_nonneg (by linarith) _) (Real.rpow_nonneg (norm_nonneg _) _)
  · intro z hz
    have hn : ‖z‖ < 1 := by simpa [disk, Brennan.Koebe.disk] using hz
    exact Real.rpow_nonneg (by linarith) _
  · intro z hz
    have hn : ‖z‖ < 1 := by simpa [disk, Brennan.Koebe.disk] using hz
    have hd : 0 < 1 - ‖z‖ := by linarith
    dsimp [A, B]
    rw [Real.mul_rpow (Real.rpow_nonneg hd.le _) (Real.rpow_nonneg (norm_nonneg _) _),
      ← Real.rpow_mul hd.le, ← Real.rpow_mul (norm_nonneg _), ← Real.rpow_mul hd.le]
    have he : α * q + (-1 / 2) * (1 - q) = 0 := by dsimp [α]; field_simp; ring
    have htq : (-2 : ℝ) * q = t := by dsimp [q]; ring
    rw [htq]
    calc
      _ = ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t * 1 := by ring
      _ = ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t * ((1 - ‖z‖) ^ (α * q) * (1 - ‖z‖) ^ ((-1 / 2) * (1 - q))) := by
        rw [← Real.rpow_add hd, he, Real.rpow_zero]
      _ = _ := by ring
}

7.2 Function means and positive derivative powers

Lemma 7.2 (Function means) & Lemma 7.3 (Positive derivative powers)
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section6_7.lean:960–1079

Lemma 7.2 Statement & Proof · 06-integrability.tex:96–156 · Eqs (7.5)–(7.7) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Lemma 7.2 (Function powers). For every $0 < p < 1/2$ there is $C_p < \infty$ such that for all $f \in \mathcal{S}$ and $0 < r < 1$, $$\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi} |f(re^{i\theta})|^p\,d\theta \le C_p. \qquad (7.5)$$ Consequently, for every $0 < a < 1$, $$\sup_{f \in \mathcal{S}} \int_{\mathbb{D}} \bigl(1 + |f(z)|\bigr)^a\,dA(z) < \infty. \qquad (7.6)$$ Proof. Fix $f \in \mathcal{S}$. Since $f$ is univalent and $f(0) = 0$, $f$ vanishes only at $0$, so $|f|^p$ is smooth on $\mathbb{D}\setminus\{0\}$ and $\Delta(|f|^p) = p^2|f|^{p-2}|f'|^2$. Put $I(r) = \frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^\pi |f(re^{i\theta})|^p\,d\theta$. Because $|f(z)| = |z| + O(|z|^2)$ near $0$, Green's identity on $\{\varepsilon < |z| < r\}$ with $\varepsilon \downarrow 0$ gives $$\frac{d}{dr}\left(r\frac{d}{dr}I(r)\right) = \frac{p^2 r}{2\pi}\int_{D(0,r)} |f(z)|^{p-2}|f'(z)|^2\,dA(z). \qquad (7.7)$$ By theKoebe growth bound (Duren [1983, Theorem 2.6]), $|f(z)| \le r/(1-r)^2$ on $D(0,r)$. Since $f$ is injective, changing variables $w = f(z)$ shows that the integral in (7.7) equals $\int_{f(D(0,r))} |w|^{p-2}\,dA(w) \le \int_{D(0, r/(1-r)^2)} |w|^{p-2}\,dA(w) = \frac{2\pi}{p}r^p(1-r)^{-2p}$. Hence $\frac{d}{dr}(r I'(r)) \le p r^{p+1}(1-r)^{-2p}$. Since $2p < 1$ and $\lim_{r\downarrow 0} r I'(r) = 0$, integrating twice from $0$ to $r$ yields $I(r) \le C_p$. Finally, for $0 < a < 1$, write $a = p + (a-p)$ with $p = (2a+1)/6 \in (1/6, 1/2)$ and $a - p = (4a-1)/6 < 1/2$. Using $|f(re^{i\theta})|^{a-p} \le (1-r)^{-2\max(a-p,0)}$ and (7.5) gives $\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^\pi |f(re^{i\theta})|^a\,d\theta \le C_p(1-r)^{-2\max(a-p,0)}$. Since $2\max(a-p,0) < 1$, integrating over $r\,dr$ proves (7.6). $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Hardy–Stein / Green Identity (7.7) & Conformal Image Area Bound (~10× Depth)
  1. Laplacian of $|f|^p$ and Green's Identity (7.7): On $\mathbb{D}\setminus\{0\}$, write $|f|^p = (f\bar f)^{p/2}$. Applying $\Delta = 4\partial_z\partial_{\bar z}$ gives $$\Delta(|f|^p) = 4\partial_z\left(\frac{p}{2}(f\bar f)^{p/2-1}f\overline{f'}\right) = p^2(f\bar f)^{p/2-1}|f'|^2 = p^2|f|^{p-2}|f'|^2.$$ By divergence theorem on the annulus $\{\varepsilon < |z| < r\}$, $2\pi r I'(r) - 2\pi\varepsilon I'(\varepsilon) = \int_{\varepsilon < |z| < r} \Delta(|f|^p)\,dA(z)$. Since $|f(z)|^p = |z|^p(1 + O(|z|))$ near $0$, $\varepsilon I'(\varepsilon) = O(\varepsilon^p) \to 0$ as $\varepsilon \downarrow 0$, and differentiating in $r$ gives (7.7).
  2. Why Univalence Turns $\int_{D(0,r)} |f|^{p-2}|f'|^2\,dA$ into a Radial Target Integral: Under the injective holomorphic map $w = f(z)$ on $D(0,r)$, the Jacobian is $|f'(z)|^2\,dA(z) = dA(w)$, and the image $f(D(0,r))$ is contained in the disk $D(0, R_r)$ of radius $R_r = \frac{r}{(1-r)^2}$ by Koebe's growth theorem. Since $|w|^{p-2} \ge 0$, $$\int_{f(D(0,r))} |w|^{p-2}\,dA(w) \le \int_{D(0, R_r)} |w|^{p-2}\,dA(w) = 2\pi \int_0^{R_r} \rho^{p-1}\,d\rho = \frac{2\pi}{p}R_r^p = \frac{2\pi}{p}r^p(1-r)^{-2p}.$$
  3. Why $0 < p < 1/2 \implies 0 < a < 1$ in Area: Because $2p < 1$, $\int_0^1 (1-r)^{-2p}\,dr < \infty$, so $I(r) \le C_p$ is uniformly bounded in $r \in (0,1)$. In our 2D area integral $\int_0^1 \bigl(\int_{-\pi}^\pi |f(re^{i\theta})|^a\,d\theta\bigr)r\,dr$, we have an extra radial integration in $r$ that tolerates any singularity $(1-r)^{-\gamma}$ with $\gamma < 1$. Splitting $|f|^a = |f|^p \cdot |f|^{a-p}$ with $p < 1/2$ and $|f|^{a-p} \lesssim (1-r)^{-2(a-p)}$ requires $2(a-p) < 1 \iff a < p + 1/2$, which reaches every $a < 1/2 + 1/2 = 1$.
File: brennan/Section6_7.lean (lines 960–1025) Open in Editor ↗

lemma schlicht_function_area {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) {a : ℝ} (ha : a < 1 / 2) :

Lemma 7.3 Statement & Proof · 06-integrability.tex:158–192 · Eq (7.8) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Lemma 7.3 (Positive derivative powers). For every $0 < t < 2/3$, $$\sup_{f \in \mathcal{S}} \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^t\,dA(z) < \infty. \qquad (7.8)$$ Proof. Fix $0 < t < 2/3$, and choose $\eta > 0$ small enough that $$a = \frac{(2+\eta)t}{2-t} < 1.$$ For $f \in \mathcal{S}$, set $u_\eta(z) = |f'(z)|^2\bigl(1 + |f(z)|\bigr)^{-2-\eta}$. Since $f$ is injective on $\mathbb{D}$, the change of variables $w = f(z)$ gives $$\int_{\mathbb{D}} u_\eta(z)\,dA(z) = \int_{f(\mathbb{D})} (1+|w|)^{-2-\eta}\,dA(w) \le \int_{\mathbb{C}} (1+|w|)^{-2-\eta}\,dA(w) = \frac{2\pi}{\eta(1+\eta)}.$$ Writing $|f'(z)|^t = u_\eta(z)^{t/2}\bigl(1+|f(z)|\bigr)^{(2+\eta)t/2}$ and applying Hölder's inequality with exponents $2/t$ and $2/(2-t)$ yields $$\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^t\,dA(z) \le \left(\int_{\mathbb{D}} u_\eta(z)\,dA(z)\right)^{t/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{D}} \bigl(1+|f(z)|\bigr)^a\,dA(z)\right)^{(2-t)/2},$$ which is uniformly bounded by Lemma 7.2 because $0 < a < 1$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — How the Weighted Jacobian $u_\eta = |f'|^2(1+|f|)^{-2-\eta}$ Produces the Threshold $t < 2/3$ (~10× Depth)

Look at how short and transparent this proof of (7.8) is:

  1. Why $\int_{\mathbb{D}} u_\eta(z)\,dA(z) \le \frac{2\pi}{\eta(1+\eta)}$ for Every Univalent $f$: Because $w = f(z)$ is injective with Jacobian $|f'(z)|^2\,dA(z) = dA(w)$, the integral of $u_\eta(z) = |f'(z)|^2(1+|f(z)|)^{-2-\eta}$ over $\mathbb{D}$ equals $\int_{f(\mathbb{D})} (1+|w|)^{-2-\eta}\,dA(w) \le 2\pi\int_0^\infty (1+\rho)^{-2-\eta}\rho\,d\rho = \frac{2\pi}{\eta(1+\eta)} < \infty$.
  2. Exact Factorization of $|f'(z)|^t$: Raise $u_\eta(z)$ to the power $t/2$: $$u_\eta(z)^{t/2} = |f'(z)|^t (1+|f(z)|)^{-(2+\eta)t/2} \implies |f'(z)|^t = u_\eta(z)^{t/2} \cdot \bigl(1+|f(z)|\bigr)^{(2+\eta)t/2}.$$
  3. Where $t < 2/3$ Comes From: Applying Hölder's inequality with conjugate exponents $p = \frac{2}{t} > 1$ and $q = \frac{2}{2-t} > 1$ ($\frac{t}{2} + \frac{2-t}{2} = 1$) raises the second factor to the power $q \cdot \frac{(2+\eta)t}{2} = \frac{(2+\eta)t}{2-t} =: a$. By Lemma 7.2(7.6), $\int_{\mathbb{D}} (1+|f|)^a\,dA < \infty$ for $a < 1$. And when does there exist $\eta > 0$ such that $\frac{(2+\eta)t}{2-t} < 1$? Precisely when $$\frac{2t}{2-t} < 1 \iff 2t < 2 - t \iff 3t < 2 \iff t < \frac{2}{3}!$$
File: brennan/Section6_7.lean (lines 1019–1079) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L1008-L1025
lemma schlicht_weighted_derivative_integrable {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) {η : ℝ} (hη : 0 < η) :
    IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv f z‖^2 * bracketSq (f z) ^ (-1 - η)) disk := by
{
  have hc : ContinuousOn (fun z => ‖deriv f z‖^2 * bracketSq (f z) ^ (-1 - η)) disk :=
    ((continuousOn_deriv_univalent ⟨hf.1, hf.2.1⟩).norm.pow 2).mul (continuousOn_smoothPower_comp hf _)
  have hn : ∀ z : ℂ, 0 ≤ ‖deriv f z‖^2 * bracketSq (f z) ^ (-1 - η) :=
    fun z => mul_nonneg (sq_nonneg _) (Real.rpow_nonneg (bracketSq_pos _).le _)
  refine ⟨hc.aestronglyMeasurable Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet, ?_⟩
  apply (hasFiniteIntegral_iff_ofReal (Eventually.of_forall hn)).mpr
  have hh := conformal_lintegral_le Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet
    (fun z hz => hf.1.differentiableAt (Metric.isOpen_ball.mem_nhds hz)) hf.2.1
    (fun w => ENNReal.ofReal (bracketSq w ^ (-1 - η)))
  simp_rw [← ENNReal.ofReal_mul (sq_nonneg _)] at hh
  refine lt_of_le_of_lt hh ?_
  exact (hasFiniteIntegral_iff_ofReal (Eventually.of_forall
    (fun z : ℂ => Real.rpow_nonneg (bracketSq_pos z).le (-1 - η)))).mp
      (bracketSq_negative_integrable hη).hasFiniteIntegral
}

lemma schlicht_positive_area {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) {t : ℝ}
    (ht : 0 < t) (ht2 : t < 2 / 3) : IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t) disk := by
{
  let q := t / 2
  let η := (1 - 3 * q) / (4 * q)
  let a := (1 + η) * q / (1 - q)
  have hq : 0 < q := by dsimp [q]; linarith
  have hq3 : q < 1 / 3 := by dsimp [q]; linarith
  have hq1 : q < 1 := by linarith
  have hη : 0 < η := div_pos (by linarith) (by positivity)
  have ha : a < 1 / 2 := by
  {
    dsimp [a]
    apply (div_lt_iff₀ (by linarith : 0 < 1 - q)).mpr
    have he : (1 + η) * q = (1 + q) / 4 := by dsimp [η]; field_simp; ring
    rw [he]
    linarith
  }
  let A : ℂ → ℝ := fun z => ‖deriv f z‖^2 * bracketSq (f z) ^ (-1 - η)
  let B : ℂ → ℝ := fun z => bracketSq (f z) ^ a
  apply integrableOn_geometric_product Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet
    (A := A) (B := B) (schlicht_weighted_derivative_integrable hf hη)
    (schlicht_function_area hf ha)
    ((continuousOn_deriv_norm_rpow ⟨hf.1, hf.2.1⟩ t).aestronglyMeasurable Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet)
    hq.le hq1.le
  · intro z _hz
    exact mul_nonneg (sq_nonneg _) (Real.rpow_nonneg (bracketSq_pos _).le _)
  · intro z _hz
    exact Real.rpow_nonneg (bracketSq_pos _).le _
  · intro z _hz
    dsimp [A, B]
    rw [Real.mul_rpow (sq_nonneg _) (Real.rpow_nonneg (bracketSq_pos _).le _),
      ← Real.rpow_natCast ‖deriv f z‖ 2, ← Real.rpow_mul (norm_nonneg _),
      ← Real.rpow_mul (bracketSq_pos _).le, ← Real.rpow_mul (bracketSq_pos _).le]
    have he : (-1 - η) * q + a * (1 - q) = 0 := by
    {
      dsimp [a]
      rw [div_mul_cancel₀ _ (by linarith : 1 - q ≠ 0)]
      ring
    }
    have htq : (2 : ℝ) * q = t := by dsimp [q]; ring
    norm_num only [Nat.cast_ofNat] at *
    rw [htq]
    calc
      _ = ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t * 1 := by ring
      _ = ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t * (bracketSq (f z) ^ ((-1 - η) * q) * bracketSq (f z) ^ (a * (1 - q))) := by
        rw [← Real.rpow_add (bracketSq_pos _), he, Real.rpow_zero]
      _ = _ := by ring
}

7.3 Change of variables, Theorem 1.1, Corollary 7.4 & Corollary 1.2

Completion of Proof of Theorem 1.1, Corollary 7.4 (Sobolev Pullback) & Proof of Corollary 1.2
✓ Lean 4 Verified (0 Sorrys) Section6_7.lean:1232–1406
▸
Completion of Proof of Theorem 1.1 · 06-integrability.tex:194–227 · Eq (7.9) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Proof of the area assertion in Theorem 1.1. Let $\Omega \subsetneq \mathbb{C}$ be an open connected set whose boundary has at least two points, and let $\varphi : \Omega \to \mathbb{D}$ be a conformal bijection with inverse $f = \varphi^{-1} : \mathbb{D} \to \Omega$. Since $\varphi'(f(z)) = 1/f'(z)$ and the real Jacobian of $f$ is $|f'(z)|^2$, changing variables $w = f(z)$ gives, for every $p \in \mathbb{R}$, $$\int_\Omega |\varphi'(w)|^p\,dA(w) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^{2-p}\,dA(z). \qquad (7.9)$$ Put $f_0(z) = (f(z)-f(0))/f'(0)$. Then $f_0 \in \mathcal{S}$ and $f'(z) = f'(0)f'_0(z)$, so $$\int_\Omega |\varphi'(w)|^p\,dA(w) = |f'(0)|^{2-p}\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'_0(z)|^{2-p}\,dA(z).$$ For $4/3 < p < 4$, the exponent $t = 2-p$ lies in $(-2, 2/3)$. The integral is $\pi$ for $t = 0$, finite by (7.4) for $-2 < t < 0$, and finite by Lemma 7.3 for $0 < t < 2/3$. Thus $\int_\Omega |\varphi'(w)|^p\,dA(w) < \infty$ for all $4/3 < p < 4$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Unpacking the Jacobian Substitution (7.9) & Normalization to $\mathcal{S}$ (~10× Depth)
  1. Jacobian Change of Variables $w = f(z)$: Since $f = \varphi^{-1} : \mathbb{D} \to \Omega$ is a biholomorphism, its real $2\times 2$ Jacobian determinant is $\det Df(z) = |f'(z)|^2 > 0$, and $\varphi'(f(z)) = 1/f'(z)$. Thus $\int_\Omega |\varphi'(w)|^p\,dA(w) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^{-p}|f'(z)|^2\,dA(z) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^{2-p}\,dA(z)$.
  2. Three Sub-Intervals of $t = 2-p \in (-2, 2/3)$:
    • $p \in (2, 4) \iff t = 2-p \in (-2, 0)$: handled by (7.4) (`schlicht_negative_area`), proved from $B_{\mathcal{S}}(-2) = 1$ via Hölder interpolation.
    • $p = 2 \iff t = 0$: $\int_{\mathbb{D}} 1\,dA(z) = \pi < \infty$.
    • $p \in (4/3, 2) \iff t = 2-p \in (0, 2/3)$: handled by Lemma 7.3 (`schlicht_positive_area`).
File: brennan/Section6_7.lean (lines 1232–1406) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L1232-L1245
lemma schlicht_area
    (h_means : ∀ ε > (0 : ℝ), ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 →
      integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε))
    {f : ℂ → ℂ} (hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) {t : ℝ}
    (ht : -2 < t) (ht2 : t < 2 / 3) : IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv f z‖ ^ t) disk := by
{
  rcases lt_trichotomy t 0 with h | h | h
  · exact schlicht_negative_area h_means hf ht h
  · subst t
    simp only [Real.rpow_zero]
    exact integrableOn_const (ne_of_lt Metric.isBounded_ball.measure_lt_top)
  · exact schlicht_positive_area hf h ht2
}

-- Source: brennan/Section6_7.lean#L1332-L1381
lemma conformal_domain_area
    (h_means : ∀ ε > (0 : ℝ), ∃ C_ε : ℝ, ∀ (f : ℂ → ℂ) (_hf : Brennan.Koebe.IsClassS f) (r : ℝ),
      (1 / 2 : ℝ) ≤ r ∧ r < 1 →
      integralMean f (-2) r ≤ C_ε * (1 - r) ^ ((-1 : ℝ) - ε))
    {W : Set ℂ} (hW : IsOpen W) {φ : ℂ → ℂ}
    (hφ : DifferentiableOn ℂ φ W) (hb : BijOn φ W disk) {s : ℝ}
    (hs : 4 / 3 < s) (hs4 : s < 4) : IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv φ z‖ ^ s) W := by
{
  let f := Function.invFunOn φ W
  have hf : UnivalentOn f disk := bijOn_inverse_univalent hW hφ hb
  have himage : f '' disk = W := bijOn_inverse_image hb
  have hfi : IntegrableOn (fun w => ‖deriv f w‖ ^ (2 - s)) disk :=
    univalent_disk_area h_means hf (by linarith) (by linarith)
  have hderiv (w : ℂ) (hw : w ∈ disk) : deriv φ (f w) = (deriv f w)⁻¹ := by
  {
    have hz : f w ∈ W := hb.surjOn.mapsTo_invFunOn hw
    have hv : φ (f w) = w := hb.surjOn.rightInvOn_invFunOn hw
    have hd := (univalent_inverse_hasDerivAt hW ⟨hφ, hb.injOn⟩ hz).deriv
    change deriv f (φ (f w)) = (deriv φ (f w))⁻¹ at hd
    rw [hv] at hd
    rw [hd, inv_inv]
  }
  have hc : ContinuousOn (fun z => ‖deriv φ z‖ ^ s) W := by
  {
    refine (hφ.deriv hW).continuousOn.norm.rpow_const (fun z hz => ?_)
    exact Or.inl (norm_ne_zero_iff.mpr (deriv_ne_zero_of_univalentOn hW ⟨hφ, hb.injOn⟩ hz))
  }
  refine ⟨hc.aestronglyMeasurable hW.measurableSet, ?_⟩
  apply (hasFiniteIntegral_iff_ofReal (Eventually.of_forall (fun z : ℂ => Real.rpow_nonneg (norm_nonneg _) s))).mpr
  have he := conformal_lintegral_eq Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet
    (fun z hz => hf.1.differentiableAt (Metric.isOpen_ball.mem_nhds hz)) hf.2
    (fun z => ENNReal.ofReal (‖deriv φ z‖ ^ s))
  change (∫⁻ z in f '' disk, ENNReal.ofReal (‖deriv φ z‖ ^ s)) =
    ∫⁻ z in disk, ENNReal.ofReal (‖deriv f z‖^2) * ENNReal.ofReal (‖deriv φ (f z)‖ ^ s) at he
  rw [himage] at he
  rw [he]
  have hp : (∫⁻ w in disk, ENNReal.ofReal (‖deriv f w‖^2) * ENNReal.ofReal (‖deriv φ (f w)‖ ^ s)) =
      ∫⁻ w in disk, ENNReal.ofReal (‖deriv f w‖ ^ (2 - s)) := by
  {
    refine setLIntegral_congr_fun Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet (fun w hw => ?_)
    rw [hderiv w hw, norm_inv, Real.inv_rpow (norm_nonneg _), ← ENNReal.ofReal_mul (sq_nonneg _)]
    congr 1
    have hn : 0 < ‖deriv f w‖ := norm_pos_iff.mpr (deriv_ne_zero_of_univalentOn Metric.isOpen_ball hf hw)
    rw [← Real.rpow_natCast ‖deriv f w‖ 2, ← Real.rpow_neg hn.le, ← Real.rpow_add hn]
    rfl
  }
  rw [hp]
  exact (hasFiniteIntegral_iff_ofReal (Eventually.of_forall
    (fun w : ℂ => Real.rpow_nonneg (norm_nonneg (deriv f w)) (2 - s)))).mp hfi.hasFiniteIntegral
}

  exact brennan_conjecture_from_disk_means main_theorem_inverse_square

Corollary 7.4 & Proof of Corollary 1.2 · 06-integrability.tex:229–303 · Eq (7.10) 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
Corollary 7.4 (Sobolev pullback on simply connected domains). Let $\Omega \subsetneq \mathbb{C}$ be an open connected set whose boundary has at least two points, and let $\varphi : \Omega \to \mathbb{D}$ be a conformal bijection. For every $p > 2$ and every $4/3 < s < 4$, put $q = ps/(p+s-2) \in (1, p)$. Then for every $u \in L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{D})$ with $\nabla u \in L^p(\mathbb{D})$, the composition $u\circ\varphi$ has weak gradient in $L^q(\Omega)$ and $$\|\nabla(u\circ\varphi)\|_{L^q(\Omega)} \le \left(\int_\Omega |\varphi'(w)|^s\,dA(w)\right)^{\frac{p-q}{pq}} \|\nabla u\|_{L^p(\mathbb{D})}. \qquad (7.10)$$ Proof. Because $\varphi$ is a smooth diffeomorphism, the weak chain rule gives $|\nabla(u\circ\varphi)(w)| = |(\nabla u)(\varphi(w))|\,|\varphi'(w)|$ a.e. Since $q/p + (p-q)/p = 1$ and $s = q(p-2)/(p-q)$, writing $|\nabla(u\circ\varphi)|^q = \bigl(|(\nabla u)\circ\varphi|^p |\varphi'|^2\bigr)^{q/p} |\varphi'|^{s(p-q)/p}$, applying Hölder's inequality with exponents $p/q$ and $p/(p-q)$, and using $\int_\Omega |(\nabla u)(\varphi(w))|^p |\varphi'(w)|^2\,dA(w) = \int_{\mathbb{D}} |\nabla u|^p\,dA$ together with Theorem 1.1 yield (7.10) (Gol'dshtein–Ukhlov [2009, 2013]). $\square$
The identity (7.9) also records the standard equivalence at the upper endpoint for an individual map: if $\int_\Omega |\varphi'(w)|^{4-\eta}\,dA(w) < \infty$ for every $\eta \in (0,2)$, then $\int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'(z)|^{-2+\eta}\,dA(z) < \infty$; since $|f'|^{-2+\eta}$ is subharmonic on $\mathbb{D}$, its value on $|z| = r$ is bounded by its mean on $D(z, (1-r)/2)$, giving $(1-r)M_{-2+\eta}[f'](r) \lesssim \int_{\mathbb{D}} |f'|^{-2+\eta}\,dA < \infty$, so $\beta_f(-2+\eta) \le 1$ and by convexity $\beta_f(-2) \le 1$.
Proof of Corollary 1.2. Let $\Omega_\kappa = \mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty, -1/4]$ and $\varphi_\kappa = \kappa^{-1} : \Omega_\kappa \to \mathbb{D}$. Theorem 1.1 gives $\int_{\Omega_\kappa} |\varphi'_\kappa|^p\,dA < \infty$ for $4/3 < p < 4$. Conversely, $1 + 4\kappa(z) = \bigl(\frac{1+z}{1-z}\bigr)^2$, so $\varphi_\kappa(w) = \frac{\sqrt{1+4w}-1}{\sqrt{1+4w}+1}$ and $\varphi'_\kappa(w) = \frac{1}{\sqrt{1+4w}(\sqrt{1+4w}+1)^2}$. For $p \ge 4$, changing variables $u = w + 1/4$ on $\{|u| < 1/8,\ |\arg u| < \pi/2\}$ gives $|\varphi'_\kappa(w)| \ge \frac{1}{8}|u|^{-1/2}$, so $\int_{\Omega_\kappa} |\varphi'_\kappa|^p\,dA \ge 8^{-p}\int_0^{\frac{1}{8}}\int_{-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} r^{1-\frac{p}{2}}\,d\theta\,dr = \infty$. For $p \le 4/3$, on $S = \{w \in \mathbb{C} : |w| > 1,\ |\arg w| < \pi/2\}$ one has $|\sqrt{1+4w}| \le 3|w|^{1/2}$ and $|\sqrt{1+4w}+1| \le 4|w|^{1/2}$, so $|\varphi'_\kappa(w)| \ge \frac{1}{48}|w|^{-3/2}$ and $\int_{\Omega_\kappa} |\varphi'_\kappa|^p\,dA \ge 48^{-p}\int_1^\infty\int_{-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} R^{1-\frac{3p}{2}}\,d\theta\,dR = \infty$. Finally, on $\{0 < |w| < 1/2,\ |\arg w| < \pi/3\}$, the map $\psi(w) = w^{1/2}$ has $|\psi'(w)| = \frac{1}{2}|w|^{-1/2}$, whose $p$-th power is integrable only for $p < 4$, and on $\{0 < |w| < 1/2,\ |\arg w| < \pi/6\}$, $\tilde\psi(w) = w^2$ has $|(\tilde\psi^{-1})'(\zeta)| = \frac{1}{2}|\zeta|^{-1/2}$ on $\{0 < |\zeta| < 1/4,\ |\arg\zeta| < \pi/3\}$. $\square$
🔍 Level 2 — Sobolev Pullback Exponent $q = \frac{ps}{p+s-2}$ & Endpoint Sharpness Analysis (~10× Depth)
  1. Sobolev Pullback Identity (7.10): Notice why the exponent $s = \frac{q(p-2)}{p-q} \iff q = \frac{ps}{p+s-2}$ arises: in the integral $\int_\Omega |\nabla(u\circ\varphi)|^q\,dA = \int_\Omega |(\nabla u)\circ\varphi|^q |\varphi'|^q\,dA$, we must peel off $|\varphi'|^{2q/p}$ to match the conformal Jacobian $|\varphi'|^2\,dA(w) = dA(z)$ inside $\bigl(\int_\Omega |(\nabla u)\circ\varphi|^p |\varphi'|^2\,dA\bigr)^{q/p} = \|\nabla u\|_{L^p(\mathbb{D})}^q$. The remaining derivative power is $|\varphi'|^{q - 2q/p} = |\varphi'|^{q(p-2)/p}$, which under Hölder's conjugate exponent $\frac{p}{p-q}$ becomes $|\varphi'|^{q(p-2)/(p-q)} = |\varphi'|^s$.
  2. Sharpness at $p \ge 4$ (Tip of Slit $w \to -1/4$) vs. $p \le 4/3$ ($w \to \infty$):
    • Near $w = -1/4$ ($u = w + 1/4 \to 0$), $\sqrt{1+4w} = 2\sqrt{u}$, so $|\varphi'_\kappa(w)| \asymp |u|^{-1/2}$. In 2D polar coordinates $dA(u) = r\,dr\,d\theta$, $\int_0^{1/8} r^{-p/2} r\,dr = \int_0^{1/8} r^{1-p/2}\,dr = \infty \iff 1 - p/2 \le -1 \iff p \ge 4$.
    • Near $w = \infty$ ($|w| = R \to \infty$), $\sqrt{1+4w} \asymp 2|w|^{1/2}$, so $|\varphi'_\kappa(w)| \asymp |w|^{-3/2}$. In 2D polar coordinates $dA(w) = R\,dR\,d\theta$, $\int_1^\infty R^{-3p/2} R\,dR = \int_1^\infty R^{1-3p/2}\,dR = \infty \iff 1 - 3p/2 \ge -1 \iff p \le 4/3$.

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L979-L1007
theorem IntegrableOn_koebeInv_iff (p : ℝ) :
    IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv koebeInv z‖ ^ p) KoebeSlitDomain volume ↔
    IntegrableOn (fun w => ‖deriv koebe w‖ ^ (2 - p)) (Metric.ball 0 1) volume := by
{
  have h_inj : InjOn koebe (Metric.ball 0 1) := by
  {
    intro z1 hz1 z2 hz2 h_eq
    rw [Metric.mem_ball, dist_zero_right] at hz1 hz2
    have h1 := koebeInv_koebe z1 hz1
    have h2 := koebeInv_koebe z2 hz2
    rw [h_eq] at h1
    exact h1.symm.trans h2
  }
  have hf' : ∀ w ∈ Metric.ball 0 1, HasFDerivWithinAt koebe (fderiv ℝ koebe w) (Metric.ball 0 1) w := by
  {
    intro w hw
    rw [fderiv_koebe_eq w hw]
    exact koebe_hasFDerivWithinAt w hw
  }
  have h_meas : MeasurableSet (Metric.ball (0 : ℂ) 1) := Metric.isOpen_ball.measurableSet
  have h_change := integrableOn_image_iff_integrableOn_abs_det_fderiv_smul volume h_meas hf' h_inj (fun z => ‖deriv koebeInv z‖ ^ p)
  rw [koebe_image_eq] at h_change
  rw [h_change]
  apply integrableOn_congr_fun _ h_meas
  intro w hw
  simp only
  rw [smul_eq_mul]
  exact integrand_pullback_eq p w hw
}

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L1848-L1855
theorem KoebeSharpnessLimit (p : ℝ) (h : p ≥ 4 ∨ p ≤ 4/3) :
    ¬ IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv koebeInv z‖ ^ p) KoebeSlitDomain volume := by
{
  rw [IntegrableOn_koebeInv_iff]
  rcases h with hp | hp
  · exact cusp_divergence p hp
  · exact pole_divergence p hp
}

Appendix: Explicit Koebe Slit Bijection & Sharpness (BrennanConjecture.lean)

Constructive Formalization of $\kappa : \mathbb{D} \leftrightarrow \mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,-1/4]$ and $\varphi_\kappa = \kappa^{-1}$
✓ Lean 4 Verified (1,856 lines, 0 Sorrys) BrennanConjecture.lean:1–1856
▸
Explicit Koebe Inverse & Endpoint Divergence · BrennanConjecture.lean 🔍 Click for Level 2 (10× Math)
In brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean (1,856 lines), we give a completely constructive, self-contained formalization of the Koebe function $\kappa(z) = \frac{z}{(1-z)^2}$, the slit plane $\Omega_\kappa = \mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty, -1/4]$, and the explicit conformal inverse $$\varphi_\kappa(w) = \frac{\sqrt{1+4w}-1}{\sqrt{1+4w}+1} \qquad \bigl(w \in \Omega_\kappa\bigr),$$ proving two-sided inverse identities, holomorphic differentiability, the exact derivative formula $\varphi'_\kappa(w) = \frac{1}{\kappa'(\varphi_\kappa(w))}$, and the full iff characterization $\int_{\Omega_\kappa} |\varphi'_\kappa(w)|^p\,dA(w) < \infty \iff 4/3 < p < 4$.
🔍 Level 2 — Algebraic Inversion of $\kappa(z) = w$ via the Principal Square Root $\sqrt{1+4w}$ (~10× Depth)

Notice that $\kappa(z) = \frac{z}{(1-z)^2}$ satisfies $$1 + 4\kappa(z) = 1 + \frac{4z}{(1-z)^2} = \frac{(1-z)^2 + 4z}{(1-z)^2} = \left(\frac{1+z}{1-z}\right)^2.$$ For $z \in \mathbb{D}$, the Cayley transform $u = \frac{1+z}{1-z}$ has strictly positive real part ($\operatorname{Re}u = \frac{1-|z|^2}{|1-z|^2} > 0$), so $1+4\kappa(z) = u^2$ lies in the slit plane $\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty, 0]$, and the principal branch of the complex square root recovers $u = \sqrt{1+4w}$ uniquely. Inverting $u = \frac{1+z}{1-z}$ gives $z = \frac{u-1}{u+1} = \frac{\sqrt{1+4w}-1}{\sqrt{1+4w}+1}$.

File: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean (lines 45–60, 343–375, 979, 1848) Open in Editor ↗ 
-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L46-L46
noncomputable def koebe (z : ℂ) : ℂ := z / (1 - z) ^ 2

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L49-L49
def KoebeSlitDomain : Set ℂ := {z | z.re > -1/4 ∨ z.im ≠ 0}

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L100-L104
noncomputable def koebeInv (w : ℂ) : ℂ :=
  if w = 0 then 0
  else ((2 * w + 1) - csqrt (4 * w + 1)) / (2 * w)

--  The principal complex square root has a positive real part on the slit plane.

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L343-L364
theorem koebeInv_koebe (z : ℂ) (hz : ‖z‖ < 1) : koebeInv (koebe z) = z := by
{
  by_cases h : koebe z = 0
  · unfold koebeInv
    rw [if_pos h]
    have hz0 : z = 0 := by
    {
      have h_div : z / (1 - z) ^ 2 = 0 := h
      have h_or := div_eq_zero_iff.mp h_div
      rcases h_or with hz0 | h_den
      · exact hz0
      · have : 1 - z = 0 := sq_eq_zero_iff.mp h_den
        have hz1 : z = 1 := (sub_eq_zero.mp this).symm
        have h_norm : ‖z‖ = 1 := by rw [hz1, norm_one]
        linarith
    }
    subst hz0
    simp
  · exact koebeInv_koebe_nonzero hz h
}

--  The Koebe function is the right-inverse of the inverse Koebe function on the slit domain.

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L365-L464
theorem koebe_koebeInv (w : ℂ) (hw : w ∈ KoebeSlitDomain) : koebe (koebeInv w) = w := by
{
  unfold koebe koebeInv
  split_ifs with h
  · -- w = 0
    subst h
    simp
  · -- w ≠ 0
    let z := ((2 * w + 1) - csqrt (4 * w + 1)) / (2 * w)
    have hz1 : z ≠ 1 := by
    {
      intro hz_eq
      have hz_eq' : ((2 * w + 1) - csqrt (4 * w + 1)) = 2 * w := by
      {
        have h_div : ((2 * w + 1) - csqrt (4 * w + 1)) / (2 * w) = 1 := hz_eq
        have h_mul := div_eq_iff (by exact mul_ne_zero (by norm_num) h) |>.mp h_div
        rw [one_mul] at h_mul
        exact h_mul
      }
      have h1 : 1 = csqrt (4 * w + 1) := by
      {
        calc 1 = (2 * w + 1 - csqrt (4 * w + 1)) - 2 * w + csqrt (4 * w + 1) := by ring
        _ = 2 * w - 2 * w + csqrt (4 * w + 1) := by rw [hz_eq']
        _ = csqrt (4 * w + 1) := by ring
      }
      have h2 : (1 : ℂ) ^ 2 = csqrt (4 * w + 1) ^ 2 := congrArg (fun x => x ^ 2) h1
      have h_plane := koebe_domain_mapping w hw
      rw [csqrt_sq (4 * w + 1) h_plane] at h2
      simp only [one_pow] at h2
      have h_zero : w = 0 := by
      {
        calc w = (4 * w + 1 - 1) / 4 := by ring
        _ = (1 - 1) / 4 := by rw [← h2]
        _ = 0 := by ring
      }
      exact h h_zero
    }
    have hB2 : csqrt (4 * w + 1) ^ 2 = 4 * w + 1 := by
    {
      have h_plane := koebe_domain_mapping w hw
      exact csqrt_sq (4 * w + 1) h_plane
    }
    have h_eq2 : 2 * w * z = 2 * w + 1 - csqrt (4 * w + 1) := by
    {
      unfold z
      rw [mul_comm]
      exact div_mul_cancel₀ (2 * w + 1 - csqrt (4 * w + 1)) (mul_ne_zero (by norm_num) h)
    }
    have h_eq3 : 2 * w * (1 - z) = csqrt (4 * w + 1) - 1 := by
    {
      calc 2 * w * (1 - z) = 2 * w - 2 * w * z := by ring
      _ = 2 * w - (2 * w + 1 - csqrt (4 * w + 1)) := by rw [h_eq2]
      _ = csqrt (4 * w + 1) - 1 := by ring
    }
    have h_sq : (2 * w * (1 - z)) ^ 2 = (csqrt (4 * w + 1) - 1) ^ 2 := by rw [h_eq3]
    have h_sq2 : 4 * w ^ 2 * (1 - z) ^ 2 = 4 * w + 2 - 2 * csqrt (4 * w + 1) := by
    {
      calc 4 * w ^ 2 * (1 - z) ^ 2 = (2 * w * (1 - z)) ^ 2 := by ring
      _ = (csqrt (4 * w + 1) - 1) ^ 2 := h_sq
      _ = csqrt (4 * w + 1) ^ 2 - 2 * csqrt (4 * w + 1) + 1 := by ring
      _ = (4 * w + 1) - 2 * csqrt (4 * w + 1) + 1 := by rw [hB2]
      _ = 4 * w + 2 - 2 * csqrt (4 * w + 1) := by ring
    }
    have h_final : 4 * w ^ 2 * (1 - z) ^ 2 = 4 * w * z := by
    {
      rw [h_sq2]
      calc 4 * w + 2 - 2 * csqrt (4 * w + 1) = 2 * (2 * w * z) := by
      {
        rw [h_eq2]
        ring
      }
      _ = 4 * w * z := by ring
    }
    have h_cancel : w * (1 - z) ^ 2 = z := by
    {
      have h_mul : 4 * w * (w * (1 - z) ^ 2) = 4 * w * z := by
      {
        calc 4 * w * (w * (1 - z) ^ 2) = 4 * w ^ 2 * (1 - z) ^ 2 := by ring
        _ = 4 * w * z := h_final
      }
      exact mul_left_cancel₀ (mul_ne_zero (by norm_num) h) h_mul
    }
    have h_div_eq : z / (1 - z) ^ 2 = w := by
    {
      apply (div_eq_iff ?_).mpr
      · exact h_cancel.symm
      · intro hz_zero
        have hz_one : z = 1 := by
        {
          have h_sub : 1 - z = 0 := sq_eq_zero_iff.mp hz_zero
          calc z = 1 - (1 - z) := by ring
          _ = 1 - 0 := by rw [h_sub]
          _ = 1 := by ring
        }
        exact hz1 hz_one
    }
    exact h_div_eq
}

--  The inverse Koebe function can be written in a simplified form without piecewise definition.

-- Source: brennan/BrennanConjecture.lean#L1848-L1855
theorem KoebeSharpnessLimit (p : ℝ) (h : p ≥ 4 ∨ p ≤ 4/3) :
    ¬ IntegrableOn (fun z => ‖deriv koebeInv z‖ ^ p) KoebeSlitDomain volume := by
{
  rw [IntegrableOn_koebeInv_iff]
  rcases h with hp | hp
  · exact cusp_divergence p hp
  · exact pole_divergence p hp
}
